### Phase 4 — EISA Registration (`modules/eisa-registration/`) & EISA Confirmation (`modules/eisa-confirmation/`)

Built as two separate module folders, each with its own nav item, records and workflow — neither is a tab inside EISA Instrument Validation (CR-019) or inside each other (CR-035).

- **CR-024** (registration started by the SDP for a PUBLISHED sitting, not by validation finishing) → `eisa-registration/api.ts` `listRegistrableSittings()` reads `eisa-calendar/api.ts`'s `listPublishedSittings()` only; `startRegistration()` has no dependency on instrument-validation at all.
- **CR-036** (learners picked from a simulated already-enrolled list, not a spreadsheet; SOR upload UI element) → `mock/data/enrolledLearners.ts` (seed roster) + `mock/data/sdpDirectory.ts` (seed SDPs), read via `mock/api/qaDomain.ts` (`listEnrolledLearners`, `getSdpComplianceStatus`, `listSdps` — named after what the real QA domain would do, per the module `CLAUDE.md`'s "Data" section). `eisa-registration/external/RegistrationDetail.tsx`'s "Add an Enrolled Learner" panel + per-learner "Upload SOR" button (`api.ts` `addLearner`, `uploadLearnerSor`, both UI-only file handling).
- **CR-042** (RPL/ARPL indicator on the registration) → `types.ts` `RegistrationLearner.rplArpl`, toggled per learner via a `Switch` in `RegistrationDetail.tsx` while the SDP is still building the registration.
- **CR-041** (SDP compliance indicator; non-compliant SDP visually blocked from submitting) → `mock/api/qaDomain.ts` `getSdpComplianceStatus()` (one seeded SDP, "Vuma Trade Centre", is non-compliant); checked both in the UI (`external/NewRegistrationDialog.tsx` shows a red block alert and disables "Start Registration") and again server-side in `api.ts` `startRegistration()`.
- **CR-040** (block a learner already on another active/concurrent registration) → `api.ts` `findConcurrentRegistration()` checks every other registration record (any stage) for that learner before `addLearner()` allows it, with a message naming the other qualification and sitting date.
- **Category split: Trades / Non-Trades** → `types.ts` `RegistrationCategory`, chosen by the SDP in `NewRegistrationDialog.tsx`; `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` splits the Assistant Director's queue into two tabs.
- **Assistant Director processing** (validates documentation, creates the registration number) → `workflow.ts` `REGISTRATION_WORKFLOW` (`sdp-submission → ad-review → registered`), `internal/RegistrationWorkspace.tsx`, `api.ts` `performRegistrationAction()`. The registration number is generated as `EISA-REG-{year}-{sequence}`.
- **CR-038** (flag an individual SOR as non-compliant with a reason; notifies the SDP to correct and resubmit) → built as a per-learner flag that does **not** move the registration's own workflow stage (the record stays in `ad-review` throughout) — `types.ts` `SorStatus`, `api.ts` `flagLearnerSor()` (Assistant Director, mandatory reason) and `uploadLearnerSor()` (re-upload while flagged clears the flag and notifies the Assistant Director). See "Assumptions made" for why this reads as a learner-level flag rather than a whole-record send-back.
- **PM-08** (Non-Trades path prepares files for the QP and shares the SDP list with QA) → `api.ts` `performRegistrationAction()`'s `reg-create-number` branch sets `nonTradesHandoff` (timestamps) and writes an audit entry + QP notification when `category === 'non-trades'`. Flagged as an open question below — the register doesn't specify the exact file contents or a QA-side screen to receive the "shared" list.
- **CR-042/CR-044** (RPL/ARPL-flagged registration automatically creates a Site Visits monitoring item — the ONLY automatic trigger into Site Visits) → `mock/api/siteVisitsStore.ts` (new; `createMonitoringItem`, `listMonitoringItems`, keyed `siteVisits:monitoringItems` so Phase 5 reads the same store instead of inventing a second one), called from `performRegistrationAction()`'s `reg-create-number` branch — one monitoring item per registration, covering every RPL/ARPL learner on it. Confirmation (CR-043) deliberately never calls this.
- **CR-037, CR-039** (backend-only, no screen) → `api.ts` calls `validateSorAgainstModules()` on every SOR upload and `verifyLearnerIdentity()` on every learner add, both from the existing Phase 0 stubs in `mock/integrations/`; results are awaited and ignored, per "call the stubs, no screens".
- **CR-035** (registration record shows its learners, each learner's SOR and the supporting documents, synced) → `external/RegistrationDetail.tsx` and `internal/RegistrationWorkspace.tsx` both render the full learner/SOR table plus a registration-level "Supporting Documents" (QA Report) upload list from the same `EisaRegistrationRecord`.
- **CR-043** (rename "EISA Notification" to "EISA Confirmation"; consolidated learner list; QP acknowledges then confirms centres/counts/marking-moderation dates; does NOT create a site visit) → `modules/eisa-confirmation/` built as its own module. `workflow.ts` `CONFIRMATION_WORKFLOW` (`awaiting-qp-ack → awaiting-qp-confirmation → confirmed`) models the two separate QP actions. `api.ts` `generateConfirmation()` reads `eisa-registration/api.ts`'s `listRegisteredRegistrationsForSitting()` — only once registration is complete for that sitting, never automatically. `confirmDetails()` deliberately never touches `mock/api/siteVisitsStore.ts` or anything in Site Visits — a code comment calls this out explicitly next to the CR-044 reference.
- Label `eisaConfirmation` (`config/labels.ts`, already set in Phase 0) is used throughout; no screen or string says "EISA Notification".

#### EISA Registration + Confirmation assumptions made

- **The assessment centre defaults to the registering SDP.** The old build's registration screen never captured a separate "assessment centre" field, so `eisa-confirmation/api.ts` `generateConfirmation()` groups the consolidated learner list by `registration.sdpName`. The QP's "confirm the assessment centres" step confirms learner counts per that same grouping rather than renaming/splitting centres. Revisit if QCTO says assessment centres are a distinct concept from SDPs.
- **"Dates within the sitting" (CR-043) is read as on/after the sitting date.** `eisa-confirmation/api.ts` `confirmDetails()` blocks a marking date before the sitting date and a moderation date before the marking date — the register doesn't give an exact window, and marking/moderation logically can't happen before the exam is written.
- **"Active or concurrent EISA registration" (CR-040) means the learner appears on any other registration record, in any stage** (`sdp-submission`, `ad-review`, or `registered`), not only a fully `registered` one — a learner mid-registration elsewhere is still "concurrent".
- **PM-08's Non-Trades hand-off is simulated as an audit entry plus a QP notification**, not a new screen or a QA-domain inbox — the Quality Assurance domain has no role in this build's `Role` union to notify directly, and the register doesn't specify what "sharing the SDP list" looks like on QA's side.
- **The Assistant Director both processes registration and generates the EISA Confirmation** (`eisa-confirmation/config.ts` → `CONFIRMATION_GENERATOR_ROLE`) — the register names only "QCTO officials" for Confirmation, so it follows the same role that already owns Registration immediately before it, same pattern as the EISA Calendar's `CALENDAR_REVIEWER_ROLE` assumption.
- **CR-038's SOR flag is a per-learner flag inside `ad-review`, not a whole-record send-back to `sdp-submission`.** The register says the official "can flag an individual SOR... which notifies the SDP to correct and resubmit" — read literally as scoped to that one learner, so the registration itself never leaves Assistant Director review and the SDP only gets a narrow "re-upload this one SOR" action.
- **One registration can hold learners with mixed RPL/ARPL flags; the automatic monitoring-item trigger fires once per registration**, listing every flagged learner on it, rather than creating one monitoring item per learner. The register says the indicator "automatically creates a monitoring item" (singular) without specifying granularity.
- **A QP visiting EISA Registration, and an SDP visiting EISA Confirmation, see the same list read-only.** No CR restricts either direction (unlike CR-018's explicit SDP block on Instrument Validation), so both nav items stay visible to both external roles; only the role that the CR names for each action (SDP for registration, QP for confirmation) sees the action buttons.

### Phase 5 — Site Visits & Monitoring hub (`modules/site-visits/`)

Built as one standalone module with a single `SiteVisit` record shape shared by all four visit types — no tab lives inside Registration, Confirmation or any other module.

- **CR-044** (standalone hub; the only automatic trigger is RPL/ARPL) → `modules/site-visits/` is its own nav item on both portals. `api.ts` `syncAutoCreatedVisits()` reads the Phase 4 `mock/api/siteVisitsStore.ts` (`listMonitoringItems()`) and converts any new item into a `SiteVisit` via `buildAutoCreatedVisit()` — the only path that creates a visit without an official explicitly doing so through `createVisit()`.
- **Visit types** (EISA Readiness — PM-05, Marking & Moderation, RPL/ARPL Monitoring, Post-EISA Monitoring) → `types.ts` `VisitType`, `visitTypeLabels.ts`. Marking & Moderation reuses the Phase-0 `mock/data/siteVisitFormSections.ts` checklist (copied verbatim from the old build). Post-EISA Monitoring's checklist is newly copied from the old PEM report's Sections B–D (`mock/data/postEisaMonitoringSections.ts`, AQP→QP renamed). EISA Readiness and RPL/ARPL Monitoring have no old-build or QCTO template to copy — `mock/data/eisaReadinessVisitSections.ts` and `mock/data/rplArplVisitSections.ts` are built fresh, in the same checklist shape, flagged below as needing QCTO's real templates (CR-048).
- **CR-045** (no "Schedules Received"; "Reports Received" not "Results Received") → `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` Dashboard tab's four cards (Total Visits, Pending Planning, Awaiting Report, `LABELS.reportsReceived`) — no schedules-received card anywhere.
- **CR-046** (no leftover tabs) → built clean; the only tabs are Dashboard / Scheduling / All Visits (internal) and a single QP view (external) — nothing ported from the old build's per-stage tabs.
- **CR-047** (scheduling in its own tab; QPs only see their own visits) → `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`'s "Scheduling" tab lists only visits still in the planning chain. `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`'s `QpVisitsView` filters by a "Viewing visits for QP" picker (see "Assumptions made" — there is no QP login to filter by automatically).
- **CR-050** (link to a published sitting; auto-populate) → `internal/NewVisitDialog.tsx`'s "Link to a Published EISA Sitting" dropdown (reads `eisa-calendar/api.ts`'s `listPublishedSittings()` via `site-visits/api.ts`'s `listSchedulableSittings()`); selecting one disables and auto-fills QP, qualification and date.
- **CR-051** (Notified/Unannounced flag with configurable lead time) → `types.ts` `NotificationFlag`, `config.ts` `DEFAULT_NOTICE_LEAD_TIME_DAYS`, captured on `NewVisitDialog.tsx`.
- **Planning chain** (Assistant Director plans → Deputy Director recommends → Domain Director approves → Sub Domain Admin books, as four separate roles) → `workflow.ts` `PLANNING_WORKFLOW` (`ad-planning → dd-recommend → domain-director-approval → sda-booking → booked`), driven from `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s Planning tab. Forward-only — CR-055/056's send-back/reject/reroute are scoped to the report workflow only (see "Assumptions made").
- **CR-048/CR-049** (dynamic, visit-type-driven execution form with QP/qualification context) → `components/ExecutionForm.tsx` (one shared shell: `Checklist` + `freeTextFields.ts`'s per-type free-text fields + completion fields), `freeTextFields.ts`. QP/qualification are captured at creation time and shown (editable pre-execution) on `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s Details tab, satisfying CR-049 without a second, separate selector on the form itself.
- **CR-053** (ASD submits, Deputy Director — a different role — approves; nobody approves their own report) → `workflow.ts` `REPORT_WORKFLOW` (`awaiting-execution → dd-report-review → approved`/`rejected`), `config.ts` `REPORT_SUBMITTER_ROLE` (`'ASD'`) / `REPORT_APPROVER_ROLE` (`'Deputy Director'`), `report-approve`'s `requiresDifferentActor: true`. Role-gating alone already makes self-approval impossible (ASD can't act in `dd-report-review`, DD can't act in `awaiting-execution`); the different-actor check is kept as the same defence-in-depth every other workflow in this domain has.
- **CR-056** (send back for clarification / reject, with a reason, on the report approval screen) → `REPORT_WORKFLOW`'s `report-send-back` and `report-reject` actions, both `requiresNote: true`; buttons + `shared/ui/ReasonDialog.tsx` on `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s Execution & Report tab.
- **CR-055** (Partial — manual re-route only logs a mandatory-reason audit entry; it does not change who owns the stage, because this build has no individual logins to hand the record to) → `report-reroute`. Deliberately does **not** go through the shared workflow engine — see "Assumptions made" for why — `api.ts` `performReportAction()` checks the role/stage gate itself and writes a plain audit entry instead.
- **CR-054** (Open item — cross-unit report visibility) → `config.ts` `REPORT_VISIBILITY_RULES` (a literal, editable table: every site-visits-team internal role sees every report; a QP sees only their own) and `visibilityScopeForRole()`. Not yet wired into the UI as a hard filter beyond the existing CR-047 QP filter — flagged below.
- **CR-057** (Facilitator & Learner Questionnaires only on Post-EISA Monitoring, only once results are approved) → `mock/data/pemQuestionnaires.ts` (question text copied from the old build's external questionnaire forms, AQP→QP renamed), rendered inside `components/ExecutionForm.tsx` only for that visit type, gated on `SiteVisit.resultsApprovedForSitting`. That flag is a per-visit simulated toggle (see "Assumptions made" — Phase 6 doesn't exist yet to provide the real status).
- **CR-058** (rename/redesign "Submit Approved Results" into an outcome-sharing step) → `config.ts` `SHARE_VISIT_OUTCOME_LABEL` placeholder ("Share Visit Outcome"), `REPORT_WORKFLOW`'s `share-visit-outcome` (QCTO-side, Post-EISA Monitoring only, after report approval) and `qp-acknowledge-outcome` (QP's response) actions. `external/VisitDetail.tsx` is where the QP acknowledges.
- **PM-05** (EISA Readiness visit type) → built as one of the four visit types with its own fresh checklist (above); still an open item whether it's required at all (flagged below).
- **PM-06** (PEM only from a low-competency approved results batch) → `config.ts` `POST_EISA_MONITORING_COMPETENCY_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` (unconfirmed placeholder). No Phase 6 results-batch data exists yet to check against, so Post-EISA Monitoring visits are created manually by an official for now, same as every other non-automatic visit type — flagged below.
- **PM-07** (PEM report shared with QA, QP, Accreditation, SDP) → `api.ts`'s `share-visit-outcome` branch sends real in-app notifications to `QP` and `SDP` (both exist as roles here) and writes an audit entry documenting the simulated QA/Accreditation hand-off (neither domain has a role in this build's `Role` union to notify directly — same pattern as Phase 4's PM-08).

#### Site Visits assumptions made

- **The RPL/ARPL auto-created visit skips the planning chain entirely** (`planningWorkflow` starts at `booked`, not `ad-planning`) — it is already a known, real requirement the moment the registration creates it, not a plan an Assistant Director still needs to propose and get approved. Every other visit type always starts at `ad-planning`.
- **Fixed in Phase 6: the auto-created visit's QP name now carries over from the registration, instead of the placeholder this bullet originally flagged.** `EisaRegistrationRecord` (Phase 4) gained a `qpName` field, populated from the EISA Calendar sitting (`EisaSitting.qpName`) at `startRegistration()`; `mock/api/siteVisitsStore.ts`'s `RplArplMonitoringItem` carries it through to `site-visits/api.ts`'s `buildAutoCreatedVisit()`. `AUTO_VISIT_QP_PLACEHOLDER` has been removed from `site-visits/config.ts`. The QP name is still editable on `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s Details tab if it ever needs correcting.
- **CR-055/CR-056's send-back/reject/reroute are scoped to the report approval workflow only, not the planning chain.** The developer's own build instructions placed these explicitly under "Report approval," separate from the "Planning approval" bullet — the planning chain (`PLANNING_WORKFLOW`) is forward-only by design here.
- **Re-routing (CR-055) is logged as an audit entry and does not change workflow state**, instead of running through the shared workflow engine like every other action. The engine always stamps the acting role's name onto `submittedBy` on every transition it performs; since there is no individual login (every Deputy Director shares the literal role label "Deputy Director"), running a same-stage reroute through it would silently overwrite the original ASD's `submittedBy` and then immediately trip CR-053's different-actor check on the very next approval attempt. There is also no second "other Deputy Director" to actually hand the stage to, so nothing in the workflow state should change — a plain audit note is the honest representation of what this build can actually do.
- **CR-057's "results approved" gate is a plain boolean on the visit (`resultsApprovedForSitting`)** — Phase 6's `createPemVisitForBatch()` now sets it to `true` automatically the moment a Post-EISA Monitoring visit is created from an approved batch, closing the CR-057 loop for that path. For a PEM visit created manually (not from a batch), it is still a toggle an official flips by hand on `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`. `config.ts` → `RESULTS_APPROVAL_IS_SIMULATED` is kept as-is since neither path reads a *stored* "approved" status off the batch record directly — it is set once, at visit-creation time.
- **PM-06's automatic low-competency trigger is now wired up in Phase 6** — `modules/approval-of-results/api.ts`'s `createPemVisitForBatch()` reads this same `config.ts` → `POST_EISA_MONITORING_COMPETENCY_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` constant (still a placeholder pending QCTO confirming the real number) and offers to create a Post-EISA Monitoring visit once an approved batch's competency rate is below it. The visit is still created manually by an official clicking a button, not fully automatically — see the Phase 6 section below.
- **CR-047's "QPs only see their own visits" is simulated with a local "Viewing visits for QP" picker** (`external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`), the same no-individual-login pattern used everywhere else in this build (QSA Addendum, EISA Calendar, etc.) — there is nothing to build a real identity filter against yet.
- **SDP has no view in this hub.** The register's "Who's involved" list for the Site Visits hub names only QP as "view only" — SDP isn't mentioned at all, unlike EISA Registration/Confirmation where neither side was explicitly excluded. `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx` shows the SDP role a simple "not applicable" card rather than the QP's filtered view.
- **CR-054's visibility rule table is built but not yet enforced as a hard list filter** beyond the existing QP-only-own-visits behaviour — every internal role on the site-visits team can already open every record from `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`'s tables. The table (`config.ts` → `REPORT_VISIBILITY_RULES`) exists so the real per-unit rules QCTO eventually provides are a data change, not new code, but applying it as an actual list/record filter is still open (flagged below).
- **"Share Visit Outcome" only exists for Post-EISA Monitoring.** Every other visit type's report workflow ends at "Approved" — CR-058's rename/redesign was specifically about the old "Submit Approved Results" step, which only ever applied to PEM.

### Phase 6 — Approval of Results (`modules/approval-of-results/`)

- **CR-060** (reserve the "Approval of Results" name for this module) → `config/labels.ts`'s `LABELS.approvalOfResults` (already set in Phase 0) is used nowhere else; `config/nav.ts` only ever had this label pointing at this module (previously tagged "Phase 6", now built). Satisfied by construction rather than by renaming an old-build tab — see "Assumptions made" below for why.
- **CR-059** (QP submits a results batch within a 21-working-day SLA; QCTO compliance review, evidence evaluation, folder-by-folder checks; approve/decline through AD → DD → Director → Chief Director) → `types.ts` (`ResultsBatch`), `workflow.ts` (`RESULTS_WORKFLOW`: `ad-review → dd-review → director-review → chief-director-review → approved`/`declined`, decline available at every one of the four stages, `batch-resubmit` loops `declined → ad-review`), `api.ts` (`submitResultsBatch`, `performResultsAction`), `external/SubmitResultsBatchDialog.tsx`, `internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx`'s Compliance Review and Approval Chain tabs. The Assistant Director's three sub-steps (compliance review notes, evidence evaluation notes + uploaded evidence, folder-by-folder checklist) all gate the one `ad-advance` action — `shared/checklist/Checklist.tsx` reused for the folder checklist with mandatory per-line comments (CR-011), items built fresh in `mock/data/resultsComplianceFolders.ts` (no old-build or QCTO template exists for this new module — flagged below, same situation as Phase 5's EISA Readiness/RPL-ARPL checklists).
- **CR-059's 21-working-day deadline** → `shared/dates/workingDays.ts`'s `addWorkingDays`/`workingDaysBetween` (built in Phase 3 for exactly this) via `workflow.ts`'s `submissionDeadlineFor`, `deadlineState` (on-track/due-soon/overdue) and `workingDaysRemaining`; shown via `components/DeadlineBadge.tsx` as a live countdown before submission and as an on-time/late indicator afterwards, on both the external "Awaiting Submission" list and every internal batch table/workspace.
- **CR-059 decline + resubmission** → `ad-decline`/`dd-decline`/`director-decline`/`chief-director-decline` actions (`requiresNote: true`), QP notified via `addNotification`; `batch-resubmit` (QP-only, from `declined`) resets the checklist and review notes for a fresh cycle (`cycleCount`), keeping every past decision in `declineHistory` — same resubmission shape as Instrument Validation's CR-023 loop.
- **CR-052** (linked Marking & Moderation monitoring status on the review screen) → `api.ts`'s `listMarkingModerationVisitsForSitting()` (filters Phase 5's `site-visits/api.ts` `listVisits()` by `visitType: 'marking-moderation'` and the batch's `sittingId`), shown on `internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx`'s Details tab.
- **CR-059 hand-off** (recommendation to the Certification domain on Chief Director approval) → `chief-director-approve` sets `ResultsBatch.recommendation` (timestamp + approver) and writes an audit entry documenting the simulated hand-off (Certification has no role in this build's `Role` union to notify directly — same pattern as Phase 4/5's QA/Accreditation hand-offs, PM-08/PM-07); visible status "Approved — Recommended to Certification" via `workflow.ts`'s `resultsStatusLabel`.
- **PM-06** (offer a Post-EISA Monitoring visit from a low-competency approved batch) → `workflow.ts`'s `competencyRatePercent()` shown on every batch view; `internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx`'s Details tab offers "Create Post-EISA Monitoring Visit" once `workflow.stageId === 'approved'` and the rate is below Phase 5's `site-visits/config.ts` → `POST_EISA_MONITORING_COMPETENCY_THRESHOLD_PERCENT` (reused directly rather than duplicated — still a QCTO-unconfirmed placeholder, flagged below); `api.ts`'s `createPemVisitForBatch()` creates the visit via Phase 5's `site-visits/api.ts` `createVisit()` and sets `pemVisitId` so the offer only fires once per batch.
- **CR-057 unlock** (approved results unlock the PEM questionnaires built in Phase 5) → `createPemVisitForBatch()` calls Phase 5's `setResultsApprovedForSitting(visit.id, true)` immediately on creation, so a PEM visit created from an approved batch has its Facilitator/Learner Questionnaires visible from the start — no manual toggle needed for that path (the toggle still exists for a manually-created PEM visit with no batch behind it).

#### Approval of Results assumptions made

- **"QCTO can approve or decline" (CR-059) is read as available at every one of the four internal review stages** (Assistant Director, Deputy Director, Director, Chief Director), not only the final one — mirroring this domain's running "decline/send-back available on every approval stage" theme (CR-056), since the register doesn't restrict decline to a single stage the way it does for the QSA Addendum's IAC-only decline.
- **The three AD sub-steps (compliance review, evidence evaluation, folder-by-folder checklist) are read as one combined review stage**, not three separate workflow stages/approvals — the register lists them with arrows under the single heading "Assistant Director compliance review -> evidence evaluation -> folder-by-folder compliance checks", all before the first hand-off to the Deputy Director, the same way the QSA Addendum bundles a checklist and an evaluation report into one ASD stage.
- **A results batch can only be submitted for a sitting with a fully `confirmed` EISA Confirmation record**, not directly off the EISA Calendar or Registration — the register doesn't state the exact upstream dependency, but Confirmation is the step immediately before Approval of Results on the main highway and already carries the QP-confirmed, consolidated learner list this module needs (QP name, qualification, learner count). `api.ts`'s `listSittingsAwaitingSubmission()`/`submitResultsBatch()` read only from `eisa-confirmation/api.ts`.
- **The "due soon" window (5 working days) is an unconfirmed placeholder**, same pattern as every other unconfirmed figure in this build (EISA Calendar's lead time, Site Visits' notice lead time) — `config.ts` → `RESULTS_DUE_SOON_WINDOW_WORKING_DAYS`.
- **The folder-by-folder compliance checklist is built fresh**, with no old-build or QCTO source to copy from (this is an entirely new module) — `mock/data/resultsComplianceFolders.ts`'s four folders and ten items are a reasonable first pass, flagged below for QCTO's real folder list and wording, same situation as Phase 5's EISA Readiness/RPL-ARPL checklists.
- **CR-060 is satisfied by construction, not by renaming anything in the old build.** The module `CLAUDE.md`'s hard rules forbid editing `src/pages/assessment/` at all, and v2's own nav/labels never used "Approval of Results" for anything except this module (Phase 0's `LABELS.approvalOfResults` sat unused until now). Which literal old-build tab CR-060 refers to (the register itself says "Confirm which tab the client meant") is still an open question for QCTO, but it doesn't block this build, since the old screen is explicitly out of this project's scope to touch.
- **A results batch is a single batch-level record, not per-learner results.** The register's own wording ("the batch competency rate") and every other Phase 6 ask (a 21-day deadline, folder-level compliance, one approve/decline decision) describes one record per sitting, not an individual pass/fail per learner — so `ResultsBatch` carries a `learnerCount`/`competentCount` pair rather than a results row per learner.
- **Evidence documents are optional and UI-only** (file name/size only, same as every other upload in this domain) — the register doesn't specify what "evidence evaluation" evidence actually consists of, so the QP can optionally attach supporting files at submission time for the Assistant Director to reference during evidence evaluation.

### Phase 7 — FISA (`modules/fisa/`) and RPL Toolkit Evaluation (`modules/rpl-toolkit/`, behind a feature flag)

Built as two standalone modules, neither nested inside the EISA flow nor a fallback of it — each has its own triggers, participants and approvers, per the CLAUDE.md "Build FISA and RPL Toolkit Evaluation as separate modules" instruction.

**FISA (`modules/fisa/`) — CR-026, PM-01, PM-02, PM-03, PM-10, PM-11.** Rebuilt as a fully independent process for Skills Programmes with all five stages. It is never triggered by the SAQA gate check and never joins the EISA/QSA Addendum flow — it shares nothing with it except shared components (`shared/checklist`, `shared/workflow`, `shared/audit`, `shared/notifications`).

- **CR-026 — FISA Standards** (`types.ts` `FisaStandardRecord`, `workflow.ts` `FISA_STANDARD_WORKFLOW`, `api.ts`'s `createFisaStandard`/`performStandardAction`, `mock/data/fisaStandardChecklistItems.ts`, `internal/NewStandardDialog.tsx`, `internal/StandardWorkspace.tsx`) — Qualifications Development supplies the source documents (`qd-submission`) → ASD evaluates (`asd-evaluation`, shared checklist, CR-011 mandatory comment per line) → Deputy Director reviews (`dd-review`) → IAC (`iac-review`) → **PM-10**: final sign-off (`qd-signoff` stage) is the Qualifications Development role, not the CEO — no CEO stage exists anywhere in this workflow. CR-056's send-back/reject are on every review stage (`standard-*-request-docs`/`standard-*-reject`/`standard-*-return`).
- **Instrument development + CR-026/PM-01/PM-02/PM-03/PM-11 — FISA Instrument Validation** (`types.ts` `FisaValidationRecord`, `workflow.ts` `FISA_VALIDATION_WORKFLOW`, `api.ts`'s `startFisaValidation`/`performFisaValidationAction`, `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx`, `external/ValidationDetail.tsx`/`NewValidationDialog.tsx`) — the SDP develops/uploads the instrument and submits (**PM-11**, `fval-submit`); the Assistant Director allocates to an ASD (`fval-allocate`, `awaiting-allocation` stage); the ASD validates using the exact same checklist as EISA Instrument Validation (`buildInstrumentValidationChecklist()`, imported from `modules/instrument-validation/../mock/data/instrumentValidationChecklistItems.ts` — that file's own header comment already flagged this reuse back in Phase 2); the outcome (`fval-approve`/`fval-approve-with-conditions`/`fval-not-approved`) goes **straight back to the SDP** — there is no Deputy Director stage anywhere in `FISA_VALIDATION_WORKFLOW` (**PM-01**). Module/screen names are exactly "FISA Instrument Validation" and "FISA Validation Report" (**PM-02**, **PM-03** — `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx`'s tab label, never "EISA Validation Report" on a FISA screen).
- **FISA Registration** (`types.ts` `FisaRegistrationRecord`, `workflow.ts` `FISA_REGISTRATION_WORKFLOW`, `api.ts`'s `startFisaRegistration`/`performFisaRegistrationAction`, `external/NewRegistrationDialog.tsx`/`RegistrationDetail.tsx`, `internal/RegistrationWorkspace.tsx`) — a simplified version of EISA Registration: the SDP registers learners for a FISA sitting against a **validated** FISA instrument (`listValidatedFisaValidations()`), picking from the same simulated QA-domain enrolled-learner list and reusing the same backend-dependent stubs (`verifyLearnerIdentity`, `validateSorAgainstModules`) as EISA Registration. No EISA Calendar equivalent exists for FISA, so the SDP records the sitting date directly on the registration rather than picking a published sitting; no Trades/Non-Trades split and no RPL/ARPL indicator — those are EISA-specific concerns the register never asks for on the FISA side.

**RPL Toolkit Evaluation (`modules/rpl-toolkit/`) — PM-09.** Not in the change request register yet (confirmed with the developer before building — see "RPL Toolkit go-ahead" below). Built as a standalone module, self-contained with no inbound or outbound links to the other maps, behind `config/featureFlags.ts` → `RPL_TOOLKIT_ENABLED` (default `true`). Setting it to `false` hides the nav item on both portals (`config/nav.ts`'s `navForPortal`) and blocks the route itself (`index.tsx`'s `ModuleRoute`, same `RoleBlocked` defence-in-depth pattern as CR-018's SDP block), without deleting any code.

- Shape: QP submits (`external/NewApplicationForm.tsx`) → Assistant Director allocates to an ASD (`ad-allocation` stage, mirrors FISA Instrument Validation's allocation step) → ASD evaluation (shared checklist, `mock/data/rplToolkitChecklistItems.ts`, freshly built — no old-build or QCTO precedent exists) → Deputy Director review → Director & Team review (the existing `'Director & Team'` role from `config/roles.ts`, already wired to the `rpl-toolkit` team since Phase 0) → IAC review → CEO signs the outcome only (`RPL_OUTCOME_WORKFLOW`, no checklist, mirrors the QSA Addendum's CR-008 rule — the CEO never appears in `RPL_EVAL_WORKFLOW`). Corrections only ever travel back through the ASD (`rpl-return-to-qp`), never straight from DD/Director & Team/IAC to the QP — the same CR-012 rule applied domain-wide.
- `config/roles.ts`: ASD, Assistant Director and Deputy Director all gained the `fisa` and `rpl-toolkit` teams this phase (they were missing — only `Assistant Director`/`IAC`/`Qualifications Development` had `fisa`, and only `IAC`/`CEO`/`Director & Team` had `rpl-toolkit`, both left over as placeholders from Phase 0's module skeleton).

#### FISA / RPL Toolkit assumptions made

- **"Allocating to an ASD" is a free-text name, not a real assignment to one of several ASD accounts.** This build has no individual logins — only a role switcher — so `fval-allocate`/`rpl-allocate` just record a name for the audit trail/display (`FISA_VALIDATION_ALLOCATOR_ROLE` in `modules/fisa/config.ts`). Revisit if QCTO's real build gets individual ASD accounts.
- **PM-10's "Qualifications Development Director & Team" reuses the existing `'Qualifications Development'` role** for both supplying the source documents and the final sign-off, rather than introducing a new role — `config/roles.ts` already wired this role to the `fisa` team in Phase 0, and there is no separate "Director & Team" variant inside the Qualifications Development domain in this build's `Role` union.
- **The FISA Standards evaluation checklist (`mock/data/fisaStandardChecklistItems.ts`) and the RPL Toolkit checklist (`mock/data/rplToolkitChecklistItems.ts`) are both freshly built**, with no old-build or QCTO precedent — the old build only ever reached "FISA" via a failed QASA, reusing the QASA checklist, and RPL Toolkit Evaluation didn't exist in the old build at all. Flagged below for QCTO's real checklists. (FISA Instrument Validation's checklist is the one exception — it reuses `buildInstrumentValidationChecklist()` verbatim, per that file's own Phase 2 comment anticipating this reuse.)
- **FISA Registration has no calendar equivalent to publish sittings against** — the register's own wording for stage 4 is "the SDP registers learners for a FISA sitting (a simplified version of EISA registration; reuse shared pieces)", without asking for a FISA Calendar. The SDP enters the sitting date as a plain field on the registration instead.
- **RPL Toolkit go-ahead**: PM-09 was flagged per the developer's own build-prompt instruction as "not in the change request register yet — STOP and ask before building." Asked and confirmed before this phase began; the developer chose to build it now, behind the feature flag described above, rather than defer it.

### Fix 1B-i — Named-person identity

Built per `09-visibility-matrix.md`'s approved Section 3 (plus the "Approved corrections" appended to that doc). Identity only — no visibility filtering (that is 1B-ii).

- **Named users** → `mock/data/users.ts` (`USERS`, two per internal role, `managerId` models Grace Mokoena → Sipho Ndlovu and Peter Botha → Thandeka Zulu), `mock/data/qpDirectory.ts` (`QP_DIRECTORY`, stable `qpId`), `mock/data/sdpDirectory.ts` (gained `sdpId`, kept `name`/`compliant`).
- **Unified identity lookup** → `shared/people/types.ts` (`Person`), `shared/people/directory.ts` (`ALL_PEOPLE`, `personsForPortal`, `personsForRole`, `getPerson`, `defaultPersonForRole`, `defaultPersonForPortal`) — combines `USERS`, `QP_DIRECTORY`, `SDP_DIRECTORY` into one lookup keyed by a single `id`.
- **Role switcher → role-then-person switcher** → `shared/ui/RoleContext.tsx` now tracks `personId` as the actual state; `role` is derived from the active person. Storage is versioned (`assessmentsV2:activePersonId:v2:{portal}`); on first load it migrates the old `assessmentsV2:currentRole:{portal}` value to that role's first seeded person, and falls back to the portal's default person if both are missing/invalid — old localStorage never crashes the app. `shared/ui/RoleSwitcher.tsx` is now two `Select`s (role, then person); `shared/ui/useV2Role.ts` exposes `{ portal, role, setRole, person, personId, setPersonId }`.
- **"Reset demo data" wired up** → it existed only as an unused function before this fix. `shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx` now has a visible "Reset demo data" button next to the switcher that calls `resetDemoData()` and reloads the page (the identity key shares the `assessmentsV2:` prefix, so it's cleared too and falls back to the default person).
- **CR-053 "different person," not "different role"** → `shared/workflow/types.ts` (`WorkflowRecordState.submittedById`), `shared/workflow/engine.ts` (`performAction` now takes `actorId`, compares `submittedById` when present, falls back to the old name comparison only for state that predates this fix), `shared/audit/types.ts` (`AuditEntry.actorId`). Every module's `api.ts` threads `actorId` through every `performAction`/`appendAuditEntry` call; every internal/external screen's `const actorName = roleDescriptor(role)?.label ?? role` became `const { person } = useV2Role()` (`person.id` / `person.name`). `shared/audit/AuditTrailView.tsx` and the per-module audit lists now show the person's name, not just their role.
- **"Allocated to" is a named person, not free text** → FISA Instrument Validation (`modules/fisa/types.ts`/`api.ts`, `allocatedAsdId`) and RPL Toolkit (`modules/rpl-toolkit/types.ts`/`api.ts`, `allocatedAsdId`) both now allocate via a `Select` of `personsForRole('ASD')` instead of a typed name; the api validates the target is actually a seeded ASD.
- **CR-055 re-route targets a named colleague with the same role** → `modules/site-visits/types.ts` (`SiteVisit.reroutedToId`/`reroutedToName`), `api.ts`'s `performReportAction` (`report-reroute` now requires `targetPersonId`, validates the target holds `REPORT_APPROVER_ROLE`, and names them in the audit entry instead of "another Deputy Director"), `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s new `RerouteDialog` (person picker + mandatory reason).
- **"My items" helper** → `shared/people/myItems.ts` (`isMyItem(personId, ...candidateIds)`) — the predicate the 1B-ii "My items / All in my team" switch will call. Not wired into any list yet (that is visibility filtering, out of scope here).
- **Records link by id, display by name** → every record type across all 9 modules gained `qpId`/`sdpId` alongside the existing `qpName`/`sdpName` (QSA Addendum, Instrument Validation, EISA Calendar sittings/batches, EISA Registration, EISA Confirmation, Site Visits, Results Batches, FISA Validation/Registration, RPL Toolkit). Populated at creation time from the active person or from the upstream record it was derived from (e.g. a registration's `qpId` comes from the EISA Calendar sitting it was started against).
- **External "who am I" forms replaced with the active identity** → QSA Addendum's "Name of QP", RPL Toolkit's "Name of QP", EISA Registration's SDP picker, and both FISA external forms' SDP field no longer take free text / let you pick a different QP or SDP than yourself — they display the active person's name (read-only) and submit their id. Same change applied to the two remaining "viewing as QP" manual pickers (Site Visits' and Approval of Results' external dashboards), which now filter by `person.qpId` directly. Internal pickers that choose *someone else's* QP/SDP (Site Visits' `NewVisitDialog`, EISA Calendar's `AddEditSittingDialog`) stay as pickers, now sourced from `QP_DIRECTORY`/`SDP_DIRECTORY` by id instead of free text.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json`, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 1B-i assumptions made

- **CEO, Domain Director, Chief Director, Sub Domain Admin, Director & Team and Qualifications Development each got a second named person** beyond the one `09-visibility-matrix.md` Section 3 listed, so every internal role has at least two people (needed for "a different person, not just a different role" everywhere, not only on the roles the matrix already doubled up). Added: Nokuthula Dube (Domain Director), Francois Le Roux (Chief Director), Palesa Nkosi (IAC), David Molewa (CEO), Katlego Mahlangu (Sub Domain Admin), Johannes Pretorius (Director & Team), Ayesha Patel (Qualifications Development).
- **`requiresDifferentActor` falls back to a name comparison when `submittedById` is absent**, rather than treating missing state as "always different" or "always same." This only matters for workflow state created before this fix (there is none seeded — every record starts empty and is created through the UI — so in practice this fallback is dead code today, kept only so old localStorage from a pre-1B-i session degrades instead of behaving unpredictably).
- **A results batch, EISA sitting/batch, and EISA registration's "created by" identity doesn't get its own `*ById` field** on the few creation paths where no workflow `requiresDifferentActor` check ever reads it (FISA Standard's `qd-submission`, FISA Registration/Validation's first stage) — the workflow engine stamps `submittedById` itself from the first real transition onward, so this only skips an id that nothing would have checked anyway.

### Fix 1B-ii — Visibility enforcement

Built per `09-visibility-matrix.md` Sections 1–2 and the gap list in Section 4. **Note:** at the time this fix was built, `progress.md`'s Fix 1B-i entry referred to an "Approved corrections" section appended to `09-visibility-matrix.md` that did not yet exist in the committed doc — this fix was built directly off the matrix's Section 1/Section 2 tables and Section 5 assumptions as committed, plus that session's own instructions. QCTO's corrections have since been added to `09-visibility-matrix.md`'s "Approved corrections" section (2026-10-22); see "Fix 1B-ii correction — Approved corrections & 'My items'" below for what changed as a result.

- **ONE shared helper** → `shared/visibility/` (new): `config.ts` (the matrix's Table A as `EXTERNAL_MODULE_HIDDEN_FOR_ROLE`, Table B as `INTERNAL_MODULE_TEAM` + `INTERNAL_MODULE_ROLE_OVERRIDE` for EISA Confirmation's team-less gap), `moduleAccess.ts` (`isModuleVisible(portal, modulePath, role)` — the single yes/no used everywhere a module can be shown or hidden), `recordVisibility.ts` (`filterExternalRecords` for QP/SDP ownership filtering, `filterByVisibilityScope` for CR-054-style role→scope tables, `filterByScope`/`ListScope` for the internal "My items / All in my team" switch, reusing Fix 1B-i's `shared/people/myItems.ts`). `shared/ui/ScopeToggle.tsx` (new) is the one switch component every internal list renders.
- **Nav, dashboard tiles, route guards** → `config/nav.ts` no longer carries per-item `hiddenForRoles` arrays; `shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx` (nav), `shared/ui/Dashboard.tsx` (tiles), and `index.tsx`'s `ModuleRoute` (route guard) all call `isModuleVisible` instead. A direct URL to a module the active role can't see renders `shared/ui/RoleBlocked.tsx` ("You do not have access") — no data of any kind ever mounts. Internal dashboard tiles are now genuinely filtered by the active role's `teams` (closes Section 4 gap #7); previously every internal role saw all 9 tiles.
- **External nav hides added** (Section 1 Table A, Section 5 items 1–5) → SDP: `qsa-addendum`, `eisa-confirmation`, `site-visits`, `approval-of-results`, `rpl-toolkit` (plus the pre-existing CR-018 `instrument-validation` hide). QP: `eisa-registration`, `fisa`.
- **External record filtering by identity, not qualification** (Section 4 gap #5 / G-05/G-19) → every external dashboard now calls `filterExternalRecords`/`filterByVisibilityScope` against `person.qpId`/`person.sdpId`: QSA Addendum, EISA Instrument Validation, EISA Registration (SDP), EISA Confirmation (QP), FISA (SDP), RPL Toolkit (QP), EISA Calendar's "My Submissions" tab (QP) — Month/List View deliberately stay unfiltered (the matrix's one "See all" carve-out: a QP needs the whole published calendar to plan around). Approval of Results and Site Visits already filtered correctly after Fix 1B-i; both were switched onto the same shared helper for one code path instead of two.
- **Internal lists: "My items / All in my team"** (Section 1 "See unit" note) → `ScopeToggle` wired into all 9 internal dashboards (QSA Addendum, EISA Instrument Validation, EISA Calendar's batch-review queue, EISA Registration, EISA Confirmation, Site Visits, Approval of Results, FISA's three tabs, RPL Toolkit), default "My items". Each module supplies its own small `idsOf` function listing every id a record could be personally associated with (every workflow state's `submittedById`, `allocatedAsdId`, `reroutedToId`) — `filterByScope` does the actual filtering once. EISA Confirmation had no personal id at all (`generatedBy` was a name-only field); added `generatedById` alongside it, same pattern as every other id/name pair in this build, so the Assistant Director's own queue can be told apart from a colleague's.
- **CEO signature queue** (B-19) → QSA Addendum's and RPL Toolkit's internal dashboards special-case `role === 'CEO'`: the CEO sees only records at the `ceo-signature` stage, with the My items/team switch hidden (the queue is already maximally filtered — it isn't a team-breadth choice).
- **CR-054 wired into the real filter, not just a documented table** (Section 4 gap #6) → Site Visits' external `QpVisitsView` now calls `filterByVisibilityScope(visits, visibilityScopeForRole(role), person, v => v.qpId)` against `site-visits/config.ts`'s existing `REPORT_VISIBILITY_RULES`/`visibilityScopeForRole` — same result as before (QP sees only their own visits) but now demonstrably driven by the rule table, so a future QCTO-confirmed subdivision is a data change in that one table. Internal site-visits-team roles all have `scope: 'all'` in that table, which is why their "All in my team" view is genuinely the whole team's queue, not a second filter layer.
- **G-06: Site Visits planning form's SDP picker removed** → `site-visits/internal/NewVisitDialog.tsx` no longer lets a planner pick an SDP by hand (an SDP has no access to Site Visits at all). `site-visits/api.ts` gained `listSdpsForSitting()` (reads `eisa-registration/api.ts`'s `listRegisteredRegistrationsForSitting()` for the real SDP(s) already registered against the linked sitting) and `hooks.ts` gained `useSdpsForSitting()`; the dialog shows the result as plain read-only text ("Not applicable" until a sitting is linked, the registered SDP name(s) once it is) and submits the first match's id/name — never an editable field.
- **EISA Confirmation's visibility owner** (Section 4 gap #8) → `shared/visibility/config.ts`'s `INTERNAL_MODULE_ROLE_OVERRIDE` hard-codes `['Assistant Director']` for this one module path, per the matrix's own `[ASSUMPTION]`. Still an open question for QCTO (see below) — this is a one-line change once a real `confirmation` team exists.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` passes clean with zero errors; a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) reports zero errors inside `assessments-v2` (the pre-existing errors it surfaces in `src/pages/qualifications`, `src/pages/research`, `src/pages/QA`, `src/users` and `src/services` predate this fix and are outside this module's remit); `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 1B-ii — not yet enforced / open items

- **Tab-level sub-visibility** (matrix's "Sub-tabs" note — EISA Instrument Validation's Workspace/Status Dashboard tabs, EISA Calendar's Calendar/Audit Trail tabs) needed no separate code: every role that can reach those modules at all is already the one role on their single-role team (ASD), so module-level gating already covers it. Not re-verified tab-by-tab beyond that reasoning.
- **Detail-view guards are not independently re-checked.** Filtering happens at the list level (My items/All in my team, QP/SDP ownership); once a record is filtered out of a list there is no UI path left to open it, but a detail component itself does not re-assert "does this person have the right to view this specific record" if some future change opens a direct link to a record id. Flagged for Fix 2A's module-by-module audit to pick up if it becomes relevant.
- **EISA Calendar's directly-added sittings have no personal id to filter "My items" by** — `addSitting`'s `createdById` input is only ever used for the audit entry, not stored on the `EisaSitting` record itself. The My items/All in my team switch on the Proposals (batch review) tab works fully (batches do carry `state.submittedById`); the List View tab of directly-added/edited sittings is not switch-filtered. Revisit if QCTO wants per-ASD subdivision of directly-added sittings too.
- **"See unit" still means "whole process-stage team"** everywhere except the "My items" personal-id filter — there is still no finer subdivision (e.g. a sub-team within Site Visits), per the matrix's own note that this is unresolved pending QCTO.
- ~~The "Approved corrections" section referenced by Fix 1B-i's `progress.md` entry does not exist in `09-visibility-matrix.md`.~~ **Resolved 2026-10-22** — see "Fix 1B-ii correction — Approved corrections & 'My items'" below.

### Fix 1B-ii correction — Approved corrections & "My items" (2026-10-22)

QCTO's corrections were written back into `09-visibility-matrix.md`'s new "Approved corrections" section. Two of them required code changes beyond the doc itself:

- **SDP on Site Visits confirmed `None`** — already correct in the code (`site-visits/external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`'s `NotApplicableView`); no change needed. The SDP picker was already removed from visit planning in Fix 1B-ii's G-06 item (`site-visits/internal/NewVisitDialog.tsx`).
- **"My items" redefined** — it is no longer only "records this person is named on." It is now the union of (1) records submitted by or allocated to the active person (unchanged) and (2) unassigned records currently sitting at a stage the active person's **role** can act on, within their team. `shared/visibility/recordVisibility.ts`'s `filterByScope` gained a 5th, optional `isUnassignedActionable` callback; every one of the 9 internal dashboards that call it (`qsa-addendum`, `instrument-validation`, `eisa-registration`, `eisa-calendar`'s `BatchReviewPanel`, `site-visits`, `approval-of-results`, `fisa`'s three record types, `rpl-toolkit`) now passes a per-module function built on `shared/workflow/engine.ts`'s existing `canActInStage(definition, stageId, role)`, so a brand-new QP submission an ASD has never touched now correctly shows under their "My items" the moment it reaches a stage their role can act on. Where a record carries a specific-person field (`allocatedAsdId` in FISA Instrument Validation/RPL Toolkit, `reroutedToId` in Site Visits), that exclusivity is preserved: once assigned to a *named* person, it only shows as "mine" for that person, not for every other person who shares their role. EISA Confirmation was left unchanged — once generated, its only remaining stages are QP-only, so there is no internal "unassigned, actionable" state for it to catch. "All in my team" is unchanged.

### Fix 2A — Workflow-bypass bugs & form validation

Rows 5, 6, 10, 11 of `08-test-findings.md` (B-05, B-08, B-10, B-11, B-15), plus a module-by-module audit of the other 8 modules for the same pattern.

- **Root cause of rows 5/6/B-10/B-11/B-15** → `modules/qsa-addendum/internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s evaluation tab had three `ActionBar`s (ASD's, inside the "Deputy Director Review" box, inside the IAC box) that were all driven off the record's *current* stage rather than each bar's *own* owning stage — so whichever stage was active, every bar independently recomputed "does this role act in this stage" and rendered it, producing duplicate/triple buttons and showing the DD's checklist+button to the ASD before the DD's turn. Fixed by gating each bar (and the DD/IAC boxes themselves) to render only while `evalState.stageId` exactly equals that bar's own stage (DD review hidden entirely until `dd-review`; IAC box hidden until `iac-review` or a final decision). Each stage now renders exactly one action bar, and a stage's checklist is only visible once that stage has actually been reached.
- **Same pattern found and fixed in FISA Standards** (`modules/fisa/internal/StandardWorkspace.tsx`) → the Details tab rendered an unconditional `ActionBar` (no completeness gate) *and* the Evaluation tab rendered a second one for the same stage — the ASD could send to the Deputy Director straight from the Details tab without finishing the checklist/report, and every later stage (DD/IAC/QD) got duplicated the same way. Removed the Details-tab bar; the Evaluation tab's (correctly gated) bar is now the only one.
- **Row 10 (B-08)** → `modules/qsa-addendum/workflow.ts`'s `ack-send-back` action gained `requiresNote: true`. `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s Acknowledgement tab now shows the DD's reason inline to the ASD (in addition to the existing Audit Trail tab) whenever the letter is back at `asd-preview` with a "Send back to ASD" entry.
- **Row 11 (B-05) — ONE shared helper** → `shared/validation/` (new): `types.ts` (`ValidationIssue`), `ValidationSummary.tsx` (`ValidationSummary` — the summary banner listing every outstanding issue; `FieldError` — the per-field companion placed directly under an input). `shared/checklist/types.ts` gained `getChecklistIssues()` (the per-line messages behind `isChecklistComplete`'s yes/no); `shared/checklist/Checklist.tsx` now renders `ValidationSummary` itself whenever `!readonly`, so **every** checklist in the domain (QSA, Instrument Validation ×2, Site Visits, Approval of Results, FISA ×2, RPL Toolkit) gets the summary for free with no per-module wiring. Wired explicitly (summary + per-field `FieldError`) into every evaluation-report-style form that blocks Submit on non-checklist fields: QSA Addendum's evaluation report and new-application SAQA-gate-check form (B-05's actual screen — the Submit button was disabled with no field-level or summary message for a missing NQF Level or an incomplete gate check), FISA Standards' evaluation report, RPL Toolkit's evaluation report, Instrument Validation's (EISA and FISA) outcome buttons, Approval of Results' compliance-review advance action.
- **Found during the module audit (item 2), not from a listed row** → `modules/site-visits/internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s "Submit Report" button had no completeness gate at all (`disabled={reportAction.isPending}` only) — the ASD could submit a report with a blank checklist, bypassing CR-011. Gated on `isChecklistComplete` and wired to `ValidationSummary`.
- **Modules audited for duplicate action bars / actions bypassing `shared/workflow` / wrong-role-visible actions / premature-enabled Submit** (CLAUDE.md's "workflows are data, not scattered `if` statements" rule): `instrument-validation` (clean — one outcome-button set, correctly role/stage-gated; added `ValidationSummary` for consistency), `eisa-calendar` (clean — single action set per row, all routed through `useBatchAction`/`useSittingLifecycleAction`), `eisa-registration` (clean — single "Create Registration Number" action, already blocked with a visible message while any SOR is flagged), `eisa-confirmation` (clean — exactly one stage-gated action at a time), `site-visits` (one bug found and fixed, above — planning chain and report-review action bars were already correctly single/stage-gated), `approval-of-results` (clean — the Compliance Review tab's bar and the Approval Chain tab's bar use mutually exclusive stage conditions, so they never both show; added `ValidationSummary`), `fisa` (one bug found and fixed, above, in Standards; Instrument Validation and Registration sub-flows were already clean), `rpl-toolkit` (clean — one checklist, one `ActionBar`; added `ValidationSummary`/`FieldError` to its evaluation report).
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 2A assumptions made

- **A stage's checklist/action-bar box is hidden entirely (not just read-only) until that stage is actually reached**, rather than shown read-only in advance — the test findings specifically describe the ASD seeing the DD's checklist/button *while doing their own evaluation* (i.e. ahead of time), so "owns that stage" was read as a visibility gate, not just an edit gate. Once a box's stage is reached, its checklist stays visible read-only to every later-stage viewer (e.g. IAC can see the DD's completed checklist) — nothing in the test findings asked for hiding completed work from downstream reviewers, and they need that context to do their own review.
- **The acknowledgement "send back" note is shown inline only while the letter is back at the ASD's stage with an unresolved send-back** (not permanently) — the full history is always available on the Details tab's Audit Trail regardless.

### Fix 2B — QSA Addendum flow changes

Rows 7, 8, 9, 13, 14 and 16 of `08-test-findings.md` (B-10, B-11, B-13, B-14, B-18, B-21, B-24, B-25). Row 15 skipped (blocked until the QCTO documents). All transitions still go through `shared/workflow`.

- **Row 7 — automatic evaluation outcome** → `mock/integrations/aiValidation.ts`'s new `autoEvaluateQsaChecklist()` (a simulated stand-in for CR-009/CR-010's real AI-assisted check — unlike the `notImplemented()` stubs above it in that file, this one actually runs now). A "No" on a mandatory checklist line makes the outcome NEGATIVE, otherwise POSITIVE; which lines count as mandatory is `qsa-addendum/config.ts` → `QSA_AUTO_EVALUATION_MANDATORY_ITEM_IDS` (`'all'` by default — every line is mandatory, since the register doesn't name a smaller subset). The ASD still has two separate workflow actions (`eval-send-to-dd`, `eval-return-to-qp`) — not one conditional action — but `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s evaluation tab now renders only the ONE button the computed outcome picked (an outcome banner explains which, and that it's automatic), and `api.ts`'s `performEvaluationAction` re-checks the same computation server-side and rejects the call if the ASD's action doesn't match it — the ASD cannot override the outcome, only add findings/notes.
- **Rows 8/9 — redo visibility and the QP's negative-report loop** → `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s Deputy Director and IAC review boxes are no longer gated to "only while that's the current stage" (that was Fix 2A's fix for the *duplicate-button* bug) — they now also stay visible, read-only, once "touched" (`ddReviewTouched`/`iacTouched`, derived from whether the DD checklist has any answers / an IAC presentation was uploaded), so when a DD return or IAC remediation sends the record back to `asd-evaluation`, the ASD still sees the reviewer's completed checklist. The DD's "Return to ASD" reason and the IAC's "Send for remediation" reason are also surfaced inline on the evaluation tab while the ASD is back at that stage (same pattern as Fix 2A's acknowledgement send-back note). The redone report runs through the exact same `eval-send-to-dd`/`eval-return-to-qp` auto-routing as the first cycle — no special-case code was needed for "a redone report gets re-evaluated automatically" since it's the same stage, same logic, every cycle. `components/CorrectionsPanel.tsx` (shown to the QP whenever `evalState.stageId === 'qp-corrections'`, on both portals) now also renders the full evaluation report (findings, recommendation) and the checklist itself (read-only, via the shared `Checklist` component) — not just the one-line send-back note — so "the report reaches the QP together with the evaluation" is literal. `eval-qp-resubmit` (already existed) is how the QP corrects and resubmits; there is still no DD → QP action anywhere in the code.
- **Row 13 — new approval-letter flow** → `workflow.ts`'s `APPROVAL_LETTER_WORKFLOW` is now four stages (`asd-draft → dd-review → signed → sent-to-qp`) instead of one (`pending-signature → signed`). `types.ts` gained `ApprovalLetterDraft` (`{ notes: string }`, the ASD's one editable field — `shared/letters`' `LetterData` only exposes one free-text slot) and `approvalLetterSignedBy` (the Deputy Director's name, captured the moment they sign in `api.ts`'s `performApprovalLetterAction`, because `approvalState.submittedBy` itself gets overwritten by the ASD's name on the later "send to QP" step). `api.ts`'s `performOutcomeAction` now creates the approval record at `asd-draft` with an empty draft, instead of straight at the old `pending-signature`. New action `saveApprovalLetterDraft()` / `useSaveApprovalLetterDraft()`. `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s Approval Letter tab: ASD edits the draft notes and submits (`approval-submit-to-dd`); the Deputy Director signs (`approval-sign`) or declines with a mandatory note (`approval-decline`, back to `asd-draft`, reason shown inline to the ASD the same way as the other send-back notes in this module); once signed, the ASD sends it on to the QP (`approval-send-to-qp`) — the QP only ever sees the letter (on the external `ApplicationDetail.tsx`) once it reaches `sent-to-qp`. `APPROVAL_LETTER_SIGNER_ROLE` (`config.ts`) stays the one constant controlling who signs — still Deputy Director, now answered rather than merely defaulted (see "Open questions" below). Because "approved" now has two intermediate stages before the letter physically reaches the QP, `workflow.ts` gained `isApprovalLetterFinal()` (`signed` OR `sent-to-qp`) for the places that only care "is this QSA Addendum approved" — `listApprovedQsaAddenda()` (read by EISA Instrument Validation and the EISA Calendar) and both dashboards' "Approved" stat card — while the two dashboards' and letter view's own fine-grained stage display still distinguishes `signed` from `sent-to-qp`.
- **Row 14 — tabs** → the inline `AuditTrail` component inside `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx` was extracted to `components/AuditTrail.tsx` (`QsaAuditTrail`) and is now its own "Audit Trail" tab on both views, no longer buried inside Details. `external/ApplicationDetail.tsx` (previously a single scrolling dialog, no tabs at all) was rebuilt with the same `Tabs` shape as the internal workspace: Details / Acknowledgement / Outcome Letter / Approval Letter / Audit Trail — each letter only renders once it's actually reached the QP's side of the flow (e.g. the Approval Letter tab stays "not yet available" until `sent-to-qp`), otherwise a plain explanatory message.
- **Row 16 (Ask QCTO)** → left as-is; already tracked in "Open questions for QCTO" (CR-004's new-qualification pathway) from an earlier round — not duplicated here.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 2B assumptions made

- **The ASD's "editable fields" on the approval letter draft means the one free-text `notes` field** `shared/letters`' `LetterData` exposes — the register doesn't name specific letter fields as editable vs. fixed, and the letter's identifying data (QP name, qualification, SAQA ID, NQF level) is already correct from the application record, so there's nothing else on the letter that should be hand-edited.
- **The automatic evaluation outcome's "mandatory line" defaults to every QSA checklist line being mandatory** (`QSA_AUTO_EVALUATION_MANDATORY_ITEM_IDS = 'all'`) — the test finding's own wording ("any No on a mandatory line, or a configurable rule") doesn't name a smaller subset, and the checklist type has no existing "mandatory" flag to read one from. One array to change if QCTO names a narrower set of lines.
- **Sending the negative report back to the QP no longer requires a note** (`eval-return-to-qp` dropped `requiresNote: true`) — it used to be a manual, discretionary "I'm sending this back" decision (CR-056-style), but it's now an automatic consequence of the checklist, not a judgement call that needs justifying; the checklist's own mandatory per-line comments (CR-011) and the evaluation report's findings already carry the reasoning. The ASD can still add free-text findings — just not through a mandatory note dialog on this specific action.
- **The DD/IAC review boxes staying visible "once touched" is not reset on a redo cycle** — unlike Instrument Validation's CR-023 loop (which explicitly rebuilds a blank checklist every cycle), nothing in the register or the test findings asks for the DD review checklist or IAC presentation to be cleared when the ASD redoes and resubmits. They stay as read-only context for whoever reaches that stage again, and remain editable again once it's genuinely their stage once more.

### Fix 2C — Instrument Validation, EISA Calendar, EISA Registration, help text

Rows 17, 18, 20, 21, 22, 23, 24, 25, 26, 27 of `08-test-findings.md` (C-06, C-07, C-11, D-08, D-11, D-12, D-17, E-06, E-07, E-10, E-12, F-02, F-05). Row 19 skipped — blocked until the QCTO instrument-validation document arrives, per instruction.

- **Row 17 (C-11) — mandatory conditions note** → `modules/instrument-validation/workflow.ts`'s `val-approve-with-conditions` action gained `requiresNote: true`. `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx`'s "Approved with conditions" button now opens a `ReasonDialog` (gained an optional `fieldLabel`/`placeholder` prop on `shared/ui/ReasonDialog.tsx` for this — "Conditions \*" instead of the default "Reason \*") instead of recording the outcome directly. The note lands in `ValidationOutcome.notes` exactly like every other outcome, so it was already wired to show on: the QP's outcome view (`external/ValidationDetail.tsx`'s "Validated" banner), the internal outcome history, and the audit trail (the action's note is passed straight into `performAction`, which writes it to the shared audit log) — no separate plumbing needed once the note became mandatory and captured.
- **Row 18 (C-06, C-07) — QP-side tabs** → `modules/instrument-validation/external/ValidationDetail.tsx` rebuilt from one long scrolling dialog into the same tabbed shape as the internal workspace: Details / Scheduling / Instrument / Validation outcome / Audit trail. The audit-trail renderer was extracted from `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx` into a new shared `components/AuditTrail.tsx` (`InstrumentValidationAuditTrail`) so both views render identical entries from one place instead of two copies drifting apart.
- **Row 21 (D-08) — rejected batch edit & resubmit** → `modules/eisa-calendar/types.ts`'s `EisaSittingBatch` gained `rejectionHistory: BatchRejectionHistoryEntry[]` (every past rejection reason, `rejectionReason` still holds only the latest for backward display compatibility). `workflow.ts`'s `BATCH_WORKFLOW` `rejected` stage is no longer terminal — it now allows the `QP` role and a new `batch-resubmit` action (`rejected → qcto-review`). `api.ts`'s new `resubmitBatch()` re-validates the corrected dates (count, duplicates, year, `checkSittingDate`) exactly like the original proposal, replaces the batch's sittings with fresh `proposed` rows, and never clears `rejectionHistory`. New `external/ResubmitBatchDialog.tsx` (QP-only, shows every past rejection reason above the editable date list) wired into `external/QpCalendarView.tsx`'s "My Submissions" tab via a new "Correct & resubmit" button, shown only while `state.stageId === 'rejected'`. `internal/BatchReviewPanel.tsx` also switched from showing only the latest `rejectionReason` to the full `rejectionHistory` list, so the ASD sees the same history the QP does.
- **Rows 22/23 (D-11, D-12, D-17 — Ask QCTO, built the stated default)**:
  - **Postpone gets an optional proposed new date** → `types.ts`'s `EisaSitting.proposedNewDate`, `api.ts`'s `performSittingLifecycleAction` accepts an optional `proposedNewDate` (postpone-only), folds it into both the audit note (`"<reason> (Proposed new date: <date>)"`) and the SDP/QP notification body, and stores it on the sitting for display. New `internal/PostponeSittingDialog.tsx` (reason textarea + optional date picker) replaces the shared `ReasonDialog` for postpone specifically (cancel still uses `ReasonDialog`, unchanged — it already required a reason). `components/SittingListView.tsx` shows the proposed date under the postponement reason. No negotiation loop was built — exactly the suggested default.
  - **Delete is blocked when a sitting already has registrations** → `modules/eisa-registration/api.ts` gained `listRegistrationsForSitting()` (any stage, not only `registered`) and `listSittingIdsWithRegistrations()`; `eisa-calendar/api.ts`'s `deleteSitting()` calls the former and throws a clear message ("...cannot be deleted. Postpone or cancel it instead.") instead of deleting. `internal/InternalCalendarView.tsx` also pre-empts this in the UI: a new `useSittingIdsWithRegistrations()` hook feeds `components/SittingListView.tsx`'s `sittingIdsWithRegistrations` set, which disables the Delete button (with an explanatory `title` tooltip) before the official even clicks it — the thrown error is still the defence-in-depth backstop. Edit was left exactly as it was (no new restriction) — only Delete changed.
  - Both open questions recorded below.
- **Row 24 (E-07) — withdraw a draft + stop the stuck-duplicate bug at the root** → `modules/eisa-registration/api.ts`'s `startRegistration()` now blocks a second *open* (not yet `registered`) registration for the same `sdpId` + `sittingId`, which is the actual cause of row 24's "duplicate 'Building registration' with no learners left to add" — an SDP can no longer create that second record in the first place. New `withdrawRegistration()` additionally lets the SDP delete a draft (`sdp-submission` stage only) outright, for the rare case where one is already stuck from before this fix, or simply no longer wanted. `external/RegistrationDetail.tsx` gained a "Withdraw registration" button (shown while `canAct && isBuilding`) behind a shadcn `AlertDialog` confirmation, closing the detail dialog on success.
- **Row 25 (E-10) — five-state SOR status** → `modules/eisa-registration/types.ts`'s `SorStatus` is now `'uploaded' | 'under-review' | 'accepted' | 'flagged' | 'replaced'` (nullable on the learner — `null` means no SOR uploaded yet). `api.ts`: `uploadLearnerSor()` sets `'uploaded'` on a learner's first SOR and `'replaced'` on every subsequent one (whether or not it was previously flagged); two new functions, `markLearnerSorUnderReview()` and `acceptLearnerSor()` (both Assistant-Director-only, `ad-review` stage only, each writing its own audit entry), sit alongside the existing `flagLearnerSor()`. New `components/SorStatusBadge.tsx` (5 colours) is shown on the learner row on **both** sides: `internal/RegistrationWorkspace.tsx` (plus two new buttons, "Under review" and "Accept", next to the existing "Flag SOR") and `external/RegistrationDetail.tsx` (status only — the SDP doesn't drive these transitions, matching CR-038's existing pattern where only the Assistant Director decides SOR status).
- **Row 26 (E-12, Ask QCTO) — confirmed by the register** → SOR stays per-learner, unchanged. Recorded as an open question below per instruction, even though the register already reads unambiguously ("an SOR element per learner").
- **Row 27 (F-02, F-05) — one-line help text** → every one of the five named sections already had *some* one-line description under its title; this pass rewrote the thin/generic ones into "what is this section for" statements and, specifically for the Confirmation ↔ Site Visits confusion the testers hit (F-02: "why is confirmation there if the qualification is already in the sitvisit section"; F-05: "it was already listed at sitvisits but it did not create a new one"), used the exact text supplied for Confirmation and added the mirror-image sentence to Site Visits:
  - `modules/eisa-confirmation/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` and `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`: "After registration is complete [the QP/you] confirms centres, learner counts and marking/moderation dates. RPL monitoring items in Site Visits come from the registration, not from Confirmation."
  - `modules/site-visits/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` and `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`: added "...an RPL/ARPL monitoring item is created automatically from a registration, never from EISA Confirmation."
  - `modules/instrument-validation/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` and `modules/eisa-registration/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`: replaced a one-line "this is a separate section from X" note with a line that also says what the section is *for*, not just what it isn't.
  - EISA Calendar's and the external Instrument Validation/Registration dashboards' existing one-liners were already clear "what this is for" statements and were left unchanged.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 2C assumptions made

- **"A replacement sets Replaced" (row 25) applies to any re-upload that overwrites an existing SOR file, not only a corrected-after-flag one.** The row's own wording pairs "Uploading sets Uploaded" against "a replacement sets Replaced" without scoping the second case to the flagged-correction path specifically, and `uploadLearnerSor()`'s existing `canUpload` guard already permits re-uploading during `sdp-submission` (before the flagged-correction scenario can even exist) — so the simpler, uniform reading (first file ever = Uploaded, every file after that = Replaced) was built instead of a narrower one.
- **Registration-number creation still only requires "no flagged SOR,"** not "every SOR Accepted" — row 25 only asked for the five-state display and the Assistant Director's three review actions to exist; strengthening the `reg-create-number` gate to require universal acceptance would be a behaviour change beyond what was asked, so `performRegistrationAction`'s existing gate is untouched. Revisit if QCTO wants every SOR formally Accepted before a registration number can be created.
- **"An SDP cannot start a second open registration for the same sitting" (row 24) is read as scoped to that one SDP** (`sdpId` + `sittingId`), not "only one open registration per sitting across every SDP" — multiple SDPs legitimately register different learners for the same published sitting.
- **Postpone's proposed new date (row 22) is purely informational — it does not create a new `EisaSitting` row or reserve the date.** The suggested default explicitly says "no negotiation"; QCTO still needs to separately add a new sitting (CR-027) if the postponed one is actually rescheduled, same as the existing "postponing does not auto-create a replacement date" assumption from Phase 3.
- **"Has registrations" (row 23/D-17's delete guard) means any registration record at all (draft or completed), not only a completed one.** A draft (`sdp-submission`) or in-review (`ad-review`) registration already represents real SDP/learner work tied to that sitting — deleting the sitting out from under it would orphan that work just as much as deleting it under a completed one would.
- **EISA Calendar reading from EISA Registration (`eisa-calendar/api.ts` importing `listRegistrationsForSitting`/`listSittingIdsWithRegistrations` from `eisa-registration/api.ts`) is the first reversal of this domain's usual upstream-reads-downstream direction** (Registration already reads Calendar's `listPublishedSittings`). This is a circular ES module reference; it is safe here because both sides only call each other's exported functions at runtime (never at module-evaluation time), which Vite/Rollup handle correctly — but it is a deliberate one-off, not a pattern to repeat casually. Flag to the developer if this needs un-tangling later (e.g. by moving the registration-count check into a small shared store, the same way Phase 4/5's RPL/ARPL trigger used `mock/api/siteVisitsStore.ts` to avoid a similar cycle).

### Fix 4 (Part 1) — Site Visits planning and execution

Rows 32, 33, 34, 35 of `08-test-findings.md` (G-08, G-09, G-13, G-18) — planning and execution only. Part 2 (Share Visit Outcome/report visibility/results-per-learner, rows 36-40) is built — see "Fix 4 (Part 2)" below.

- **Row 32 — the plan no longer disappears** → `modules/site-visits/internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s `PlanningTab` was rewritten from "one mutually-exclusive box per current stage" (which is what made the Assistant Director's plan note vanish once the Deputy Director's box took over, and vanish again at Sub Domain Admin booking) into a persistent stack: the Assistant Director's plan note box is always shown; each of the three review boxes (Deputy Director, Domain Director, Sub Domain Admin) renders once that stage has ever been reached — using the audit trail (`getAuditTrail('site-visit-planning', visit.id)`), not the current stage index, so a box stays visible even after a later send-back moves the record behind it — and shows either its live action buttons (current stage), a "done" read-out (passed forward), or a "sent back for clarification" read-out with the actual reason (behind its own position because of a send-back). Nothing is hidden once it has happened.
- **Row 33 — Reject and Send back on every planning review stage** → `modules/site-visits/workflow.ts`'s `PLANNING_WORKFLOW` gained a new terminal stage (`planning-rejected`) and six new actions: `plan-dd-send-back`/`plan-dd-reject` (Deputy Director, from `dd-recommend`), `plan-domain-director-send-back`/`plan-domain-director-reject` (Domain Director, from `domain-director-approval`), `plan-sda-send-back`/`plan-sda-reject` (Sub Domain Admin, from `sda-booking`) — all `requiresNote: true`, enforced by the shared workflow engine exactly like every other mandatory-note action in this domain. Send back returns to the immediately preceding stage; Reject is terminal. `modules/site-visits/api.ts`'s `performPlanningAction` notifies the right role for each (send-back notifies the stage being returned to; reject notifies the Assistant Director, since there's no "next stage" to tell and they're the one who'd need to raise a fresh plan) and writes an audit entry via the engine either way. **Not given a Reject/Send-back pair: `ad-planning` itself** — see "Fix 4 (Part 1) assumptions" below.
