- **Row 34 — QP and qualification selectors on the execution form (CR-049)** → `modules/site-visits/components/ExecutionForm.tsx` gained a new `QpAndQualificationSelectors` block at the top of the form (above the checklist): a Qualification `Select` sourced from `mock/data/qpDirectory.ts`'s `QP_DIRECTORY`, and a QP `Select` filtered to only the QPs linked to the chosen qualification. Changing the qualification resets the QP to the first match (or clears it) if the current QP no longer qualifies. Both are pre-filled from the visit's existing `qpId`/`qualificationTitle` (themselves already pre-filled from the linked sitting at creation time, per CR-050 — nothing new needed there), editable only while the ASD can execute (`readonly` prop, same gate as the rest of the form), and write through the existing `updateVisitDetails` mutation (`api.ts`'s patch type gained `qpId` so the selector's choice doesn't get silently dropped the way the Details tab's plain QP Name text field already could).
- **Row 35 — re-route notifies the new owner** → the targeting rules (named colleague, same role only, mandatory note, shown as the new owner on the record) were already built in Fix 1B-i; the one gap was that `performReportAction`'s `report-reroute` branch wrote only an audit entry, never a notification. `modules/site-visits/api.ts` now also calls `addNotification` for the target's role when a re-route happens. See "Open questions" below for the notification's role-level (not person-level) limitation, and for the new open question on allowed re-route targets.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms only `modules/site-visits/{workflow.ts,api.ts,components/ExecutionForm.tsx,internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx}` changed, all inside `src/features/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 4 (Part 1) assumptions made

- **`ad-planning` has no Reject/Send-back pair.** It's the stage where the plan originates — there is no prior owner's submission to reject or send back, unlike the three review stages downstream of it. This mirrors CR-056's existing "every approval stage" pattern elsewhere in the domain (approval stages review someone else's prior work; an originating stage doesn't). If QCTO wants the Assistant Director to be able to abandon/withdraw a plan before submitting, that's a different action (delete a draft) than Reject/Send-back and isn't built here.
- **Each send-back/reject action got its own distinct workflow label** (e.g. "Send back to Assistant Director" vs. "Send back to Deputy Director" vs. "Send back to Domain Director") instead of reusing one generic "Send back for clarification" label across all three stages, purely so the UI can look up the right historical note by action text from the audit trail without ambiguity. The button text shown to the user still reads "Send back for clarification" / "Reject" everywhere, matching the rest of the domain's language.
- **The re-route notification is role-level, not person-level** (`shared/notifications/store.ts` only ever keyed notifications by `recipientRole`) — every Deputy Director sees it, not only the named target, the same limitation every other notification in this domain already has (nothing in Fix 1B-i person-ified notifications, only workflow/audit). The notification body names the specific colleague so it still reads correctly for them.
- **The Qualification/QP selector's "known qualifications" list is `QP_DIRECTORY`'s own list** (Electrical Trades / Plumbing / Welding) — the same source `NewVisitDialog.tsx`'s QP picker already uses — rather than a separate master qualifications list, since none exists elsewhere in this build. If a visit's existing qualification title isn't one of these (e.g. typed freely elsewhere), it's kept as an extra selectable option so the selector never silently discards existing data.
- **Row 32's "to the QP where the matrix allows" is read as: nowhere, for planning.** `09-visibility-matrix.md`'s Section 2 "Site Visit (planning)" row lists only the four internal planning roles as viewers — QP isn't one of them (unlike "Site Visit Report," which the matrix explicitly opens to the QP once shared). `external/VisitDetail.tsx` was left untouched; it already shows only Status/Visit Date/Venue/Notification and the outcome-sharing step, never the plan note or planning chain.

### Fix 4 Part 1 correction — Deputy Director's "Recommend" button missing (2026-10-23)

**Cause.** Row 32's rewrite of `modules/site-visits/internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s `PlanningTab` (making each reviewer's box a persistent, audit-trail-driven stack instead of one box per current stage) computed `ddReached`/`domainDirectorReached`/`sdaReached` by checking the audit trail only for actions taken **FROM** that stage (e.g. `ddReached` looked for `'Recommend to Domain Director'` or `'Send back to Assistant Director'`). It never checked for the action that **enters** the stage in the first place (`'Submit Plan'`, whose `toStage` is `dd-recommend`). The moment the Assistant Director submitted a plan, the only audit entry that existed was `'Submit Plan'` itself — which `ddReached` didn't recognise — so the whole "Deputy Director recommendation" box, including its action bar, never rendered. The same gap existed one stage further down for the Domain Director (missing `'Recommend to Domain Director'` as an entry trigger) and the Sub Domain Admin (missing `'Approve plan'`). Each reviewer's box was structurally unable to appear the first time their stage was actually reached — it could only appear in its read-only "already passed" state, after the fact. `workflow.ts`'s `PLANNING_WORKFLOW` itself, `config.ts`'s role constants, and the dashboard's "My items" visibility filter (`InternalDashboard.tsx`'s `isUnassignedActionable`, from the Fix 1B-ii correction) were all already correct — the Deputy Director could already see the visit in their queue and open it; the button inside was simply never drawn.
- **The fix** → `VisitWorkspace.tsx` gained `stageReached(stageId, auditTrail)`, which derives the full set of relevant audit-entry labels directly from `PLANNING_WORKFLOW.actions` (every action whose `fromStage` **or** `toStage` equals the stage in question), instead of a hand-picked, incomplete pair of labels per stage. `ddReached`/`domainDirectorReached`/`sdaReached` now all call it. Because the label set is derived from the workflow definition itself, this can't silently drift out of sync with `workflow.ts` again the way the hard-coded string pairs did.
- **Also fixed while tracing the chain** → `api.ts`'s `performPlanningAction` notified the next approver on `plan-recommend` (Domain Director) but not on `plan-submit` (Deputy Director) or `plan-approve` (Sub Domain Admin) — so even once the button is visible, the Deputy Director and Sub Domain Admin had no in-app notification telling them a plan was waiting; they'd only find it by opening the dashboard. Both now call `addNotification`, matching the existing `plan-recommend` pattern.
- **The check that would have caught it** → the Phase 5/Fix 4 Part 1 click-through script was never actually re-run end-to-end as four *different* role switches after the Row 32 rewrite — only the "boxes stay visible" visual behaviour was checked, from a state already past `dd-recommend`. Walking the chain forward one stage at a time, switching role at each step (Assistant Director → Deputy Director → Domain Director → Sub Domain Admin), would have hit the missing button on the very first handoff. That walk is now the click-through script below, and is worth re-running after any future change to `PLANNING_WORKFLOW` or `PlanningTab`.
- Verified the same fix covers both Marking & Moderation and Post-EISA Monitoring visits: both visit types share the exact same `planningWorkflow: WorkflowRecordState` field, the same `PLANNING_WORKFLOW` definition, and the same `PlanningTab` component — nothing in that component branches on `visitType` (only on `sourceRegistrationId`, for the RPL/ARPL auto-created visits that skip the chain entirely, per the existing Fix 4 Part 1 assumption above). There is only one planning-chain code path in this module, not one per visit type.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms only `modules/site-visits/{api.ts,internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx}` changed, both inside `src/features/assessments-v2/`.

### Fix 4 (Part 2) — Share Visit Outcome, report visibility, per-learner results

Rows 36, 37, 38, 39, 40 of `08-test-findings.md` (G-19, G-23, G-24, G-25, G-26, G-06, G-07, H-05, H-11), plus rows 29 and 30. Rows 31 and 41 skipped (blocked until the QCTO documents), per instruction.

- **Row 37 (G-23) — the actual bug** → `modules/site-visits/workflow.ts`'s `REPORT_WORKFLOW` had `{ id: 'approved', allowedRoles: [] }` — so the moment the Deputy Director clicked "Share Visit Outcome" (an action FROM `approved`), the shared workflow engine's `canActInStage` check in `performAction` silently rejected it ("Role may not act in stage 'approved'"), and nothing was ever shared. Fixed by giving that stage `allowedRoles: [REPORT_APPROVER_ROLE]` — the only role with any action from that stage. `modules/site-visits/internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s `ReportTab` button is still the single action bar for this stage (unchanged — it only ever rendered one button), but its gate changed from a bare `role === REPORT_APPROVER_ROLE` comparison to `canActInStage(REPORT_WORKFLOW, stage, role)`, so the button and the engine can never drift apart again. The button label and the workflow action's own label both now read from `config.ts`'s `SHARE_VISIT_OUTCOME_LABEL` constant (previously declared but unused) instead of a duplicated string literal.
- **Row 37 — "a QP must see nothing before it is shared"** → before this fix, a QP's own visit list/detail already hid the actual report content pre-share, but the status badge itself leaked the outcome early — `overallStatusLabel`/`overallStatusTone` (`workflow.ts`) would show "Report with Deputy Director" or "Report approved" to the QP while the Deputy Director was still reviewing a Post-EISA Monitoring report, before `share-visit-outcome` was ever clicked. Both functions gained an optional `{ forQp?: boolean }` second argument; when set, a Post-EISA Monitoring visit at `dd-report-review` or `approved` now shows a neutral "Visit completed — outcome pending" instead. `components/StatusBadge.tsx`'s `SiteVisitStatusBadge` gained a `forQp` prop that forwards this; `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`'s visits table and `external/VisitDetail.tsx`'s status row both now pass `forQp`. Every other visit type has no share step at all, so a QP's status there is unaffected — "Report approved"/"Report rejected" is already the legitimate final word for those.
- **Rows 36/38 (G-19, G-26) — report visibility + showing the report on Approval of Results** → `modules/site-visits/config.ts`'s `REPORT_VISIBILITY_RULES` (CR-054) gained explicit entries for `Director` and `Chief Director` (`scope: 'all'`), since Approval of Results — built in Phase 6, after the visibility matrix was drafted — now reads this same table from a screen those two roles use but aren't on the `site-visits` process-stage team for. Built as the stated default (every Approval-of-Results reviewer sees the full linked report, same as every site-visits-team role already does); without this, `visibilityScopeForRole` would have returned `null` for both and the no-rule default (`filterByVisibilityScope` treats `null` as "all") would have granted the same access invisibly, undocumented. `modules/approval-of-results/internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx`'s "Marking & Moderation Monitoring Status (CR-052)" panel on the Details tab now shows, for each linked visit: its real status label (`site-visits/workflow.ts`'s `overallStatusLabel`, new export), and — once the report is actually `reportIsApproved` (approved, outcome-shared or closed) and the viewing role's `visibilityScopeForRole` is `'all'` — a short report summary (checklist lines answered/total via the new `checklistCompletionSummary`, who completed it and when, and any free-text notes), gated behind a "Report details are restricted for your role (CR-054 — still open with QCTO)" message otherwise. This answers G-26 ("does it go to the QP, is the report saved somewhere, does it feed into Approval of Results") directly: yes to the Post-EISA Monitoring QP share (Row 37), yes it's saved on the visit record, and yes it now feeds Approval of Results as more than a bare status string.
- **Row 39 (G-25) — readiness notice / visit request names** → still placeholders, per instruction. They already lived in `config/labels.ts` (`eisaReadinessNoticePlaceholder`, `markingModerationVisitRequestPlaceholder`, from PM-04/Phase 0) but nothing actually read them, so "one edit renames them everywhere" wasn't true yet. `modules/site-visits/api.ts` gained `bookedVisitNotificationTitle(visitType)`, used as the notification title sent to the ASD when an EISA Readiness or Marking & Moderation visit is booked (every other visit type keeps the generic "Visit booked — ready to conduct" title, since the register names no distinct trigger for them).
- **Rows 29/30 (G-06, G-07) — nothing to change** → confirmed per instruction. G-06 (an AD picking an SDP that can't access Site Visits) was already fixed in Fix 1B-ii/G-06 — `internal/NewVisitDialog.tsx` has had no SDP picker since then, only read-only text sourced from the linked sitting's registrations. G-07 (can officials still create an RPL/ARPL monitoring visit by hand) — confirmed yes: `VISIT_TYPE_OPTIONS` includes `rpl-arpl-monitoring` and `NewVisitDialog.tsx` lets any official pick it; the automatic registration-triggered path (CR-042/044) is additional, not exclusive. Both recorded below as open questions, not code changes.
- **Row 40 (H-05, H-11, H-12, G-24) — per-learner results** → `modules/approval-of-results/types.ts` replaced the single hand-typed `competentCount: number` with `learnerResults: LearnerResult[]` (`{ learnerId, fullName, outcome: 'competent' | 'not-yet-competent' }`), listing the learners from the sitting's EISA Confirmation registrations (`ConsolidatedLearnerRow`). `workflow.ts`'s `competencyRatePercent` now derives the rate from a new `competentCountOf(batch)` helper instead of reading a stored count directly — this is what makes a genuinely low rate (and the PM-06 Post-EISA Monitoring suggestion that depends on it) actually reachable instead of always "N found competent out of N added = 100%" (H-05's exact complaint). `external/SubmitResultsBatchDialog.tsx`'s single number input was replaced with one `Select` (Competent / Not yet competent) per learner, each row from the roster; Fix 2A's shared `ValidationSummary`/`ValidationIssue` shape lists every learner still missing a result and the Submit button stays disabled until all are answered — `api.ts`'s `submitResultsBatch` re-checks the same thing server-side (every registered learner's id must appear exactly once) rather than trusting the form alone. `internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx`'s Details tab gained a collapsible per-learner breakdown under the competency rate so the number is traceable, not just asserted; `external/ResultsBatchDetail.tsx`'s QP-facing competency line was updated the same way.
- **Row 40 — old data kept working** → `api.ts` gained `migrateLegacyBatches()` (same on-read, non-destructive migration pattern as Fix 1B-i's identity migration and Fix 2C's five-state SOR), called from `listBatches()`/`getBatch()`. Any stored batch missing `learnerResults` gets one synthesized from its sitting's current EISA Confirmation roster (the first `competentCount` learners, in roster order, read as Competent — so the competency rate is unchanged by the migration itself) or, failing that (the confirmation record no longer exists), from `learnerCount` placeholder rows. The migrated shape is written back via `saveAll`, so it only runs once per batch.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 4 (Part 2) assumptions made

- **The Deputy Director's "approved" stage only ever gets one new allowed action.** Giving the `approved` stage `allowedRoles: [REPORT_APPROVER_ROLE]` doesn't open any other action to the Deputy Director from that stage — `REPORT_WORKFLOW` only defines one action (`share-visit-outcome`) with `fromStage: 'approved'`, and `performReportAction` already throws if that action is attempted on a non-Post-EISA-Monitoring visit, so non-PEM visit types sitting at `approved` still have no clickable action anywhere in the UI.
- **Director/Chief Director's new `REPORT_VISIBILITY_RULES` entry is the stated default, not QCTO's answer.** The real per-unit subdivision behind CR-054 is still unconfirmed — this only makes an already-permissive fallback (anyone not listed defaults to seeing everything) into an explicit, documented rule for the two roles that newly need it.
- **The Approval of Results report summary only ever shows Marking & Moderation visits** (via the existing `listMarkingModerationVisitsForSitting`), not Post-EISA Monitoring ones — CR-052 is specifically about the Marking & Moderation link, and a PEM visit for a given sitting is typically created only after that sitting's batch is already approved (PM-06), by which point there's nothing left to "review" on the Compliance/Approval Chain tabs anyway.
- **"Report not yet approved" is shown whenever `reportIsApproved` is false, even for a role with full visibility rights** — the content genuinely doesn't exist yet at that point (the checklist is mid-review), so there's nothing restricted to hide; the message is informational, not a permissions message.

### Fix 4 Part 2 correction — Share Visit Outcome missing for Marking & Moderation and other visit types (2026-10-23)

**Bug.** On a Marking & Moderation visit, after the Deputy Director approved the report there was no "Share Visit Outcome" button — the QP (and SDP) never received anything, and nothing appeared on the QP's Site Visits list.

**Cause.** Not a regression of the engine-level fix from Fix 4 Part 2 (Row 37/G-23) — that fix (giving the `approved` stage `allowedRoles: [REPORT_APPROVER_ROLE]`) was correct and still in place. The real cause was a second, deliberate restriction layered on top of it, in two places: `modules/site-visits/internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s `ReportTab` only rendered the "Share Visit Outcome" button when `visit.visitType === 'post-eisa-monitoring'` (the button simply didn't exist for any other visit type), and `modules/site-visits/api.ts`'s `performReportAction` additionally threw `'Only a Post-EISA Monitoring visit has a "Share Visit Outcome" step.'` if the action was ever attempted on a non-PEM visit. Both were written deliberately in Fix 4 Part 2 under the reading that CR-058 ("rename/redesign 'Submit Approved Results' into an outcome-sharing step") only ever applied to the old PEM-only "Submit Approved Results" step. That reading was too narrow: CR-058 is about the mechanism (an explicit share step, QP acknowledges), and the register's "every other visit type's workflow ends at Approved" was describing the *old*, buggy build, not a rule that outcome-sharing should stay PEM-exclusive. `REPORT_WORKFLOW` itself (`workflow.ts`) was never visit-type-aware — `share-visit-outcome` is one generic action from `approved` to `outcome-shared` — so the restriction lived only in the UI gate and the API guard, not the workflow definition.

**The fix.**
- `modules/site-visits/internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`'s `ReportTab` — removed the `visit.visitType === 'post-eisa-monitoring'` condition from the button's render guard. It now shows for every visit type once `stage === 'approved' && canActInStage(REPORT_WORKFLOW, stage, role)` — still the one single action bar for this stage, still role-gated through `shared/workflow`.
- `modules/site-visits/api.ts`'s `performReportAction` — removed the non-PEM throw entirely.
- `modules/site-visits/api.ts`'s `share-visit-outcome` notification branch — now always notifies the `QP` role (naming `visit.qpName` in the body) for every visit type. The Post-EISA Monitoring extras (the `SDP` notification and the audit entry simulating the QA/Accreditation hand-off, PM-07) stay gated to `visit.visitType === 'post-eisa-monitoring'` — the register only names those extra recipients for PEM.
- `modules/site-visits/workflow.ts` — `overallStatusLabel`/`overallStatusTone`'s "mask the outcome from the QP before it's shared" logic (`QP_MASKED_PEM_STAGES`, renamed `QP_MASKED_PRE_SHARE_STAGES`) was scoped to `visit.visitType === 'post-eisa-monitoring'` only; since every visit type now has a share step, that condition was dropped so a QP never sees "Report with Deputy Director"/"Report approved" early on *any* visit type — they see the neutral "Visit completed — outcome pending" for all four visit types until shared.
- `modules/site-visits/external/VisitDetail.tsx` — added a `ReportSummary` block (new, reuses `checklistCompletionSummary` from `workflow.ts`) shown once `reportWorkflow.stageId` is `outcome-shared` or `closed`: checklist lines answered/total, who completed the visit and when (`completedBy`/`completedDate`), and any free-text notes. Previously this screen only said "The visit outcome has been shared with you," with no actual report content — the before/after contrast the Part 2 instruction asked for ("no report content before sharing" vs. "a read-only report summary after") wasn't fully built for any visit type, PEM included.

**The check that would have caught it.** Fix 4 Part 2's own click-through script was run once, for a Post-EISA Monitoring visit, and recorded as passing — which it genuinely did. Nothing in that pass exercised a second visit type through the same report-approval stage, so the PEM-only gate (deliberate, not accidental) never got questioned against the other three visit types until this bug report named one explicitly.

**Open question (CR-058 / G-26) — which visit types should share the report with the QP?** The default now built: **every** visit type (EISA Readiness, Marking & Moderation, RPL/ARPL Monitoring, Post-EISA Monitoring) gets the same single "Share Visit Outcome" step and the QP sees the same before/after behaviour; only the *extra* recipients (QA/Accreditation simulated hand-off, SDP notification) stay Post-EISA-Monitoring-specific, since PM-07 is the only place the register names those extra recipients. Confirm with QCTO whether EISA Readiness and RPL/ARPL Monitoring visits should really reach the QP this way too, or whether outcome-sharing should stay narrower than "every visit type" for some of them.

`npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms only `modules/site-visits/{workflow.ts,api.ts,external/VisitDetail.tsx,internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx}` changed, all inside `src/features/assessments-v2/`.

### Fix 5 — FISA and RPL Toolkit

Rows 43, 44, 45, 46, 48 of `08-test-findings.md` (I-08, I-10, J-03, J-04, J-05, K-08). Rows 42 and 47 skipped (blocked until the QCTO documents), per instruction.

- **Row 43 (I-08) — full FISA Validation Report** → new `modules/fisa/components/FisaValidationReport.tsx`: a read-only, printable view (every checklist section with its answers and comments via the shared `Checklist` component in `readonly` mode, the outcome history with reviewer/date, and the audit trail via `shared/audit/AuditTrailView`). `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx`'s old single "FISA Validation Report" tab (which actually held the ASD's working controls — allocation, the editable checklist, the outcome buttons) was renamed to "Validate"; a new tab, correctly labelled `LABELS.fisaValidationReport`, shows the full report once `instrumentStatus === 'validated'` (otherwise "Not yet available"). `external/ValidationDetail.tsx` (previously one scrolling, non-tabbed dialog) was rebuilt with a `Tabs` shape (Details / FISA Validation Report) so the SDP gets the identical read-only report once validation completes.
- **Row 44 (I-10) — FISA Registration learner source, add and remove** → the SDP could already add learners (reading the same simulated QA-domain enrolled-learner list `eisa-registration` reads, via `listFisaEnrollableLearners` → `mock/api/qaDomain.ts`'s `listEnrolledLearners`), but there was no way to remove one, and nothing on screen said where the list came from — which is exactly what the tester flagged. `fisa/api.ts` gained `removeFisaRegistrationLearner()` (SDP-only, `sdp-submission` stage only, mirrors `addFisaRegistrationLearner`'s guard), `fisa/hooks.ts` gained `useRemoveFisaRegistrationLearner()`. `external/RegistrationDetail.tsx`: a "Remove" button now sits on every learner row while the registration is still being built, and the "Add an Enrolled Learner" panel gained a help line naming the source ("...as recorded by the Quality Assurance domain's learner enrolment (simulated data for this build)"). "Learner source = QA enrolment" recorded as an open question below, per instruction.
- **Row 45 (J-04, K-08) — RPL Toolkit outcome letter** → the CEO-signs-the-outcome workflow (`RPL_OUTCOME_WORKFLOW`) already existed from Phase 7, but no screen ever rendered an actual letter — only the sign/decline action bar. `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s existing "Outcome Letter" tab and `external/ApplicationDetail.tsx` (rebuilt with a `Tabs` shape: Details / Outcome Letter, same pattern as the QSA Addendum) now both render a real letter via the shared `generateLetter('outcome', ...)`/`LetterView` (`shared/letters/`) — same template system, same `signatoryTitle: 'CEO'` — previewed before/while awaiting signature and showing the signing CEO's name once signed.
- **Row 46 (J-03, Ask QCTO — built the default) — team-level moderation before the IAC** → the Director & Team review stage already existed in the workflow (`dd-review → director-team-review → iac-review`) but had no checklist of its own — it just advanced. New `mock/data/rplToolkitModerationChecklistItems.ts` (freshly built, no precedent) backs a new `moderationChecklist` field on `RplToolkitApplication`, editable only by `Director & Team` at the `director-team-review` stage (CR-011's mandatory per-line comment applies), and gates `rpl-director-team-send-to-iac` in `api.ts` the same way the ASD's checklist+report gates `rpl-asd-send-to-dd`. The IAC now also records an `iacEndorsementNote` (a free-text endorsement, mirroring the QSA Addendum's mandatory "IAC Summary Presentation" gate) before it can approve, decline or send for remediation — "the IAC reviews and endorses the report" is read as this note, not a second checklist. `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx` gained two new stage-gated boxes (Director & Team Moderation, IAC Review & Endorsement) ahead of the one shared `ActionBar`, which is still the only action bar for any given stage. The order itself (moderation → IAC → CEO signature) was left as the existing default and flagged as an open question below, per instruction.
- **Row 48 (J-05) — update an existing toolkit as a new version** → `RplToolkitApplication` gained `version` (starts at 1) and `updatesToolkitId`. `api.ts`'s new `listApprovedRplToolkitsForQp(qpId)` feeds a new Select on `external/NewApplicationForm.tsx`, shown only when "Updated toolkit" is picked: the QP chooses one of their own approved toolkits, and `createRplToolkitApplication()` requires that pick, validates it belongs to that QP and is approved, and sets the new submission's `version` to one more than the toolkit it updates. Both dashboards (`internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`, `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`) gained a Version column; both detail views (`internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`, `external/ApplicationDetail.tsx`) show the version and, when linked, which toolkit and version it updates.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

#### Fix 5 assumptions made

- **The FISA Validation Report's "reviewer" is the allocated ASD, and "date" is the latest outcome's decided-at timestamp.** The register names both fields without pointing at a specific existing field — `allocatedAsdName`/`outcomeHistory[last].decidedAt` were the closest existing data, so no new field was added.
- **"Learner source = QA enrolment"** — FISA Registration reuses the exact same simulated QA-domain enrolled-learner list EISA Registration reads (`mock/data/enrolledLearners.ts`, filtered by SDP name), per the instruction's "like EISA registration." There is no Skills-Programme-specific enrolment list in this build; the help text says so plainly rather than implying a separate source exists. Recorded again below as an open question, per instruction.
- **Remove is scoped to the SDP's own build stage** (`sdp-submission` only, same as Add) — the register doesn't ask for removal once a registration has been submitted, and allowing it later would let an SDP edit a record an Assistant Director is already reviewing.
- **The IAC's "review and endorse" is a single free-text endorsement note, not a second checklist.** The register's own wording ("reviews and endorses the report") reads as an IAC sign-off on the Director & Team's already-moderated report, not a parallel re-evaluation — the ASD's checklist and the new moderation checklist already cover the line-by-line detail; a third checklist at IAC level would be the same questions asked three times with no new information.
- **The moderation checklist is built fresh** (`mock/data/rplToolkitModerationChecklistItems.ts`), same situation as the RPL Toolkit evaluation checklist itself (Phase 7) — no old-build or QCTO precedent exists for this step, since RPL Toolkit Evaluation didn't exist in the old build at all.
- **A toolkit must be `approved` (not just submitted) before it can be the target of an "Updated toolkit" submission**, and only the submitting QP's own approved toolkits are offered — the register's own wording ("once the QP wants to add another toolkit... select from existing toolkits to update from") implies the prior one already succeeded; nothing asks for updating a declined or still-in-review toolkit.
- **Version numbers are per-toolkit-lineage, not global.** A brand-new toolkit always starts at version 1; each "Updated toolkit" submission against it is the previous version's number plus one, tracked only by the linked chain of `updatesToolkitId` references, not a shared counter across all of a QP's toolkits.

### FISA Standards correction — Standard never reaches the ASD (2026-10-23)

**Bug.** Qualifications Development (Fundiswa Ngcobo) could create a FISA Standard, but it never appeared in the ASD's (Sipho Ndlovu's) FISA evaluation queue.

**Root cause.** Not a visibility-filter bug and not a missing action — the `standard-submit` action (`fisa/workflow.ts`'s `qd-submission → asd-evaluation` transition) always existed and was correctly role/stage-gated. The bug was that it was only ever drawn inside `modules/fisa/internal/StandardWorkspace.tsx`'s **Evaluation** tab (the single `ActionBar`, driven by `record.workflow.stageId`, that every later review stage also shares) — a tab whose visible content (the ASD's checklist and evaluation-report form, headed "Complete the report before sending to the Deputy Director") is built for the ASD/DD/IAC/QD review stages, not for Qualifications Development's own initial submission. There was no control at all on the **Details** tab, where Qualifications Development's own source documents and notes actually live, and nothing hinted that a second step, in a different tab, was still needed. This was compounded by `fisa/hooks.ts`'s `useCreateFisaStandard` success toast reading "FISA Standard submitted." immediately after `createFisaStandard()` — which only creates the record at `qd-submission`; it was not submitted to anyone yet. A Qualifications Development user had every reason to believe they were done. (This is `StandardWorkspace.tsx`'s half of the duplicate-action-bar fix from Fix 2A — removing the Details-tab bar there correctly killed a duplicate button for the ASD/DD/IAC stages, but it also removed the only bar a submitter would ever see for their own stage, since `qd-submission`'s action had never been duplicated onto the Details tab in the first place.) Compare with FISA Instrument Validation's `external/ValidationDetail.tsx`, which puts the SDP's "Upload instrument" and "Notify QCTO" button side by side in the one tab the SDP actually looks at — that is the pattern this fix brings FISA Standards' submission step in line with.

**The fix.**
- `modules/fisa/internal/StandardWorkspace.tsx` — added a box on the **Details** tab, shown only while `workflow.stageId === 'qd-submission'`: an inline "Sent back for clarification" note (read from the audit trail via `getAuditTrail`, shown to anyone) plus, for Qualifications Development only, a one-line instruction and the `ActionBar` for that stage. The Evaluation tab's `ActionBar` is now skipped for this one stage (`!atQdSubmission`) so the button never renders twice.
- `modules/fisa/hooks.ts` — the creation success toast now reads "FISA Standard created — open it and send it to the ASD when ready." instead of the misleading "submitted."
- `modules/fisa/workflow.ts` — `qd-signoff` had `requiresNote`-gated "Sign off" and "Send back for clarification" actions but no **Reject**, breaking CR-056's "send-back and reject available on every approval stage" rule (every other review stage in this workflow already had both). Added `standard-qd-reject` (`qd-signoff → declined`, mandatory note).
- `modules/fisa/api.ts`'s `performStandardAction` — only two of the workflow's nine transitions ever called `addNotification` (a send-back-to-`qd-submission` note, and the two terminal states). ASD, Deputy Director and IAC never got an in-app notification that a record was waiting for them — they would only find out by opening their dashboard and happening to look. Replaced the two hand-picked cases with one rule derived from the workflow definition itself: every transition notifies the role that can act in the stage it lands on (read from `FISA_STANDARD_WORKFLOW.stages[].allowedRoles[0]`), and a terminal stage (`approved`/`declined`) notifies Qualifications Development, since they started the chain. This can't go stale against `workflow.ts` the same way the old hard-coded pair could.

**Traced and confirmed already correct, no change needed:**
- The rest of the Standards chain (ASD evaluates with the mandatory-comment checklist, Deputy Director reviews, IAC reviews, Qualifications Development signs off) already has exactly one `ActionBar` per stage (Fix 2A), Send back and now Reject on every review stage, and an audit entry on every transition via `shared/workflow/engine.ts`.
- FISA Instrument Validation and FISA Registration (SDP submits, Assistant Director allocates to a named ASD, ASD validates, outcome straight back to the SDP, FISA Validation Report tab for both sides) — all already wired correctly from Fix 5, including allocation/outcome notifications. Once a Standard can actually reach `approved`, `external/NewValidationDialog.tsx`'s "Start Instrument Development" already lists it via `listEligibleFisaStandards()` and lets the SDP pick which approved Standard it's for.
- Visibility: `fisa` stays nav-hidden for QP (`shared/visibility/config.ts`) and is not in the CEO's or QP's `teams` (`config/roles.ts`); the SDP-side dashboards already filter by `person.sdpId`. None of this needed touching.

**Seed data.** `mock/data/fisaStandardsSeed.ts` (new) now seeds six FISA Standards the first time `fisa/api.ts`'s `loadStandards()` runs with nothing in storage: one at each of `qd-submission`, `asd-evaluation`, `dd-review`, `iac-review`, `qd-signoff`, and one already `approved` (so the SDP can start instrument development immediately in a demo without walking the whole chain first). `resetDemoData()` already clears every `assessmentsV2:`-prefixed key without change, so the next load falls back to this seed again. No other module in this build pre-seeds its own workflow records (every other module's `loadX()` falls back to `[]`) — this is a deliberate, FISA-Standards-only exception per the developer's explicit instruction, not a pattern applied elsewhere.

**Could not verify.** This environment has no browser-automation tool available this session, so the click-through below was verified by tracing the code (role/stage gates, the workflow definition, and the mock API) rather than by driving the actual UI. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean with zero errors inside `assessments-v2`; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`. The developer should click through the script below once to confirm the button actually renders and the toast/seed data read as intended in the browser.

### RPL Toolkit correction — Deputy Director had no button to continue the chain (2026-10-23)

**Bug.** The ASD (Sipho Ndlovu) completes the RPL Toolkit evaluation and clicks "Send to Deputy Director," but the Deputy Director (Grace Mokoena) has no button to review it and send it on — so the chain can't reach the Director & Team (Elsabe Steyn), the IAC (Thabiso Molefe), the CEO's outcome-letter signature, or the QP seeing the signed letter.

**Root cause — a visibility/list-filtering bug, not a missing action or a wrong gate.** `RPL_EVAL_WORKFLOW`'s `dd-review` stage (`modules/rpl-toolkit/workflow.ts:13`) already correctly lists `allowedRoles: ['Deputy Director']`, and `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s one `ActionBar` (line 226) is correctly gated by `canActInStage` against the record's actual current stage — once the Deputy Director has the record *open*, the three buttons (Send to Director & Team / Return to ASD / Reject) render exactly as they should. The problem is that the Deputy Director can never get the record open in the first place, because it never appears in her default "My items" list.

`internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`'s `isUnassignedActionable` (the predicate that decides whether a not-yet-personally-touched record belongs in "My items" for the active person — added by Fix 1B-ii's "Approved corrections") read:
```ts
function isUnassignedActionable(app, role, personId): boolean {
  if (!canActInStage(RPL_EVAL_WORKFLOW, app.evalState.stageId, role)) return false;
  if (app.allocatedAsdId && app.allocatedAsdId !== personId) return false;   // <- bug
  return true;
}
```
The second line was meant to keep a record allocated to one named ASD from also showing as "mine" for every other ASD sharing that role — but it applied unconditionally, for every role and every stage, not only while the record is actually sitting at the ASD's own stage. Once an Assistant Director allocates an application to an ASD, `allocatedAsdId` is set permanently (it is never cleared). From that point on, for every Deputy Director, Director & Team member and IAC member, `app.allocatedAsdId !== personId` is always `true` (none of them is the allocated ASD), so the function returns `false` for them forever — regardless of which stage the record has actually reached. The record silently drops out of the Deputy Director's (and every later role's) default "My items" view the moment an ASD is allocated, long before it even reaches `dd-review`. The dashboard's "With Deputy Director" count is computed from the same already-filtered list, so it reads `0` even when a real record is sitting there — there is nothing visibly wrong to click into. (Switching the `ScopeToggle` to "All in my team" does bypass the bug and would have shown the record — but nothing on the default view points a tester at that toggle, and the counts actively suggest there's nothing to find.)

This is the same class of mistake `site-visits/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`'s `isUnassignedActionable` (CR-055's `reroutedToId`) gets right: it only checks `reroutedToId` exclusivity *inside* the branch where `canActInStage(REPORT_WORKFLOW, ...)` is already true for the report-review stage — i.e., the named-person exclusivity is scoped to the one stage it actually pins down, not applied blindly to every later stage too.

**The fix.**
- `modules/rpl-toolkit/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` — scoped the `allocatedAsdId` exclusivity check to `app.evalState.stageId === 'asd-evaluation'` only. Outside that stage, an application is "mine" for any role/person `canActInStage` already says can act on it. The Deputy Director, Director & Team and IAC now see a newly-arrived record under "My items" the moment it reaches their stage, exactly like every other role in every other module.
- `modules/fisa/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` — found the identical pattern in `isValidationActionable` (FISA Instrument Validation's own `allocatedAsdId` check) and fixed it the same way, for consistency. **No observable behaviour change today** — `FISA_VALIDATION_WORKFLOW` has no stage after `asd-validation` for any other internal role to be blocked from (the outcome goes straight back to the SDP, per PM-01), so this half of the fix is defensive, not a reported bug.
- `modules/rpl-toolkit/internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx` — while tracing every later stage, found a second bug in the same area: `showModerationBox`/`showIacBox` were gated to the *exact current stage* (`stageId === 'director-team-review'` / `'iac-review'`) with no fallback, so the Director & Team's completed moderation checklist disappeared the instant the record moved on to `iac-review` (the IAC could never see the moderation it was meant to be endorsing), and the IAC's own endorsement note disappeared the instant the record was decided (`approved`/`declined`) — hiding it from the CEO/QP-facing review and from anyone revisiting the record afterwards. Fixed using the same "once reached, stays visible read-only" rule Fix 2A already established for the QSA Addendum's DD/IAC boxes: `showModerationBox = stageId === 'director-team-review' || moderationTouched` and `showIacBox = stageId === 'iac-review' || decided || iacTouched`, where `moderationTouched`/`iacTouched` check whether that box's own data has ever been filled in, independent of the record's current stage.
- `modules/rpl-toolkit/api.ts`'s `performRplEvaluationAction` — while fixing the visibility, also found that only the ASD-allocation and final-decision transitions ever called `addNotification`. The three stage-advance handoffs (ASD → DD, DD → Director & Team, Director & Team → IAC) and every send-back-to-ASD action (`rpl-dd-return-to-asd`, `rpl-director-team-return-to-asd`, `rpl-iac-remediation`) notified nobody — the next actor would only find out by happening to open their dashboard. Replaced with one rule derived from `RPL_EVAL_WORKFLOW` itself (the same generic pattern used by `fisa/api.ts`'s `performStandardAction`, and now also by the FISA Standards correction above): every transition that isn't already one of the two QP-facing cases notifies whichever role `RPL_EVAL_WORKFLOW.stages[].allowedRoles[0]` says can act on the stage it lands on.

**Traced and confirmed already correct, no change needed:**
- Every stage in `RPL_EVAL_WORKFLOW` already has a Send back (or Return to QP/ASD) and a Reject, both mandatory-note, satisfying CR-056 — this was never missing.
- The single `ActionBar` in `ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`'s Evaluation tab is correctly the only action bar for the whole eval chain (confirmed clean by Fix 2A's earlier module audit, re-confirmed here) — no duplicate/stage-leaked bars.
- `requiresDifferentActor` on `rpl-dd-send-to-director-team`, `rpl-director-team-send-to-iac` and `rpl-iac-approve` works correctly against `submittedById`, which the engine stamps with whoever last acted — CR-053 holds at every one of these stages.
- The CEO's outcome-letter signature and the external QP view (`external/ApplicationDetail.tsx`) were already correct: the CEO-only `ActionBar` only renders once `evalState` is decided, and the QP sees the signed letter the moment `outcomeState.stageId === 'signed'`.
- The "Updated toolkit" flow (row 48) was already correct: `external/NewApplicationForm.tsx`'s "Which toolkit is this updating?" picker is sourced from `listApprovedRplToolkitsForQp(qpId)` (only that QP's own `approved` records), and `api.ts`'s `createRplToolkitApplication` validates the picked id is really one of them before linking `updatesToolkitId` and bumping the version.

**Systematic check — every module, every `allowedRoles` stage, is there a real click path to see-and-act on it.**

| Module | Stage(s) checked | Role | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| rpl-toolkit | `ad-allocation`, `asd-evaluation`, `qp-corrections`, `dd-review`, `director-team-review`, `iac-review`, `ceo-signature` | Assistant Director, ASD, QP, Deputy Director, Director & Team, IAC, CEO | **Fixed** — `dd-review`/`director-team-review`/`iac-review` were unreachable via the default "My items" list once an ASD was allocated (root cause above); `ad-allocation`/`asd-evaluation`/`qp-corrections`/`ceo-signature` were already fine. |
| fisa (Instrument Validation) | `sdp-instrument`, `awaiting-allocation`, `asd-validation` | SDP, Assistant Director, ASD | **Fixed defensively** — same `allocatedAsdId` exclusivity leak found in `isValidationActionable`, but no stage after `asd-validation` for it to actually hide today; corrected for consistency, no behaviour change. |
| fisa (Standards) | `qd-submission`, `asd-evaluation`, `dd-review`, `iac-review`, `qd-signoff` | Qualifications Development, ASD, Deputy Director, IAC | Already fixed by the FISA Standards correction (above, same session day) — no new issue found here. |
| fisa (Registration) | `sdp-submission`, `ad-review` | SDP, Assistant Director | OK — plain `canActInStage`, no exclusivity field, no leak possible. |
| qsa-addendum (evaluation/ack/outcome/approval) | `asd-preview`/`asd-evaluation`/`asd-draft`, `dd-review` (×3, different sub-flows), `iac-review`, `ceo-signature`, `signed` | ASD, Deputy Director, IAC, CEO | OK — already audited clean by Fix 2A; `isUnassignedActionable` uses plain `canActInStage`, no personId exclusivity field exists on this record type. |
| instrument-validation | `awaiting-qp-proposal`/`-response` (×2), `qp-instrument`, `asd-validation` | QP, ASD | OK — plain `canActInStage`; already audited clean by Fix 2A. |
| eisa-calendar | `qcto-review`, `approved`, `published` (lifecycle) | QP, QCTO reviewer role | OK — `BatchReviewPanel`/`InternalCalendarView` gate directly off `canActInStage`, no list-filtering predicate with an exclusivity field at all. |
| eisa-registration | `sdp-submission`, `ad-review` | SDP, Assistant Director/registration-processor role | OK — plain `canActInStage`; already audited clean by Fix 2A. |
| eisa-confirmation | `awaiting-qp-ack`, `awaiting-qp-confirmation` | QP | OK — both QP-only, single external flow, no internal stage to hide. |
| site-visits | `ad-planning`, `dd-recommend`, `domain-director-approval`, `sda-booking`, `awaiting-execution`, `dd-report-review`, `approved`, `outcome-shared` | Assistant Director, Deputy Director, Domain Director, Sub Domain Admin, ASD, QP | OK — the one exclusivity field (`reroutedToId`) is already correctly scoped inside the `canActInStage(REPORT_WORKFLOW, ...)` branch only (this is the pattern the rpl-toolkit/fisa fix above was brought in line with); already audited for duplicate bars by Fix 2A and for planning-chain visibility by Fix 4 Part 1. |
| approval-of-results | `ad-review`, `dd-review`, `director-review`, `chief-director-review` | Assistant Director, Deputy Director, Director, Chief Director | OK — one shared compliance checklist visible unconditionally to every stage (not per-role boxes that could hide), one `ActionBar` driven by `canActInStage` against the current stage only; no exclusivity field exists on `ResultsBatch`. Already audited clean by Fix 2A. |

No other module needed a code change. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean with zero errors; `git status --short` confirms every change stayed inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/`.

Found during Phase 0 discovery — files outside `src/pages/assessment` that import from it and will need re-pointing or removal when v2 replaces the old domain:

## Assumptions made

- The v2 role switcher is fully self-contained (`shared/ui/RoleContext.tsx`), independent of the app-wide `AppContext.currentRole`. The old build's role selector mutates one global role shared by every domain; v2's hard rule 3 forbids importing `AppContext` anyway, so this was a forced — and, since it stops a v2 role switch from leaking into other open domains, desirable — design choice. Confirmed with the developer before Phase 0B build began.
- React Query cache is never the source of truth for workflow/domain state — `mock/api` (memory + localStorage) always is. The query client is scoped to `index.tsx` only, instantiated once at module scope, and never touches `App.tsx`'s client (which root `CLAUDE.md` already flags as recreated-every-render — a pre-existing issue outside this module's remit to fix).
- `/assessments-v2` is reachable directly by URL with no department-login gate (matching `06-swap-plan.md`), so a persistent "Demo — mock data only" banner is shown in the v2 header at all times.
- **SAQA registry check lives in `mock/api/saqaRegistry.ts`, not `mock/integrations/`.** The module `CLAUDE.md`'s "Data" section explicitly lists "SAQA registry" as cross-domain data simulated in `mock/api`, while `mock/integrations/` is reserved for the backend-only CRs with no screen (CR-001, 002, 009, 010, 020, 022, 037, 039). Since the gate check has a screen (the New Application form), it follows the `mock/api` convention. There is no real SAQA API, so the QP simulates the outcome with "Registered"/"Not Registered" buttons, same UX as the old build.
- **"A different actor" means a different role, not a different individual.** This build has no per-user login — only a role switcher (`RoleContext`) — so `requiresDifferentActor` (CR-053) compares the role label stored as `submittedBy` against the current role's label. Within a single role this is indistinguishable from a real per-user check because the workflow already prevents the same role from acting twice in a row (the stage moves on); it only becomes a real gap if QCTO later wants to stop e.g. two different ASDs approving each other's work, which needs real individual logins.
- **The external dashboard shows every application, not just one QP's.** "My QSA Addendum applications" has no QP identity to filter by — the QP name on an application is a free-text form field, not a login. Revisit if/when the app gains real QP accounts.
- **IAC decline vs. remediation are different terminal directions.** A decline moves straight to the outcome-letter flow (the CEO signs a letter saying "Declined" — confirming the process was followed, per CR-008's intent). "Send for remediation" instead loops back to the ASD so the application can be reworked and re-evaluated. Process-flow.md's wording ("a decline sends it back for remediation") reads as if these are the same thing, but CR-016 lists them as separate actions ("decline or send for remediation") — built as two distinct buttons so both are directly testable.
- **Approval letter signer defaults to the Deputy Director.** Open question (CR-007/CR-008 follow-up) answered with the simplest reading — one constant, `modules/qsa-addendum/config.ts` → `APPROVAL_LETTER_SIGNER_ROLE`, to change if QCTO names someone else. Confirmed again, with the full draft/sign/decline/send flow built around it, in Fix 2B (row 13) — see "Fix 2B — QSA Addendum flow changes" above.
- **Approved EISA sittings per year is captured on the QSA Addendum evaluation.** Open question (CR-030) answered with the simplest reading — a field on the ASD's evaluation report (`evaluationReport.approvedEisaSittingsPerYear`), not a separate screen. Move it if QCTO confirms a different home for this value.
- **IAC remediation goes to the ASD, who can then return it to the QP.** "Remediation goes back down the chain" (CR-016) is built as IAC → `asd-evaluation` directly — the same stage the ASD already works from, which still has its own "Return to QP for corrections" action if the ASD decides the QP needs to make changes. There is no separate remediation-only stage.

- **No specific role is named for the EISA Calendar, so the ASD reviews/publishes/postpones/cancels.** The register and process-flow.md both say only "QCTO officials" (CR-027/CR-032). Implemented as `modules/eisa-calendar/config.ts` → `CALENDAR_REVIEWER_ROLE` (`'ASD'`) and added to the ASD's teams in `config/roles.ts` (`eisa-calendar`, alongside the ASD's existing `qsa-evaluation`/`instrument-validation` teams) — one constant to change if QCTO names a different role.
- **The minimum lead time is expressed in working days (21), using the same shared helper Phase 6 needs for its 21-working-day results SLA.** Neither the figure nor the unit (calendar vs working days) is in the register (CR-032 open question). `config/holidays.ts` → `MIN_LEAD_TIME_WORKING_DAYS`.
- **The SA public holiday list is a best-effort 2026–2027 list, not from a QCTO-confirmed source.** `config/holidays.ts` → `SA_PUBLIC_HOLIDAYS`. Confirm the real source (CR-032 open question) before relying on it past the demo.
- **"Approve" and "publish" are two separate QCTO actions, not one combined step.** CR-028's prose reads sequentially ("QCTO validates and approves them, then they are published"), and the developer's own requested click-through script lists them as three separate steps (approve / reject with reason / publish) — built that way in `BATCH_WORKFLOW` (`qcto-review → approved → published`).
- **The automatic date checks (CR-032) hard-block a QP's proposal, but only warn (non-blocking) when QCTO adds or edits a sitting directly.** CR-027 gives QCTO unrestricted add/edit/delete ("at any time"), while CR-032's "reject dates automatically" reads as being about the QP-facing submission path. QCTO can override; `internal/AddEditSittingDialog.tsx` shows the same `checkSittingDate` result as a dismissible warning instead of a block.
- **Postponing a sitting does not auto-create a replacement date.** The register doesn't ask for this, and "stages are not auto-triggered" is a running theme in this build (CR-024, CR-044) — QCTO adds a new date separately (CR-027) if the sitting needs to be rescheduled.
- **The external "EISA Calendar" screens show every QP's/SDP's submissions and sittings, not filtered to "my own".** Same reason as the QSA Addendum module's existing assumption: there is no QP/SDP login, only the role switcher, so there is no identity to filter by.
- **Cancellation/postponement notifications go to both the SDP (explicitly required by CR-034) and the QP** (their qualification's sitting changed) — `api.ts` `performSittingLifecycleAction` calls `addNotification` twice, once per recipient role.
- **SDPs see only `published`, `postponed` and `cancelled` sittings** (`external/SdpCalendarView.tsx`) — the earlier internal-only stages (`proposed`, `approved`, `rejected`) are a QP ↔ QCTO matter, mirroring CR-018's "SDPs never see EISA Instrument Validation".
- **The "Quality Assurance module's scheduling methodology" CR-017 says to reuse could not be located as a distinct propose/accept/counter-propose feature anywhere under `src/pages/QA/`.** That folder's site-visit code (`subtabs/SiteVisitManagement.tsx`) is a one-sided evaluation tool with no joint negotiation. The closest shape that does exist is `src/pages/accreditation/SiteVisitManagement.tsx`'s one-sided schedule → send → applicant-accept/reschedule-request chain (a different domain, read-only reference per the module's hard rule 3). `modules/instrument-validation/workflow.ts`'s `SCHEDULING_WORKFLOW` is a fresh two-party propose → accept/counter-propose state machine built in the same shape (a `WorkflowDefinition` through the shared engine) rather than a literal port of either. Flag to the developer: confirm whether a different QA scheduling feature exists that this should have matched instead.
- **One instrument validation per QSA Addendum.** The register doesn't say whether a qualification can have more than one validation record (e.g. for a second instrument). `api.ts` `startValidation()` blocks starting a second one for the same addendum; `listEligibleQsaAddenda()` drops any addendum that already has one. Revisit if QCTO confirms multiple instruments per qualification are normal.
- **"QSA agenda" (CR-025) is read as the QSA Addendum queue** — the same applications already listed on the QSA Addendum module's own internal dashboard — shown alongside instrument validation status in one combined view (`internal/StatusDashboard.tsx`), not as a separate, differently-shaped screen. Filters: free-text search (qualification/QP/stage), type, and a submitted/created date range.
- **The QSA Addendum doesn't capture a QP postal address or a named contact person**, so two of CR-021's pre-filled fields (Address of QP, Contact Person/Number) always start as a placeholder needing Recapture — there is nowhere upstream to pull a real value from yet. `modules/instrument-validation/config.ts` → `PREFILL_NOT_CAPTURED_PLACEHOLDER`. Raise with QCTO whether these belong on the QSA Addendum submission form instead.
- **"Not approved" is treated as "changes required"; "Approved" and "Approved with conditions" are both treated as not requiring changes.** The old build had a separate "Changes Required" checkbox alongside the 3-option outcome decision; CR-023 only says "if changes are required the QP fixes and resubmits" without naming a separate flag, so the simpler reading — Not Approved always loops back, the other two always mark the instrument Validated — was built instead of adding an extra field the register never asked for.
- **A resubmission cycle gets a brand-new checklist and pre-filled fields, not the previous cycle's answers carried forward.** CR-011's mandatory-comment checklist is meant to be a genuine re-check of the resubmitted instrument, not a resave of stale answers; `outcomeHistory` keeps every past cycle's decision and notes so nothing is lost.

Add a real `confirmation` team once QCTO confirms this is right, or names a different owner.

- ~~The "Approved corrections" section `progress.md`'s Fix 1B-i entry refers to is missing from `09-visibility-matrix.md`.~~ **Resolved 2026-10-22** — see "Fix 1B-ii correction — Approved corrections & 'My items'" above.
- **Row 22/23 (D-11, D-12) — postpone/cancel negotiation.** Should postponing or cancelling a sitting let the ASD propose a new date that the QP accepts or counters, like Instrument Validation's joint scheduling (CR-017)? Built as the suggested default instead: postpone carries an optional proposed new date shown in the notification and audit trail, with no negotiation; cancel just requires a reason (already built). `modules/eisa-calendar/workflow.ts`'s `SITTING_LIFECYCLE_WORKFLOW` has no negotiation stages.
- **Row 23 (D-17) — Edit/Delete for the ASD.** Confirmed: Edit changes a sitting's date/details (unchanged by this round); Delete removes it outright. Still open: Delete is now blocked (not just warned) when the sitting already has any registration against it (draft or completed) — confirm this is the right rule, versus e.g. a warn-and-override like the date-validation checks already allow QCTO on direct add/edit.
- **Row 20 (C-16) — required documents list.** The old build had six required documents for EISA Instrument Validation; the QP currently only sees the one instrument they uploaded. Confirm whether QCTO still wants a required-documents list, and if so, what the six documents are. No change made this round.
- **Row 26 (E-12) — SOR per learner, confirmed.** The register already reads unambiguously as one SOR per learner (not one per registration/qualification) — `modules/eisa-registration/types.ts`'s `RegistrationLearner.sor` was already built this way since Phase 4 and stays as-is. Listed here only to close the loop on the tester's question, not because anything was ambiguous or changed.
- **Row 35 — allowed re-route targets.** Today `modules/site-visits/api.ts`'s `performReportAction` only enforces "the target must hold the same role" (any named Deputy Director in `mock/data/users.ts`, with no narrower rule). Confirm with QCTO whether re-routing should be restricted further — e.g. only to a Deputy Director within the same unit/team, or only to someone not already overloaded — or whether "any named colleague with the same role" is the intended rule.
- **Row 35 — re-route notifications are role-level, not person-level.** Every Deputy Director is notified when a report is re-routed to one of them (same limitation as every other notification in this domain — `shared/notifications/store.ts` has no per-person recipient). Revisit if/when notifications are person-ified domain-wide.
- **Row 33 — `ad-planning` has no Reject/Send-back pair.** Built as the originating stage with nothing upstream to reject or send back (see the Fix 4 Part 1 assumption above) — confirm this reading is correct, versus QCTO wanting the ASD (formerly the Assistant Director, 3.11) to be able to withdraw/abandon an in-progress plan some other way.
- **Row 37/38 (CR-058/G-26) — which visit types should share the report with the QP, still open.** The Fix 4 Part 2 correction (2026-10-23) made "Share Visit Outcome" work for every visit type (EISA Readiness, Marking & Moderation, RPL/ARPL Monitoring, Post-EISA Monitoring), not only Post-EISA Monitoring — the default built. The extra PM-07 recipients (SDP notification, simulated QA/Accreditation hand-off) stayed Post-EISA-Monitoring-only. Confirm with QCTO whether every visit type really should reach the QP this way, or whether outcome-sharing should stay narrower for some of them.
- **Rows 36/38 — report visibility rules between units (CR-054), still open.** `REPORT_VISIBILITY_RULES` now has an explicit entry for every role that reads it (including Director/Chief Director, added in Fix 4 Part 2 for Approval of Results' new report summary), but every internal entry is still `scope: 'all'` — "whole process-stage team plus whoever else needs the data" is the built default, not a QCTO-confirmed per-unit rule. Confirm whether, e.g., a Deputy Director in one region should see a Marking & Moderation report booked by an ASD in another.
- **Row 39 — names for the readiness notice and the marking/moderation visit request, still placeholders.** Now actually used as the booked-visit notification title for those two visit types (`site-visits/api.ts`'s `bookedVisitNotificationTitle`) instead of sitting unread in `config/labels.ts` — still the same two placeholder strings pending QCTO's real names.
- **Row 29 — should an SDP see visits that involve them** (e.g. a notified Marking & Moderation visit)? Today SDP visibility for this hub is `None` end to end (confirmed correct in Fix 1B-ii) — raised again here per the tester's question, no change made.
- **Row 30 — confirmed: officials may still plan an RPL/ARPL monitoring visit by hand** from the hub, in addition to the automatic registration-triggered one. No change needed; recorded here to close the tester's question.
- **Row 40 — the "due soon"/PM-06 threshold number is unconfirmed, but the mechanism is now real.** Per-learner results (Fix 4 Part 2) make the competency rate and the Post-EISA Monitoring suggestion depend on genuine per-learner data instead of a hand-typed count that could never produce a low rate — the 60% threshold itself (`POST_EISA_MONITORING_COMPETENCY_THRESHOLD_PERCENT`) is still the same unconfirmed placeholder from Phase 5/6.
- **Row 44 — learner source = QA enrolment, confirm with QCTO.** FISA Registration's "already enrolled in Skills Programme" learner list is the same simulated `mock/data/enrolledLearners.ts` EISA Registration reads — there is no Skills-Programme-specific enrolment list in this build. Confirm whether Skills Programme enrolment should be a genuinely separate QA-domain list once that backend exists.
- **Row 46 — confirm the order: Director & Team moderation → IAC endorsement → CEO signature.** Built as the stated default (the order the workflow already had from Phase 7) with a moderation checklist and an IAC endorsement note added on top. Confirm this is the correct order, and whether the IAC's "endorsement" should instead be its own checklist rather than one free-text note.
- **QCTO feedback 3.10 — does QCTO have its own FISA Standards checklist?** (added 5 Nov 2026) The FISA Instrument Validation checklist is now QCTO's own "FISA Validation of Instrument Report". The FISA Standards evaluation checklist (`mock/data/fisaStandardChecklistItems.ts`) is unchanged and still the freshly built Phase 7 version; we have asked QCTO whether it has an official one.
- **QCTO feedback 3.10 — item 3.4 of the FISA Validation of Instrument Report is incomplete.** (added 5 Nov 2026) QCTO's text reads "Questions have not been repeated verbatim from previous questions OR" and stops there. Shown without the "OR". Ask QCTO for the rest of the sentence.
- **QCTO feedback 2.5 — who may plan and run visits outside Assessment?** (added 4 Nov 2026, Batch G) A visit now has a Domain (Assessment, Accreditation and Quality Assurance, Qualifications Development, Certification), but QCTO has not decided which roles outside Assessment may plan or run visits. Until then the Domain is a label and list filter only: every domain uses the Assessment planning and report chain, and no role's access changed.
- **QCTO feedback 3.14 — per-component outcomes for FISA results?** (added 5 Nov 2026) The SDP gives each learner one outcome (Competent or Not yet competent). Does QCTO want an outcome per assessment component instead?
- **QCTO feedback 3.14 — FISA results deadline and evidence tags.** (added 5 Nov 2026) FISA results have no submission deadline (the EISA 21-working-day rule is tied to a sitting), and the evidence tag list is a first version. Does QCTO want a deadline or its own tag list?
- **Batch L1 (29.31) — the EISA results calculation is an assumption.** (added 6 Nov 2026) Waiting for QCTO's calculator spreadsheet. Built: a component is competent when the mark is at least its pass mark; a learner is Competent only when competent in every component; weighted score = sum of (mark ÷ total marks × weighting) as a %; competency rate = competent ÷ wrote (absent learners left out); absenteeism = absent ÷ registered. The whole rule is one file, `modules/approval-of-results/calculation.ts`. Ask QCTO: is the final result decided per component or on the weighted score (or both)? Is there a minimum weighted score? Are absent learners counted in the rate anywhere?
- **Batch L1 (29.32) — EISA date per component is an assumption.** (added 6 Nov 2026) Waiting for QCTO's QASA control sheet. Until the EISA Calendar holds a date per component (a later batch), component 1 is on the sitting date and each next component on the next working day (`modules/approval-of-results/componentDates.ts`, shared working-day helper). Ask QCTO where the per-component dates come from and whether a component can take more than one day.
- **Batch L1.1 — is "Lowest Mark" a sub-minimum?** (added 9 Nov 2026) QCTO's control sheet has a "Lowest Mark" per component and said on 29 Oct and 5 Nov that every component has its own competence mark. Built: the Lowest Mark is the component's competence mark, so a learner below it is Not yet competent in that component even when the overall EISA Percentage Obtained passes the Addendum pass %. If QCTO means something else (for example only the lowest mark that can be awarded), change `calculation.ts` only. This replaces the L1 assumption above.
- **Batch L1.1 — whole-number or 2-decimal rounding?** (added 9 Nov 2026) QCTO's calculator writes the steps as "round to a whole number (1.4 → 1, 1.5 → 2)" but its formula rounds to 2 decimals. Built: both are shown. EISA Percentage Obtained (decimal) is rounded to 2 decimals; EISA Percentage Obtained (final) is that rounded half-up to a whole number, and the final one is compared with the Addendum pass %. Ask QCTO which one decides the result (this matters only for values such as 44.5 against a pass % of 45).
- **Batch L1.1 — what does "FLC" stand for?** (added 9 Nov 2026) The control sheet has an FLC column (Y/N). Assumed to be Foundational Learning Competence. Captured per QASA as "FLC required" (Yes/No) and written to the control sheet as Y or N; nothing else uses it yet. Ask QCTO what it means and whether Certification acts on it.
- **Batch L1.1 — File 3 layout: one row per learner, or one row per learner per EISA date?** (added 9 Nov 2026) On 29 Oct (2:56:51) QCTO said a learner with two components appears twice on File 3, once per EISA date, while our template (`approval-of-results/file3.ts`) has one row per learner with a column per component. Not changed. Ask QCTO for the real File 3 layout; if it is one row per learner per date, the template, the parser and the preview change together.
- **Batch L1.1 — control sheet "AQP" and NQF Level.** (added 9 Nov 2026) QCTO's example has an acronym ("SAPC") in AQP; we write the QP's name. The NQF Level is written as the number only ("Level 5" → 5), as in their example. Ask QCTO which values they expect.
- **Batch L2 (05.24) — the File 3 format is our own simple version.** (added 9 Nov 2026) Columns: Learner ID number, Learner name, one column per component, Absent (Y/N), CSV only. Ask QCTO for the real File 3 layout (columns, order, whether it is an Excel file, how absent is shown, whether learners are matched on ID number or learner number) and whether a file with errors should be partly accepted. The whole format is `modules/approval-of-results/file3.ts`.
- **Batch L2 (05.23) — should an invalid stored ID number block submission?** (added 9 Nov 2026) Built as a warning on the capture screen, because the QP cannot fix a learner's ID number. Ask QCTO whether results for such a learner must be held back until the learner record is corrected.
