## Styling 1 — Side navigation restyle

Styling only, as instructed: no change to routing, visibility rules, workflow logic, or which nav items show for which role. Everything below is in `shared/ui/`.

- **Shared look, both portals** — `shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx` is the single layout both `internal/*` and `external/*` routes already rendered through, so restyling it once gives both portals the same look automatically; nothing portal-specific was duplicated.
- **Side-nav header area** — the `<aside>` now opens with its own header block (an "IN"/"EX" badge + "Internal Portal"/"External Portal" label), separate from the top app header, same pattern already used elsewhere in the app (e.g. `components/ui/AccreditationLayout.tsx`'s sidebar header).
- **Icons per nav item** — a local `NAV_ICONS` map in `AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx` (cosmetic only, keyed by the same `item.path` strings `config/nav.ts` already used) assigns one `lucide-react` icon per module. Every icon chosen is already imported somewhere else in the app (old `AssessmentLayout.tsx`/`ExternalAssessmentSidemenu.tsx`/`InternalSideMenuBar.tsx`/`QAInternalSideBar.tsx`) — no new icon names introduced. `config/nav.ts` itself was not touched.
- **Hover, active, focus states** — active item gets `bg-blue-50 text-blue-700` plus `aria-current="page"`; inactive items get a `hover:bg-gray-100` hover state; every nav link and the Logout button share a `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-ring` keyboard-focus ring (the same `ring-ring` theme token `components/ui/button.tsx` already uses).
- **Role/person switcher and Reset demo data** — left in the top header exactly where they were; only spacing/wrapping was tidied (`flex-wrap gap-2` on both the header row and the switcher's two selects) so they don't overflow at narrow widths. `shared/ui/RoleSwitcher.tsx`'s two `SelectTrigger`s shrink from `w-[190px]` to `w-[150px]` below the `sm` breakpoint.
- **Logout button** — new, bottom of both side navs (`shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`). Calls a new `clearStoredPerson(portal)` helper (`shared/ui/useV2Role.ts`) for both portals, then navigates to `/assessments-v2` (the `PortalPicker`). `clearStoredPerson` only removes the one `assessmentsV2:activePersonId:v2:{portal}` localStorage key that tracks "who is the active person" — it never calls `resetDemoData()`, so no record/demo data is touched. Clearing the key for both portals means whichever one is picked next falls back to that portal's default person (`RoleContext.tsx`'s existing `resolveInitialPersonId` → `defaultPersonForPortal` fallback, unchanged).
- **Narrow width, no horizontal scroll** — the side nav is a fixed icon rail (`w-16`) below the `md` breakpoint and expands to the existing `w-64` at `md` and up (labels/the "Phase N" tag are `hidden md:inline`, each link keeps a native `title` tooltip so the icon-only rail is still identifiable); the top header wraps (`flex-wrap`) instead of overflowing; the main content area got `min-w-0` so it can't force the page wider than the viewport. No JS toggle needed — it's the same Tailwind breakpoint pattern (`md:`) already used throughout the app's other layouts.
- Also tidied: `shared/ui/PortalPicker.tsx`'s two portal cards go `grid-cols-1` below `sm` instead of always `grid-cols-2`, so they don't get cramped on a narrow screen before a portal (and its nav) is even chosen.

`npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms only `shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`, `shared/ui/PortalPicker.tsx`, `shared/ui/RoleSwitcher.tsx` and `shared/ui/useV2Role.ts` changed, all inside `src/features/assessments-v2/`.

## Styling 2 — Shared modal, page layout, letter layout

Styling only, as instructed: no change to routing, visibility rules, workflow logic, or which actions/tabs show for which role. Two new shared pieces, one reworked shared piece, rolled out to QSA Addendum and EISA Instrument Validation only (per instruction) — every other module still uses its own hand-written `Dialog`/header/card markup and is listed under "Still on the old style" below.

### The shared pieces

- **`shared/ui/Modal.tsx`** — one dialog shell for every "New X" and "View X" dialog, built on the existing shadcn `Dialog` (no new library). Props: `size` (`'sm' | 'md' | 'lg'` → `sm:max-w-md` / `sm:max-w-2xl` / `sm:max-w-4xl`), `title`, optional `description`, `children` (the body — scrolls on its own once taller than the viewport, via `flex-1 overflow-y-auto`), optional `footer` (rendered in a `DialogFooter`, so the primary action lands on the right next to Cancel, same as every dialog already did), and optional `onSubmit` (wraps body + footer in a `<form>` for the one dialog that's a form — `NewApplicationForm`). Width is `w-[calc(100vw-2rem)]` capped by the size's `sm:max-w-*`, so on a phone the dialog keeps a half-rem gutter on each side instead of touching the screen edges, and widens to the fixed size from the `sm` breakpoint up. **How to use it:** replace `Dialog`/`DialogContent`/`DialogHeader`/`DialogTitle`/`DialogDescription`/`DialogFooter` with a single `<Modal open=.. onOpenChange=.. size=.. title=.. description=.. footer={...}>{body}</Modal>`; for a form dialog also pass `onSubmit={handleSubmit(onSubmit)}` and leave the `<form>` tag out of your own JSX. `ReasonDialog` (the existing mandatory-reason dialog used across the domain) was left as-is — it's already a small, single-purpose dialog and converting it would have meant threading a form/non-form distinction through a component that doesn't need one.
- **`shared/ui/PageHeader.tsx`** — one component: `title`, optional `description` (the one-line help text), optional `actions` (buttons/toggles, right-aligned, wraps under the title at narrow widths via `flex-wrap`). Replaces the hand-written `<div className="flex items-center justify-between">...` header block every dashboard had.
- **`shared/ui/PageSections.tsx`** — three small pieces for consistent spacing, pulled out of the identical markup every dashboard already repeated by hand: `StatTileGrid`/`StatTile` (the stat-card row — `StatTileGrid` defaults to `md:grid-cols-4`, pass `className="md:grid-cols-3"` etc. to override for a different card count), `SectionCard` (the rounded card with an optional header — `title` prop — wrapping a table or other content), `EmptyState` (the centered "nothing here yet" message, `title` + optional `description`).

### Letters — one shared layout

`shared/letters/LetterView.tsx` was rebuilt around four visual blocks instead of one plain paragraph: a **heading** (kind title, e.g. "Outcome Letter"), a **reference block** (To / Qualification / SAQA ID / NQF Level / Date, read from `letter.data`), the **body** (the free-text paragraph), and a **signature area** (signatory name + title, or "Signature pending"). No wording changed — the date and signed-by lines just moved out of the body text into their own structured blocks, so `shared/letters/templates.ts`'s templates no longer end with a `Date:`/`Signed:` line (LetterView renders those from `data` directly), and `GeneratedLetter` (`shared/letters/types.ts`) gained a `title` field that `generateLetter()` fills from a `LETTER_TITLES` map. This is shared by every letter in the domain — QSA Addendum's acknowledgement/outcome/approval letters and RPL Toolkit's outcome letter all picked up the new layout automatically; RPL Toolkit's own screens were not otherwise touched.

### Rolled out to (per instruction, not further yet)

- **QSA Addendum** — `external/NewApplicationForm.tsx` (`Modal`, `size="md"`, form mode), `external/ApplicationDetail.tsx` (`Modal`, `size="md"`, tabs body, Close footer), `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx` (`Modal`, `size="lg"`, tabs body, no footer — unchanged, it never had one), `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx` and `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` (`PageHeader` + `StatTileGrid`/`StatTile` + `SectionCard` + `EmptyState`).
- **EISA Instrument Validation** — `external/NewValidationDialog.tsx` (`Modal`, `size="sm"`), `external/ValidationDetail.tsx` (`Modal`, `size="md"`, tabs body), `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx` (`Modal`, `size="lg"`, tabs body), `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`, `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` and `internal/StatusDashboard.tsx` (same `PageHeader`/`PageSections` swap; this module has no letters).

### Still on the old style (next run should convert these)

Every other module still builds its dialogs and dashboard headers by hand: `modules/eisa-calendar/`, `modules/eisa-registration/`, `modules/eisa-confirmation/`, `modules/site-visits/`, `modules/approval-of-results/`, `modules/fisa/`, `modules/rpl-toolkit/` (its dialogs/headers — its letter already picked up the new `LetterView` automatically, see above). `shared/ui/ReasonDialog.tsx` was deliberately left alone (see above). The side-nav/layout chrome from Styling 1 is untouched by this pass.

`npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, extending `../../../tsconfig.app.json` with its own `include` so it doesn't inherit that file's repo-wide `include: ["src"]`, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms only files inside `src/features/assessments-v2/` changed (the three new `shared/ui/` files, the three `shared/letters/` files, and the QSA Addendum / EISA Instrument Validation files listed above).

Internal: `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` (`PageHeader` with the Scope toggle as `actions`, `StatTileGrid`/`StatTile`, `SectionCard` for the batches table and Recent Activity, `EmptyState`), `internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx` (`Modal`, `size="lg"`). The Marking & Moderation status panel and the per-learner results `<details>` view on the Details tab were already using the same card language as the rest of the domain and needed no structural change.

The two `Link`s' `to` targets (`internal/dashboard`, `external/dashboard`) are untouched.

## Visual polish — shared shell and shared UI

A small demo-readiness pass, deliberately scoped tighter than Styling 1–3: only Tailwind classNames in `shared/ui/` changed — no JSX structure, props, handlers, conditions, routes or text copy touched anywhere, and nothing in `modules/` was opened. Eight files changed, all in `shared/ui/`.

- **Per-portal accent colour** (`AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`, `Dashboard.tsx`, `PortalPicker.tsx`) — a small `PORTAL_ACCENT` lookup (blue for Internal, teal for External — teal was picked because no status-badge tone in the domain already uses it, so it never reads as a status colour) now drives: the side-nav's "IN"/"EX" header badge, a coloured rule under the nav header and under the top app header, the active nav item's background/text/left-accent-bar, a left-accent stripe on the Dashboard's "Acting as" card, and the hover-border colour + icon-badge colour on each of the portal picker's two cards. Previously every one of these was hardcoded to the same blue regardless of portal.
- **Side-nav polish** (`AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`) — active nav item gained a 2px left accent bar (`border-l-2`, transparent when inactive so layout doesn't shift); nav items and the header block got a little more vertical breathing room (`py-2`→`py-2.5`, `space-y-1`→`space-y-1.5`, header `py-3`→`py-4`). The existing divider above Logout, the icon set, and the `md:` icon-rail/full-width breakpoint from Styling 1 are untouched.
- **Dashboard tiles** (`shared/ui/Dashboard.tsx` — the module-list landing page at `/assessments-v2/{portal}/dashboard`, not each module's own dashboard) — the module-list rows get a hover tint, the "Available"/"Phase N" text became a small rounded pill (emerald for Available, grey for a phase tag), and the "Acting as" card's labels gained slightly more weight. No icon-in-a-circle was added to either this or `StatTile` in `PageSections.tsx` — that would have meant a new child element, which the "no JSX structure changes" hard rule for this pass rules out; noted as a skipped idea rather than silently dropped.
- **`shared/ui/PageSections.tsx`** — `StatTile` (the four-card stat row every module dashboard already uses via Styling 2/3) got a clearer value/label split (`text-2xl`→`text-3xl` value, uppercase tracked label) and a hover lift (`hover:-translate-y-0.5 hover:shadow-md`); `SectionCard`'s header and `EmptyState`'s title got minor weight/tracking tweaks for consistency with the rest of this pass.
- **`shared/ui/Modal.tsx`** — the dialog shell used by every "New X"/"View X" dialog in the domain now matches the app's `rounded-2xl` card radius (was the shadcn default `rounded-lg`), a slightly stronger shadow, and a faint tint on the header/footer bands so they read as distinct from the scrolling body.
- **`shared/ui/PageHeader.tsx`** — `tracking-tight` on the title, a touch more gap above the description.
- **`shared/ui/ComingSoon.tsx` / `shared/ui/RoleBlocked.tsx`** — lightened their secondary/description line to match `EmptyState`'s muted tone; left the primary message at its original weight since both are headline messages (an unbuilt-module notice, an access-denied notice), not a muted "nothing here yet" caption.
- **Status badges** (idea 4 of the brief) — audited, not changed. Every module's own `components/StatusBadge.tsx` (nine of them, one per module) already uses the identical tone→class mapping (`amber-50/700/200`, `blue-50/700/200`, `emerald-50/700/200`, `red-50/700/200`, plus `indigo`/`purple` for a couple of extra tones) through the same `Badge variant="outline"` pattern. They live under `modules/*/components/`, which this pass's hard rules explicitly forbid touching — nothing needed changing anyway, since the mapping was already consistent domain-wide.
- **Empty states with an icon** (idea 5 of the brief) — `shared/ui/PageSections.tsx`'s `EmptyState` has no icon slot, and adding one would be a new JSX element in a shared component, which the "no structure changes" rule for this pass rules out. Text-only muted treatment kept (and tightened slightly) instead.

`npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder, same shape as Styling 2/3's, deleted afterwards) both pass clean; `git status --short` confirms only the eight `shared/ui/` files above changed.

Never stored, so existing sittings need no migration. Shown as "Registration closes" on: the propose form (per date, read-only, plus an amber notice if already passed — still allowed) and the correct-&-resubmit dialog, QP "My Submissions", internal Proposals review, List View column (internal, QP, SDP), and the new sitting details dialog (Month View, all portals). Month View: hover text shows ID, name, QP and closing date; clicking an entry opens `components/SittingDetailsDialog.tsx`. ## QCTO feedback — Batch C (phase C1)

Demo seed data for QASA, EISA Instrument Validation and the EISA Calendar (items 1.3, 1.4, 1.9; source: `10-feedback-audit.md` "the one finding" and List 3). No workflow, permission, validation or type change, and nothing was renamed.

**How seeding works**
- `mock/data/demoSeed/` — `helpers.ts` (relative dates, stable seed ids, `step()` and a log for audit entries and notifications), `qasa.ts`, `instrumentValidation.ts`, `eisaCalendar.ts`, `index.ts`. Each file starts with a comment that lists its records and stages.
- Records are built in the shape the real api functions write. `step()` uses each module's own `workflow.ts` definition and makes the same checks as `shared/workflow/engine.ts`: the action exists from that stage, the role may act there, the approver is a different person, and a note is given where one is required. It writes the same audit entry, with a past timestamp. A seed mistake throws instead of writing a record no real action could produce. The notifications and documents that each api function writes are written too: QASA documents, the IAC presentation, approval-letter notes, instrument files and validation outcomes. Letters are generated from the record state, so they need no stored data.
- Ids keep the UUID format but are fixed (`5eed0001-…` QASA, `5eed0002-…` validations, `5eed0003-…` batches, `5eed0004-…` sittings), so later phases can link to them.
- Every date is relative to today. Actions fall on working days at office hours in SAST. Notifications older than 7 days are seeded as read.
- `mock/api/storage.ts`: new `SEED_VERSION` (now `'C1'`), `isSeedCurrent()`, `markSeedCurrent()`, `clearWorkflowStores()`. `index.tsx` calls `ensureDemoSeed()` once at module load. When the stored version differs, it clears the `assessmentsV2:` keys once and re-seeds. The person selection (`activePersonId`/`currentRole`) is kept. "Reset demo data" still clears every key, the version included, so the reload re-seeds. If the seed throws, the stores are cleared and the app opens empty instead of crashing.
- Store keys are now exported (`export` added, no rename) so the seed writes to the same keys: `qsa-addendum/api.ts` `KEY`, `instrument-validation/api.ts` `KEY`, `eisa-calendar/api.ts` `BATCHES_KEY`/`SITTINGS_KEY`, `shared/audit/auditLog.ts` `KEY`, `shared/notifications/store.ts` `KEY`.

**What is seeded**
- **QASA (12):** Electrical Trades, Welding and Plumbing are approved and their approval letters were sent to the QP, so they are eligible for EISA Instrument Validation. Gas Practitioner: approval letter drafted by the ASD and waiting for the DD's signature. Millwright: approved by the IAC, with the outcome (feedback) letter waiting for the CEO (item 1.4). Boilermaker: declined by the IAC, with the outcome letter signed. Rigger: waiting for the IAC decision. Lift Mechanic: with the DD for review. Drainlayer: returned to the ASD with a DD comment, and the DD's part-done checklist stays visible. Electrical Line Mechanic: negative evaluation, so corrections were requested from the QP. Water and Wastewater Process Controller: acknowledgement letter with the DD while the ASD's evaluation is half done. Metal Fabricator: just submitted, **not registered with SAQA** (the QP uploaded the three documents), with the acknowledgement letter at the ASD.
- **EISA Instrument Validation (4):** Plumbing is scheduled (slot agreed, QP to upload the instrument). Electrical Trades is in validation (the slot was agreed after an ASD counter-proposal). Welding was not approved and returned with the changes needed (cycle 2). Electrical Trades also has an earlier instrument, approved with written conditions.
- **EISA Calendar:** Plumbing batch proposed and waiting for ASD review. Welding batch rejected with a reason, which the QP can correct and resubmit. Electrical Trades batch published (3 dates, registration open). Sittings the ASD added directly: three in the past, Electrical Trades and Welding inside 3 months (registration closed), Welding more than 3 months ahead (registration open), one Plumbing sitting postponed (reason and proposed new date) and one Electrical Trades sitting cancelled (reason). The postponed and cancelled sittings each have their audit entry plus SDP and QP notifications. Batch sizes follow the approved QASA's sittings per year (Electrical Trades 3, Plumbing 2, Welding 2).

**Checked by running the seed** (compiled outside the project with a localStorage stand-in): no throw; 87 audit entries and 51 notifications (6 unread); `listApprovedQsaAddenda()` returns the 3 approved QASAs; `listEligibleQualifications()` returns Electrical Trades 3, Plumbing 2, Welding 2 (none on the default); every batch date passes `checkSittingDate()`; registration closed/open is as listed above; the DD return note and the QP corrections note are found by the screens' audit lookups; an old seed version is cleared once and the person selection is kept.

"My items" after Reset (same predicates as the dashboards; QP/SDP = their own records):

| Person (role) | QASA | EISA Instrument Validation | EISA Calendar |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sipho Ndlovu (ASD) | 8 | 3 | 2 batches |
| Thandeka Zulu (ASD) | 4 | 2 | 2 batches |
| Grace Mokoena (DD) | 8 | — | — |
| Peter Botha (DD) | 6 | — | — |
| Thabiso Molefe (IAC) | 5 | — | — |
| Palesa Nkosi (IAC) | 3 | — | — |
| Angela Fourie / David Molewa (CEO) | 7 each (only 1 can be signed, see below) | — | — |
| Nomvula Dlamini (QP) | 4 | 2 | 1 batch, 6 own sittings |
| Johan van der Merwe (QP) | 4 | 1 | 1 batch, 2 own sittings |
| Precious Mahlangu (QP) | 4 | 1 | 1 batch, 3 own sittings |
| Each SDP | no access | no access | 11 sittings (published, postponed, cancelled) |

Found, not changed (it would be a visibility/logic change):
- **CEO queue shows QASAs that are not ready to sign.** `createApplication()` starts every QASA's `outcomeState` at `ceo-signature`, and the CEO list (`qsa-addendum/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`) filters on that stage alone. So "Awaiting Your Signature" lists all 7 QASAs with no IAC decision yet, as well as Millwright, the only one that can be signed. The others open to "Not yet available — the IAC has not reached a decision." The fix would be to also require `evalState` `approved`/`declined` in that filter.
- **Fixed (2026-10-28):** the CEO's QASA "My items" now lists only QASAs the CEO can sign now (`canSignOutcomeNow()` in `qsa-addendum/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`: IAC decided + `canActInStage(OUTCOME_WORKFLOW, …)`), so after Reset it shows only Millwright; the CEO now also gets the My items / All items for my role switch, and "All items for my role" shows every QASA. Tiles, section title, description and empty state follow the list.
- **Not changed:** RPL Toolkit's CEO queue (`rpl-toolkit/internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`) has the same bug — `createApplication()` starts `outcomeState` at `ceo-signature` and the CEO filter checks that stage alone (no RPL seed data, so it only shows once a QP submits a toolkit).

Assumptions / left open:
- There are only 4 qualifications in `accreditedQualifications.ts`/`qpDirectory.ts`, so 8 of the 12 QASAs use other trade titles under the same three QPs. These are qualifications with no accredited SDPs yet, so they never reach the calendar. The SAQA ids for them are placeholders, as in Batch B.
- The not-registered QASA has the SAQA ID "Pending" and the submission date as its registration date. The real form requires both fields even when the gate check says "not registered".
- Past sittings (3) are seeded as `published` so phase C2 (Registration, Confirmation, Approval of Results) can link to them. There is no "held" status.
- Notifications are role-addressed (existing pattern), so every QP sees every QP notification.
- Batch dates are in "Y1" (the year of today + 240 days). If the seed runs early in a year, the published Electrical Trades batch and the directly added sittings can fall in the same year.

**1.12 — evidence upload on Site Visits: done.**
- Type (approved by the developer): one optional field `evidence?: { fileName, fileSize, uploadedAt, uploadedBy, category: 'report' | 'attendance' | 'other' }[]` on `SiteVisit` (`site-visits/types.ts`). A missing value is an empty list everywhere (`evidenceOf()`), so visits stored before Batch D load unchanged. `EvidenceDocument` and Approval of Results are untouched. There is no new storage key: the files live on the visit record in the existing `siteVisits:visits` store.
- `site-visits/evidence.ts` (new): the three areas ("Visit report" required, "Attendance registers", "Other evidence"), `evidenceOf()`, `hasVisitReportFile()`, the missing-report message.
- `site-visits/api.ts`: `uploadEvidence()` (allowed only while the plan is booked and the report is at `awaiting-execution`, the stage in which the form is editable). `report-submit` now also requires at least one "Visit report" file. Hook: `useUploadEvidence()` (`hooks.ts`).
- `components/ExecutionForm.tsx`: new "Supporting Evidence" card above Completed By. Each area lists its files (name, uploaded by, date) in every stage. The file picker (native `<input type="file" multiple>`, the same UI-only pick as Approval of Results) shows only when the form is editable. A report sent back for clarification is editable again, so more files can be added then.
- `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`: Submit Report is disabled until a visit report is uploaded, and the validation summary lists the missing file (title now "Complete these before you can submit the report:").
- Approve, send back, re-route, reject, share outcome and QP acknowledge: not touched. Who can edit is unchanged (ASD at `awaiting-execution`).
- Seed (`mock/data/demoSeed/siteVisits.ts`): new `upload()` step with the same stage check, and `report-submit` checks for a report file the same way the api does. One sample visit report each on SV7 (approved, waiting to share), SV8 (outcome shared) and SV11 (closed: approved, shared and acknowledged). SV5 (report in draft) has one attendance register. **SV6 (with the DD) and SV9 (sent back) also have a visit report.** Their reports were submitted, and under the new rule a submit without a report file is impossible, so the seed would otherwise hold records no real action could produce. `SEED_VERSION` `'C3'` → `'D'` (`mock/api/storage.ts`), so older browsers re-seed once.
- Not done (outside the brief): the QP's outcome summary on the external portal and the Marking & Moderation status panel in Approval of Results do not list the files (that would be a visibility decision). Uploaded files cannot be removed (the Approval of Results upload cannot either).

**2.2 — QASA status and approved instruments for all users: done.**
- New module `modules/qasa-status/` (`api.ts`, `hooks.ts`, `StatusView.tsx`, `index.ts`): one read-only page, "QASA Status and Approved Instruments", with two sections. *QASA status* shows qualification, QP and a plain-language status (`qasaPlainStatus()`). *Approved instruments* shows EISA instrument validations with `instrumentStatus === 'validated'`: qualification, validation date (the latest outcome's date) and conditions ("None" unless Approved with conditions). The api returns only those columns. No row opens anything.
- Menu: "QASA Status and Approved Instruments" after EISA Instrument Validation in `INTERNAL_NAV` and `EXTERNAL_NAV` (`config/nav.ts`). Access: `MODULES_VISIBLE_TO_ALL_ROLES = ['qasa-status']` in `shared/visibility/config.ts`, read by `isModuleVisible()` (`shared/visibility/moduleAccess.ts`). Because the nav, the Dashboard module list and the route guard all use that function, every role gets the menu item, the Dashboard line and the route. `index.tsx` renders the same view on both portals. Nav icon `ListChecks` (`shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`).
- `09-visibility-matrix.md`: correction 6 struck through and replaced by the 2.2 decision (28 Oct 2026), with the exact scope.
- Kept: the ASD's "Status Dashboard" tab inside EISA Instrument Validation (working list of all validations).
- **Assumption:** "all system users" includes SDPs. They get the same status-only view, and they still cannot open the QASA or EISA Instrument Validation modules, screens or records.

## QCTO feedback — Batch A3

Items 2.7 and 2.12, plus the RPL outcome-letter wording left open in Batch D (source: `10-qcto-feedback-items.md`, locations from `10-feedback-audit.md`). No id, route, role key, stage id or file was renamed. No workflow stage or action was added, removed or re-pointed: `REPORT_WORKFLOW` still has `outcome-shared` and `qp-acknowledge-outcome`; the api now moves through `outcome-shared` to `closed` in one step (below). The only type change is one optional field on `SiteVisit` (pre-approved).

**2.7 — RPL/ARPL monitoring at an SDP or assessment centre, no QP, no EISA date: done.**
- Type: `assessmentCentreName?: string | null` on `SiteVisit` (`site-visits/types.ts`). A missing value is fine everywhere, since it is read through `visitSiteName()`. No new storage key, no migration. For RPL/ARPL `qpId`/`qpName` are `''` and `sittingId` is `null`. The existing `sdpId`/`sdpName` hold the SDP.
- **SDP / assessment-centre choice:** the mock data has no assessment-centre list (EISA Confirmation's "centre" defaults to the registering SDP). So the form offers **"SDP"** (a picker over `SDP_DIRECTORY`, `mock/data/sdpDirectory.ts`) or **"Assessment centre"** (a typed name stored in `assessmentCentreName`). One of the two is required. `createVisit()` also rejects an RPL/ARPL visit without one, and drops any QP or sitting sent for that type.
- `site-visits/visitTypeLabels.ts`: new `visitHasQp()`, `visitHasSittingLink()` (Marking & Moderation and Post-EISA Monitoring only), `visitHasSiteSelection()`, `visitSiteName()`, `visitSiteKindLabel()`, `visitPartyName()`. `visitSubject()` reads "<qualification> at <SDP or centre>" for RPL/ARPL. Notification text and the workspace title use it, so they name the site and not a QP.
- `internal/NewVisitDialog.tsx`: for RPL/ARPL there is no QP field and no sitting link. There is a required "SDP or Assessment Centre" choice, the qualification, and a hand-entered date and venue. Switching to RPL/ARPL clears any QP, sitting link and sitting date. Readiness (no qualification, no sitting, no SDP), Marking & Moderation (sitting, no SDP) and Post-EISA (sitting link, read-only SDP from the sitting) behave as before.
- `api.ts` `buildAutoCreatedVisit()`: the RPL/ARPL visit from an RPL-flagged registration still comes from the monitoring item and is still booked straight away. It now takes the registration's SDP, has no QP, no sitting and **no date** (it used to copy the EISA sitting date). The officials set the date and venue on the Details tab. The monitoring item itself is unchanged (it already carried the SDP).
- List row (`internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`): the column is now "QP / SDP or Centre" (`visitPartyName()`), and an empty date shows "To be scheduled". Details tab (`internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`): RPL/ARPL shows "SDP" or "Assessment centre" (read-only) in place of the QP field. Execution form (`components/ExecutionForm.tsx`): RPL/ARPL shows the qualification and the SDP/centre, with no QP selector. Changing the qualification no longer resets a QP for this type.
- Seed (`mock/data/demoSeed/siteVisits.ts`): SV4 is at Thabo Technical College (no QP, no sitting) and dated today + 20 by the officials. SV8 is at Lerato Training Institute (no QP, no sitting) and dated today − 80. `eisaRegistration.ts` needed no change.

**2.12 — external Site Visits removed: done.**
- `shared/visibility/config.ts`: `EXTERNAL_MODULE_HIDDEN_FOR_ROLE['site-visits']` is `['SDP', 'QP']` (was `['SDP']`). `isModuleVisible()` drives the menu, the Dashboard module list and the route guard. So both external roles lose the menu item and the Dashboard line, and `/external/site-visits` shows `RoleBlocked`. The nav entry stays in `EXTERNAL_NAV` so that the address is still routed to the blocked screen and does not go to a blank page. External code (`external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`, `external/VisitDetail.tsx`, `SiteVisitsExternal`) is kept, unused.
- **Auto-complete on share (our default, to be confirmed with QCTO).** Exactly what changed, all in `site-visits/api.ts` `performReportAction()`:
  1. After the engine runs `share-visit-outcome` (`approved → outcome-shared`, audited by the engine as before), the saved `reportWorkflow.stageId` is set to `closed` in the same save.
  2. A second audit entry is written: "Visit completed — outcome shared, no QP acknowledgement needed (QCTO feedback 2.12)".
  3. The "Visit outcome shared" QP notification is sent only when the visit has a QP (`visitHasQp()` and a `qpId`), so RPL/ARPL visits notify nobody external. The body now reads "QCTO has shared the outcome of the visit for …", since the QP has no screen to review it on. Post-EISA's SDP notification and the simulated QA/Accreditation audit entry are unchanged.
  - `workflow.ts` is untouched. `qp-acknowledge-outcome` is still defined, but nothing calls it any more. Visits stored before A3 at `outcome-shared` would stay there (the seed reset below replaces them). The Report tab's closed banner now reads "approved — outcome shared, visit completed".
- Seed: the share step does the same thing, with the same audit entry and the QP notification only when the visit has a QP. SV8 (Lerato RPL/ARPL) and SV11 (Plumbing Marking & Moderation) are now **closed**, with no acknowledgement step. SV11's `qp-acknowledge-outcome` step and its "QP acknowledged" notification were removed. No seeded visit is left at `outcome-shared`.
- Links from other external screens: none found. Approval of Results (external) never linked to Site Visits; it only has a code comment that mentions it. Two plain-text lines mention visits but are not links, and were left: EISA Confirmation's "This step does not create a site visit." and EISA Registration's (SDP) "An RPL/ARPL monitoring item was automatically created."

**RPL outcome letter wording: done.** `shared/letters/templates.ts`: `generateLetter()` takes a new optional last argument `subject: LetterSubject = 'qasa'`. The two RPL calls (`rpl-toolkit/internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`, `rpl-toolkit/external/ApplicationDetail.tsx`) pass `'rpl-toolkit'`. Nothing else in the RPL module changed. The `title` argument is unchanged (it only sets the heading).

- New status-only module `modules/fisa-status/` (`api.ts`, `hooks.ts`, `StatusView.tsx`, `index.ts`), built like `qasa-status` (2.2). The api returns only Skills Programme, current stage (the `FISA_STANDARD_WORKFLOW` stage label) and status (`fisaStandardStatusLabel()`). No documents, checklists, reports, notes, audit trail, actions or links into a record.
- Access, in one place: `INTERNAL_MODULE_ROLE_OVERRIDE['fisa-status'] = ['Qualifications Development']` (`shared/visibility/config.ts`), read by `isModuleVisible()`, so only that role gets the menu item, the Dashboard line and the route. Internal nav only (`config/nav.ts`, after FISA); route in `index.tsx`; icon in `shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`. No other role's access changed, and Qualifications Development keeps its full FISA module access.

**2.8 — Post-EISA Monitoring at 45%, created automatically: done.**
- a) One constant: `POST_EISA_MONITORING_COMPETENCY_THRESHOLD_PERCENT = 45` (`site-visits/config.ts`). The one comparison is `isBelowPostEisaThreshold()` (`approval-of-results/workflow.ts`), used by the api, the batch screen and the seed. Every on-screen text and error reads the constant. No other "60" was a threshold (checked: the remaining 60s are day offsets, learner ids and a QASA practical-weight sentence).
- b) `approval-of-results/api.ts`: `performResultsAction()` on `chief-director-approve` (the only action into `approved`) calls `ensurePemVisitForBatch()`. Below 45% it creates one Post-EISA Monitoring visit at `ad-planning` with **no date** and the "results approved" flag set, writes an audit entry on the visit and on the batch, and notifies the Assistant Director (planner). It is idempotent: it does nothing once the batch has `pemVisitId`, and it links (never duplicates) a Post-EISA visit that already exists for the same sitting. The "Create Post-EISA Monitoring Visit" button calls the same helper and only shows for an approved batch below 45% with no visit.
- "To be scheduled": `site-visits/workflow.ts` `planningStatusLabel()` shows "To be scheduled" for a visit at `ad-planning` with no date. The planner sets the date on the Details tab. "Submit Plan" is disabled until a date is set, and `performPlanningAction()` rejects `plan-submit` without one (`MISSING_VISIT_DATE_MESSAGE`).
- c) Batch Details tab (`internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx` `PemVisitBanner`): "Competency rate 40% is below the 45% threshold. A Post-EISA Monitoring visit has been created and is waiting for a date." (or "…is scheduled for <date> (<status>)" once dated), with "Open the visit in Site Visits & Monitoring". The link goes to `site-visits?visit=<id>`, which the Site Visits dashboard opens straight away. Roles that cannot open Site Visits (Director, Chief Director) see a line saying the Assistant Director picks the date. Before final approval it says the visit will be created automatically.
- d) Seed: A9 Welding (40%) was approved by Francois Le Roux, and its visit (SV3) was created at that moment: "To be scheduled", no date. A10 Electrical Trades (50%) is now **above** the threshold and has no visit. The old SV3 (planned from A10 under the 60% rule) is gone. `RESULTS_SEED_IDS` keys are now `belowThresholdWelding` / `aboveThresholdElectrical`. The site-visits seed throws if any approved batch below the threshold is left without a visit.
- e) No PM- or CR- text is on screen: every remaining match is in a code comment, or in the backend-only `mock/integrations/` stubs, whose messages no screen shows.

**SEED_VERSION** `'A3'` → `'A4'` (`mock/api/storage.ts`).

**Checked:** the seed was bundled outside the project (Vite's esbuild and a localStorage stand-in) and run with the clock set to each of the 7 weekdays (2–8 Nov 2026), at 00:30 and 23:30 UTC. All 14 runs pass: approved below 45% = Welding 40% with one "To be scheduled" visit with no date; above = Plumbing 80% and Electrical Trades 50%, with no visit; one Post-EISA visit per sitting; one RPL toolkit with a file. Through the api, a Chief-Director-pending batch set below 45% and approved got exactly one dateless "To be scheduled" visit; the manual button and a second approval were both refused; the visit count went from 1 to 2. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json`, deleted afterwards) pass clean.

## QCTO feedback — Batch F (answers of 29 Oct 2026)

### 2.4 — QP permissions and the postpone/cancel request flow: done (4 Nov 2026)

**a) QP permissions** (`external/QpCalendarView.tsx`): the QP proposes dates, edits their own unpublished proposals ("Edit dates" on a batch awaiting review or approved but not yet published, `api.ts` `editBatchDates()`; an approved batch goes back to review) and corrects and resubmits a rejected batch (`ResubmitBatchDialog.tsx`, now also used in "edit" mode). The QP has no publish, postpone or cancel action. The QP only ever had read-only lists, so no direct postpone/cancel had to be taken away on the external side.

**b) ASD:** Postpone and Cancel are removed from the internal List View (`components/SittingListView.tsx`, `internal/InternalCalendarView.tsx`; `internal/PostponeSittingDialog.tsx` deleted). The ASD keeps Add Sitting, Approve / Reject / Publish on proposals, and Update / Delete on **unpublished** sittings only (proposed, approved, rejected). `api.ts` `editSitting()` / `deleteSitting()` refuse a published, postponed or cancelled sitting. The Add/Update dialog text says this.

**c) Request flow** (`changeRequests.ts`, `workflow.ts` `POSTPONEMENT_REQUEST_WORKFLOW` / `CANCELLATION_REQUEST_WORKFLOW`, role constants in `config.ts`):
- Raised on a **published** sitting by the QP (own sittings only) or the DD: "Request postponement" (with a proposed new date — must be after the sitting, a working day, not a public holiday, outside the lead time) or "Request cancellation". A reason and a formal letter are required for both (`components/RaiseChangeRequestDialog.tsx`; UI-only upload: name, size, type, date — `components/letterFile.ts`). One open request per sitting.
- **Postponement:** DD recommends → Director decides (Approve / Decline; comment required to decline).
- **Cancellation:** DD recommends → Director recommends → CEO decides (Approve / Decline; comment required to decline).
- **Different person:** every action has `requiresDifferentActor`, and `hasActedOnRequest()` refuses anyone who already acted on the same request (raised or recommended it), so the person who recommends can never also decide. A DD who raises a request cannot recommend it; another DD must.
- **On approve:** `api.ts` `applyApprovedSittingChange()` (the former `performSittingLifecycleAction()`, no longer called from any screen) postpones or cancels the sitting with the existing SDP and QP notices. A postponement's notice and the request detail list the learners already registered — they stay on the original sitting and are not moved. `SITTING_LIFECYCLE_WORKFLOW`'s `published` stage now allows the Director and the CEO (the deciders), not the ASD.
- **Screens:** internal tab **Requests** with "My items / All items for my role" (`components/ChangeRequestsList.tsx`); "My items" = requests the person raised or acted on, plus open ones waiting at their role's stage that they have not acted on. Request detail with history, recommendations, decision and the action buttons for whoever's turn it is (`components/ChangeRequestDetailDialog.tsx`). QP tab **My requests** with the status of each. The DD, Director and CEO open the calendar on the Requests tab; the internal calendar now shows that role's in-app notifications.
- **Notifications:** the QP at every step (raised, each recommendation, approved, declined); the next role at each step. **Audit:** record type `eisa-calendar-request` (raise, each recommendation, decision), shown in the calendar's Audit Trail tab and in the request detail; the resulting postpone/cancel is audited on the sitting as before.
- **Access:** the Director, DD and CEO could not open the EISA Calendar (only the ASD is on the `eisa-calendar` team). Granted in `shared/visibility/config.ts` `INTERNAL_MODULE_ROLE_OVERRIDE['eisa-calendar'] = ['ASD', 'Deputy Director', 'Director', 'CEO']`; `config/teams.ts` label updated.

**d) Propose form** (`external/ProposeDatesForm.tsx`): choosing a qualification pre-fills one date row per required sitting (the approved QASA's sittings per year, batch E). Those rows have no remove button; more rows can be added. "On demand" (`eisaSittingsOnDemand` on the QASA) is now read by `listEligibleQualifications()` (`EligibleQualification.onDemand`, `EisaSittingBatch.onDemand`): no minimum beyond one date, shown as "Required sittings per year: On demand". The same rule applies in the edit/resubmit dialog and in `api.ts` (`minimumDateCount()`).

**Seed** (`mock/data/demoSeed/eisaCalendar.ts`): R1 — S6 Plumbing postponement, recommended by Grace Mokoena and approved by Zanele Mthembu (decided; S6 keeps its postponed record, now from the request). R2 — S8 Welding postponement raised by Precious Mahlangu, waiting for the DD (S8 has Khanya's registration, so its learners are listed). R3 — S17 Plumbing cancellation raised by Johan van der Merwe, recommended by Peter Botha, waiting for the Director. S7's earlier direct cancellation is kept as it was (written before this rule). Sitting ids are unchanged (same creation order). **SEED_VERSION** `'A7'` → `'A8'`.

**Checked:** seed bundled with esbuild and run with the clock on 7 consecutive days in three windows (4–10 Nov 2026, 14–20 Dec 2026, 15–21 Mar 2027): all 21 runs pass, 24 sittings and the 3 requests at the expected stages every time.

**Assumptions (docs only, not on screen):**
- The Director decides postponements (and recommends cancellations); the CEO decides cancellations. Constants `POSTPONEMENT_DECIDER_ROLE`, `CANCELLATION_SECOND_RECOMMENDER_ROLE`, `CANCELLATION_DECIDER_ROLE` in `eisa-calendar/config.ts`.
- "The DD of the EISA Readiness Unit" is the existing Deputy Director role (`REQUEST_RECOMMENDER_ROLE`); both seeded DDs can act.
- The formal letter is required for both postponement and cancellation requests.
- A recommendation step has one action, "Recommend", with an optional comment; only the deciding step can decline.
- The required sittings per year are a **minimum** on the single-date form (extra rows are special sittings), so `proposeSittingBatch()` now accepts at least the required count instead of exactly it. The bulk upload (3.4) still requires an exact match.
- Editing an approved-but-unpublished batch sends it back to "Awaiting QCTO review", because the ASD approved different dates.
- The ASD's Update and Delete apply to unpublished sittings only; a special sitting the ASD adds goes straight to published (CR-027), so after that it changes only through a request.
- A postponed sitting keeps its original date with the new date shown next to it (as before); approving a postponement does not create a new sitting on the new date.

### 3.4 — Bulk upload of EISA dates (QP side): done (4 Nov 2026)

**Library:** `package.json` has no Excel or CSV library (no `xlsx`, `exceljs`, `papaparse` or similar) and nothing in `src` imports one, so nothing was installed: the upload accepts **.csv only**, the template is generated as CSV, and the screen says "Save your spreadsheet as CSV". A small built-in parser (`bulkUpload.ts` `parseCsv()`) handles quoted fields, CRLF, a byte-order mark and semicolon-separated files.

**Screen** (`external/BulkUploadDatesDialog.tsx`, button "Upload dates from a spreadsheet" on `external/QpCalendarView.tsx`): 1. download the template, 2. choose the file, 3. preview table (every row with its own messages; the QP name is the logged-in person; the registration closing date is calculated and shown), then "Submit for QCTO Review", disabled until every row passes. The single-date "Propose Sitting Dates" form stays as it was, for special sittings.

**Template** (`buildTemplateCsv()`): fixed columns Qualification title, Qualification ID (SAQA), NQF level, Credit value, EISA date; one row per required sitting for each of this QP's qualifications with accredited SDPs, NQF level and credits filled in from the QASA when there is one (`EligibleQualification.nqfLevel` / `credits`). On reading the file, extra columns are ignored and a missing required column rejects the file with a message naming the column.

**Row rules** (`parseBulkFile()`): the qualification ID must be one of this QP's qualifications with accredited SDPs (the title, if given, must match it); the date must be a real date (YYYY-MM-DD, YYYY/MM/DD or DD/MM/YYYY); not a weekend or public holiday and outside the minimum lead time (`checkSittingDate()`, the shared working-day helper); the registration closing date must not have passed; no duplicate date for the same qualification in the file, nor one already on the calendar for it (proposed, approved, published or postponed); the number of dates per qualification and year must equal its sittings per year (at least one for "On demand").

**Submit:** one normal proposal batch per qualification and year (`toBatchInputs()`), through `api.ts` `proposeBulkSittingBatches()`, which re-checks every batch (exact count) before writing any of them. The batches then follow the usual ASD review.

**Checked:** bundled with esbuild against the seeded data: the template, a correct file (3 dates → 1 batch at "Awaiting QCTO review"), a file with an extra column, a semicolon file with DD/MM/YYYY dates, a file missing "NQF level" (rejected with the column named), and a file with an impossible date, a weekend, a public holiday, a date inside the lead time, a passed closing date, another QP's qualification, a wrong title, a duplicate and a wrong count (each row shows its own messages). The api refused an uneven file even when called directly.

**Assumptions (docs only, not on screen):**
- Bulk upload is QP-side only (QCTO has not decided on an internal one — see Open questions).
- Each qualification and year in the file becomes its own proposal batch; dates for two years in one file are two batches, each needing the full count.
- NQF level and credit value are required columns but their values are informational (shown in the preview, not checked against the QASA).
- A date already on the calendar for the same qualification counts as a duplicate.
- The public holiday list (`config/holidays.ts`) covers 2026–2027; dates in later years are checked for weekends and lead time only, until the list is extended.

## QCTO feedback — Batch G (answers of 29 Oct 2026)

All in `src/features/assessments-v2/modules/site-visits/` unless another path is given.

### 3.7 — Script verification on Marking & Moderation: done (4 Nov 2026)

**Screen:** a "Script Verification" section on the Execution & Report tab of a Marking & Moderation visit (`components/ScriptVerificationSection.tsx`, shown from `components/ExecutionForm.tsx`). A sample table with one row per verified script: script / learner reference, component, marked outcome, moderated finding (Agree / Disagree) and a comment. "Add script" adds a row and the bin icon removes one. The sample size is the number of rows and is counted automatically. Below the table, the overall result: Verified, Verified with findings or Not verified. The section is read-only once the report is submitted.

**Rule:** Submit Report needs at least one script row, every row complete (reference, component, marked outcome and finding; the comment is optional), and an overall result (`scriptVerification.ts` `getScriptVerificationIssues()`). It is enforced on screen (the missing items show in the "Complete these before you can submit" list, `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`) and in `api.ts` `performReportAction('report-submit')`.

**Data:** one optional `scriptVerification` field on `SiteVisit` (`types.ts`), read through `scriptVerificationOf()`, so visits stored before Batch G still load and still show; they get an empty section. Saved by `api.ts` `saveScriptVerification()`, only while the report is awaiting execution. QPs only: the SDP option was already removed (3.6).

**Assumptions (docs only, not on screen):**
- Script verification is part of the one Marking & Moderation visit: the existing checklist and the new script verification are two sections of the same visit and report, not a separate visit type.
- A row counts once it has a reference, component, marked outcome and finding; the comment is optional.
- The marked outcome is free text (a mark or a competency outcome), since components are marked differently.

### 2.5 — More visit types, usable across domains: done (4 Nov 2026)

**New visit types** (`types.ts` `VisitType`, labels in `visitTypeLabels.ts`): "Marking guideline discussion", "Instrument validation visit" and "QASA development visit" (each with a QP chosen from the QP directory, no SDP, no qualification and no sitting link), and "Other / special meeting" (no QP: a free-text meeting title instead). Each new type has a plan of date, QP or title, purpose and venue (`internal/NewVisitDialog.tsx`; purpose is required, `api.ts` `createVisit()`), the same planning chain (ASD → DD → Domain Director → Sub Domain Admin), and an Execution & Report step with the Batch D uploads (visit report required, attendance registers, other evidence), a notes field ("Meeting notes" for a special meeting) and Completed By / Completion Date.

**Reused form sections:** the Marking guideline discussion visit reuses the guideline lines 1.4–1.8 of the Marking & Moderation checklist (`mock/data/siteVisitFormSections.ts` `buildMarkingGuidelineDiscussionSections()`). The other three new types have no checklist: their record is the notes and the uploaded report (`api.ts` `buildChecklistForType()`).

**Domain:** an optional `domain` on every visit, one of Assessment, Accreditation and Quality Assurance, Qualifications Development or Certification (`visitDomains.ts`), default Assessment (missing = Assessment, so stored visits need no migration). Chosen in the New Visit dialog, editable on the Details tab while the report is open, shown as a "Domain" column in every visit list, with a "Domain" filter on the All Visits tab (`internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`). No role's access changed: the internal rules in `shared/visibility/config.ts` are unchanged.

**Assumptions (docs only, not on screen):**
- The cross-domain roles are open. QCTO has not yet decided which roles outside Assessment may plan or run visits, so the Domain field is a label and filter only; the planning and report chain is the Assessment one for every domain (see Open questions).
- Venue stays required for the new types (a virtual meeting can say "MS Teams").
- A special meeting has no stakeholder to share with (no QP), so it never shows "Share report with stakeholder".

### Shared visit outcomes (replaces the removed QP acknowledgement step): done (4 Nov 2026)

**Internal:** on a visit whose report has been submitted or approved (report stage `dd-report-review`, `approved` or `closed`), the Execution & Report tab shows "Share report with stakeholder" with No / Yes (`internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx` `StakeholderShareCard`). Default No. Only the Deputy Director chooses (`config.ts` `STAKEHOLDER_SHARE_ROLE`); every other role sees "Shared with …" or "Not shared". Choosing Yes asks for the outcome summary the QP will see. Sharing notifies the QP and writes an audit entry; choosing No again writes an audit entry (`api.ts` `setStakeholderShare()`). Stored as an optional `stakeholderShare` field on `SiteVisit`; missing = not shared. Only visits with a QP can be shared (`stakeholderShare.ts` `visitCanBeShared()`).

**"Share Visit Outcome" renamed "Complete Visit":** the DD's last step on an approved report (workflow action id `share-visit-outcome`, unchanged so stored audit entries still match) now only completes the visit. It no longer notifies the QP, so the per-visit choice is the only way a QP hears about a visit. The Post-EISA Monitoring hand-off to the SDP and to QA/Accreditation (PM-07) is unchanged. Its audit texts no longer carry developer notes ("Visit completed"; "Shared the Post-EISA Monitoring report with Quality Assurance and Accreditation").

**External:** a new, separate QP page "Visit Outcomes" (path `visit-outcomes`, `external/VisitOutcomesPage.tsx`), read-only: visit type, visit date, outcome summary and the shared report file name(s), for visits shared with this QP only (`api.ts` `listSharedVisitOutcomesForQp()`, which returns only those columns). No acknowledgement, no scheduling, no link into a visit. Menu item on the external portal only (`config/nav.ts`), hidden and blocked for SDPs (`shared/visibility/config.ts` `EXTERNAL_MODULE_HIDDEN_FOR_ROLE['visit-outcomes'] = ['SDP']`). The external Site Visits section stays removed: `EXTERNAL_MODULE_HIDDEN_FOR_ROLE['site-visits'] = ['SDP', 'QP']` is unchanged, so its menu item is gone and its old address still shows the "not available for your role" screen to the QP.

**Assumptions (docs only, not on screen):**
- Sharing is decided per visit, by QCTO (the Deputy Director who reviews the report), default not shared.
- The outcome summary is written by the DD when sharing, not taken from the report notes.
- A report can be shared once submitted, before approval, as QCTO asked; the DD can take the share back (No), which removes it from the QP's page.

`mock/data/demoSeed/siteVisits.ts` now has 16 visits: SV13 Marking guideline discussion (with the Domain Director), SV14 Instrument validation visit (report approved, shared with the QP), SV15 QASA development visit (Qualifications Development domain, completed and shared), SV16 Other / special meeting "Certification hand-off working session" (Certification domain, booked, no QP). SV7 (4 scripts, Verified with findings) and SV11 (2 scripts, Verified) have a filled script verification: the seed applies the same Submit Report rule as the api, so every submitted Marking & Moderation visit needs one. The QP Nomvula Dlamini (the default QP) sees two shared outcomes (SV14, SV15) on Visit Outcomes; every other visit is not shared. Behaviour: Submit Report on the draft SV5 is refused with no script rows, with an incomplete row and with no overall result, then passes; the section cannot be changed after submit; on SV7 the ASD cannot share, sharing without a summary is refused, sharing notifies the QP once and writes one audit entry, Complete Visit sends the QP nothing, and No removes it from the QP's list; a meeting cannot be shared; a meeting without a title and a new type without a purpose are refused; a new meeting defaults to Assessment with no checklist; the guideline discussion has the 5 reused lines. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and the scoped `tsc -p` check pass clean.

## QCTO feedback 3.10 — FISA Validation of Instrument Report (QCTO's form of 29 Oct 2026)

### 3.10 — FISA Instrument Validation on QCTO's own form: done (5 Nov 2026)

**What changed:** the FISA Instrument Validation checklist is replaced by QCTO's "FISA Validation of Instrument Report", with QCTO's wording. Until now FISA borrowed the EISA Instrument Validation checklist; EISA keeps that checklist unchanged. The FISA Standards checklist is **not** changed (open question: does QCTO have its own).

**Form** (`mock/data/fisaInstrumentValidationChecklistItems.ts`): section 1 Development of assessment instrument (1.1–1.6, and 1.7 as 1.7.1 / 1.7.2, each with QCTO's lead-in "The following has been indicated on the Assessment:"), section 3 Content Coverage (3.1–3.5), section 4 Marking Memorandum/Guideline (4.1–4.3), section 5 Technical Aspects (5.1–5.8): 24 Yes / No lines. Section 2 "Compliance to Standards set for Qualification Exit Level Outcomes" has no items in QCTO's file, so it is a comment box. Then 6. Findings and Recommendations and the sign-off (full names, designation, a "Signed" tick on the typed name, date).

**Header:** Name of QAP/SDP and Title of qualification/skills programme are prefilled from the instrument (editable). Evaluator full names and Date completed are filled in when the outcome is recorded (the person recording it and the time). Contact number, SP ID, NQF level, Credits, Date/period of FISA, Duration, Total marks and Competency/pass mark are typed by the evaluator.

**Screens:** the ASD fills the form on the Validate tab (`modules/fisa/components/FisaValidationFormEditor.tsx`, used by `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx`); every change saves as it is made (`api.ts` `saveFisaValidationForm()`, only during ASD validation). The FISA Validation Report tab (internal and SDP, `components/FisaValidationReport.tsx`) shows the outcome, the header, every section with its Yes / No ticks and any comments, section 2, findings and sign-off in a print-friendly layout (the Print button and the audit trail are hidden when printing).

**Rules:** before any outcome (Approved, Approved with conditions, Not approved) every line must be answered Yes or No, Findings and Recommendations filled in, and the sign-off has full names, designation and the "Signed" tick. Checked on screen (the "Complete these before recording an outcome" list) and in `api.ts` `performFisaValidationAction()` (`validationForm.ts` `getFisaValidationFormIssues()`). Comments per line are **optional** on FISA (`FISA_VALIDATION_REQUIRES_COMMENT = false`); CR-011's mandatory comment still applies to EISA and every other checklist. Allocation, outcomes, "Not approved" back to the SDP and the workflow are unchanged. After "Not approved" the next cycle starts on a blank checklist, findings and sign-off; the typed header details carry over.

**Old records:** the new fields on `FisaValidationRecord` are optional and read through `validationForm.ts` `fisaValidationFormOf()`. A stored record that is not yet validated is moved onto the new form when read (its answers on the old lines are dropped). A record validated on the old checklist keeps it, and its report shows that checklist under "Checklist" with no QCTO header.

**Assumptions (docs only, not on screen):**
- This replaces the instrument validation checklist only; the FISA Standards checklist is unchanged until QCTO answers.
- Item 3.4 is incomplete in QCTO's file (it ends with "OR"); shown without the "OR" until QCTO supplies the rest.
- Section 2 has no items in QCTO's file, so it is one optional comment box.
- The instrument and its FISA Standard hold no contact number, SP ID, NQF level or credits, so these are typed by the evaluator, not prefilled.
- The signature is a typed name plus a "Signed" tick (UI-only); the signature date is set when the tick is made.
- "Date completed" is the date the outcome is recorded.

## QCTO feedback — Batch I (answer of 29 Oct 2026)

### 3.14 — FISA results in Approval of Results: done (5 Nov 2026)

QCTO: Approval of Results covers EISA and FISA, but they work differently. EISA is centralised (the QP submits). FISA is decentralised: there is no QP, the SDP conducts the assessment and submits the results. Same evidence rules: an approval letter generated and sent to the SDP on approval, a verification report, an audit trail.

All in `src/features/assessments-v2/modules/approval-of-results/` unless another path is given.

**Tabs:** Approval of Results has an "EISA" and a "FISA" tab on both portals (`internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`, `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`). The EISA tab is the existing screen. Internally every role on the module sees both tabs; the "My items / All in my team" switch applies to both. Externally the QP sees the EISA tab and the SDP sees the FISA tab (`shared/visibility/config.ts` `APPROVAL_OF_RESULTS_EXTERNAL_TABS`).

**SDP access:** `approval-of-results` is removed from `EXTERNAL_MODULE_HIDDEN_FOR_ROLE` for the SDP (`shared/visibility/config.ts`), so the SDP has the menu item, the Dashboard line and the route. The SDP sees only its own FISA batches (`sdpId`).

**FISA results batch (SDP, `external/FisaResultsView.tsx`):** "New Results Batch" picks a skills programme whose FISA Standard is Approved (`external/NewFisaResultsBatchDialog.tsx`) and opens a draft ("Awaiting submission"). In the draft (`external/FisaResultsBatchDetail.tsx`) the SDP:
- gives each of its enrolled learners (Quality Assurance's learner enrolment, `mock/api/qaDomain.ts`) a result: Not on this batch, Competent or Not yet competent;
- uploads evidence files, each with a tag (Assessment instrument, Marksheet, Moderation report, Attendance register, Other evidence; `config.ts` `FISA_EVIDENCE_TAG_OPTIONS`). UI-only: file name, size, type and date are kept, the tag can be changed and a file removed;
- ticks the declaration and clicks "Submit results".
Learners and evidence save as they change. Submit needs at least one learner, at least one tagged file and the declaration (`workflow.ts` `fisaSubmissionIssues()`, shown on screen and enforced in `fisaApi.ts` `submitFisaResultsBatch()`). Only the SDP that created the batch can change or submit it, and only enrolled learners at that SDP, each once. A draft has no audit entry and is not shown to QCTO.

**Internal chain (`internal/FisaResultsTab.tsx`, `internal/FisaResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx`):** `workflow.ts` `FISA_RESULTS_WORKFLOW` reuses `RESULTS_WORKFLOW`'s stages and actions unchanged, with a `sdp-draft` stage and a "Submit results" action in front, and the SDP (not the QP) on `declined`. Same checks: the ASD's compliance notes, evidence evaluation notes and folder checklist must be complete before "Send to Deputy Director" (`api.ts` `assertAsdReviewComplete()`, now shared by EISA and FISA); every stage belongs to a different role (ASD → Deputy Director → Director → Chief Director), so it is a different person at every stage, as on EISA. The workspace has Details (learners, tagged evidence, declaration), Compliance Review, Approval Chain, Approval Letter (once approved) and Audit Trail. Audit record type `fisa-results-batch`.

**Declined:** a decline needs a reason at any stage and notifies the SDP; the SDP resubmits from its batch, which starts a new cycle with a blank checklist and review notes while keeping every decline reason (the same as an EISA batch).

**Final approval (`fisaApi.ts` `performFisaResultsAction()`):** the Chief Director's "Final Approval" generates the approval letter (shared letter template, new `fisa-results` subject in `shared/letters/templates.ts`), records it as sent to the SDP, notifies the SDP, records the simulated "Recommended to Certification" hand-off, and writes an audit entry for each. No Post-EISA Monitoring visit is created for FISA.

**Downloads (EISA and FISA):** approved batches show "Download approval letter" and "Download verification report" (`components/BatchDownloads.tsx`, `downloads.ts`): on the internal Approval Chain tab, the QP's EISA batch detail and the SDP's FISA batch detail. Both are HTML files built in the browser (no new libraries). The verification report lists the batch details, learner results, evidence (with tags for FISA), the approval chain with names, roles and dates (read from the audit trail, latest cycle), earlier declines and the full audit trail. The EISA approval letter uses a new `eisa-results` subject; the QASA approval letter wording is unchanged.

**Small refactor:** the decline-with-reason action bar moved from `internal/ResultsBatchWorkspace.tsx` to `components/ResultsActionBar.tsx` (takes the workflow definition) so both tracks use it. EISA behaviour is unchanged.

**Assumptions (docs only, not on screen):**
- The FISA chain mirrors EISA: ASD compliance and evidence review → Deputy Director → Director → Chief Director, with the same folder checklist and decline/resubmit handling.
- One outcome per learner (Competent or Not yet competent). Whether QCTO wants per-component outcomes for FISA is an open question.
- The SDP sees the FISA tab only; the QP sees the EISA tab only (QP is None on FISA, Table A).
- The learner list is the SDP's enrolled learners from the Quality Assurance domain (the same source as Learner EISA Registration).
- At least one evidence file is required to submit; the tag list is a first version (Assessment instrument, Marksheet, Moderation report, Attendance register, Other evidence).
- There is no submission deadline for FISA results (the 21-working-day rule is tied to an EISA sitting).
- Notifications are role-addressed, so "the SDP is notified" reaches the SDP inbox like the QP's EISA notifications do.

**Open questions added:** per-component outcomes for FISA results; whether FISA results have a submission deadline; whether QCTO has its own evidence tag list.

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION = 'A10'`)

`mock/data/demoSeed/fisaResults.ts`, all for the Approved "Electrical Installation Skills Programme": R1 Khanya Skills Academy, awaiting submission (3 learners, one marksheet, not submitted); R2 Thabo Technical College, with the ASD (review not started); R3 Khanya Skills Academy, with the Deputy Director (ASD Lindiwe Khumalo reviewed it); R4 Khanya Skills Academy, approved (Sipho Ndlovu → Grace Mokoena → Zanele Mthembu → Busisiwe Radebe), approval letter sent and recommended to Certification. The seed applies the same submission rule, review check and workflow steps as the api. The EISA seed is unchanged.

**Checked:** 7-weekday seed check (Mon 2 to Sun 8 Nov 2026, 00:30 and 23:30 UTC, 14 runs, all pass): EISA still has 10 batches at the same stages; 4 FISA batches at the four stages, all tagged, the draft with no audit entry, R4 with the letter and hand-off audit entries and a 4-person chain in date order; Approval of Results visible to the SDP and QP, tabs SDP = FISA and QP = EISA, the SDP still blocked from QASA; the QASA approval letter wording unchanged, the FISA and EISA letters name their results, the verification report lists evidence, chain and audit and escapes text. Behaviour: submit is refused without the declaration, with no learners or no evidence, by another SDP, and the draft is locked after submit; learners from another SDP and duplicates are refused; a non-approved standard is refused; submit notifies the ASD once; the ASD cannot advance before the review is complete and the review locks after; the DD and the SDP cannot act at the ASD stage; a decline without a reason is refused, a decline notifies the SDP once, only the owning SDP can resubmit (cycle 2, fresh review); final approval records the letter, the signer and the hand-off, notifies the SDP once and creates no site visit; the EISA store is untouched. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and the scoped `tsc -p` check pass clean.

## Batch K (QCTO 5 Nov review)

QCTO's review of 5 Nov 2026 (change request doc v14, rows 05.1, 05.2, 05.5, 05.6, 05.7, 05.8 and 29.5). All in `src/features/assessments-v2/modules/qsa-addendum/` unless another path is given. FISA Standards are not touched: they keep "Declined" until QCTO answers.

### 05.7 — A QASA is never declined: done (6 Nov 2026)

"Declined" is gone from the QASA: the `declined` stage and the `eval-iac-decline` action are removed from `workflow.ts` `EVAL_WORKFLOW`, and `IAC_OUTCOME_OPTIONS` is now Approved or Remediation requested. Remediation is unchanged (back to the QP with the IAC comment). The feedback letter flow is reached only after the IAC approves (`api.ts` `performOutcomeAction()` refuses anything else; the CEO's "My items" only lists IAC-approved QASAs). The letter notes, status labels (`workflow.ts` `qsaOverallStatusLabel()`, `modules/qasa-status/api.ts`) and the red "declined" badge tone are removed.

### 05.6 — One letter only: done (6 Nov 2026)

The CEO-signed feedback letter is the approval. Removed: `APPROVAL_LETTER_WORKFLOW`, `performApprovalLetterAction()`, `saveApprovalLetterDraft()`, their hooks, the `approvalState` / `approvalLetterDraft` / `approvalLetterSignedBy` fields, `APPROVAL_LETTER_SIGNER_ROLE`, the "Approval Letter" tabs (internal and QP) and the "Approval letter sent" notification. The shared `approval` letter template stays: Approval of Results uses it.

"Approved QASA" now has one meaning, `workflow.ts` `isQasaApproved()`: IAC recorded Approved AND the CEO signed the feedback letter (still true while under review, 05.2). It replaces `isApprovalLetterFinal()` everywhere:
- `api.ts` `listApprovedQsaAddenda()` → EISA Instrument Validation (`modules/instrument-validation/api.ts`, unchanged — it calls this list) and the EISA Calendar (`modules/eisa-calendar/api.ts`);
- `modules/qasa-status/api.ts` (status page);
- `mock/data/demoSeed/eisaCalendar.ts` (sittings per year);
- the QASA dashboards' "Approved" tiles (internal and QP).

The Calendar now reads the approved sittings through `assessmentDetails.ts` `approvedSittingsOf()`: the values in the Certification hand-off snapshot taken at the CEO's signature (falls back to the evaluation report if there is no snapshot). When the CEO signs, the QP gets one notification, "QASA approved — feedback letter signed", and sees the signed letter on the Feedback Letter tab (`letters.ts` `feedbackLetterOf()`; the QP only sees it once signed).

### 05.5 — Acknowledgement letter issued on submission: done (6 Nov 2026)

`api.ts` `createApplication()` sets `acknowledgementIssuedAt`, writes the audit entry "Acknowledgement letter issued on submission" (record type `qsa-addendum-acknowledgement`, by the submitting QP) and notifies the QP ("Acknowledgement letter issued"). `ACK_WORKFLOW`, `performAckAction()`, the `ackState` field, the ASD preview and DD review steps and their buttons are removed. The QP's Acknowledgement tab always shows the letter (`letters.ts` `acknowledgementLetterOf()`, dated the submission day); the "still being reviewed internally" text is gone. The internal tab shows the letter and "Issued to the QP on submission". Evaluation never waited on the acknowledgement (`performEvaluationAction()` never read `ackState`), and the dashboards no longer count acknowledgement stages under "With ASD" / "With Deputy Director".

### 05.8 — QP Progress view: done (6 Nov 2026)

On the QP's QASA detail the Audit Trail tab is replaced by **Progress** (`components/QpProgress.tsx`, data from `progress.ts` `qpProgressOf()`): Submitted → ASD evaluation → Deputy Director moderation → Director Office → IAC → CEO signature → Approved, or Remediation requested. The current stage is highlighted with the date it entered that stage; earlier stages are ticked. `qpProgressOf()` reads the audit trail only for dates and returns no names and no comments, except the remediation comment (ASD negative evaluation or IAC remediation) the QP has to act on. A QASA under review shows an "Approved — under review" banner with the date the review started. The internal Audit Trail tab is unchanged (`components/AuditTrail.tsx`; the approval-letter record type it no longer has is dropped).

### 05.1 — "Certification notified of the assessment details": done (6 Nov 2026)

`RECOMMENDED_TO_CERTIFICATION_LABEL` is renamed `CERTIFICATION_NOTIFIED_LABEL` = "Certification notified of the assessment details" (`assessmentDetails.ts`), used for the audit entry (api and seed). The hand-off fields are `notifiedAt` / `notifiedBy` (date and who kept). The Details card line reads "Certification notified of the assessment details on <date> by <who>." Approval of Results' "Recommended to the Certification domain" is not changed.

### 05.2 — Validity is the lifespan of the qualification, and review: done (6 Nov 2026)

- The validity-period-in-years field is removed from the editor (`components/AssessmentDetailsEditor.tsx`), validation (`assessmentDetails.ts` `validateAssessmentDetails()`), the record type, the card and the Certification hand-off.
- The card shows "Valid for the lifespan of the qualification (until <registration end date>)" (`assessmentDetails.ts` `qasaValidityText()`), and the feedback letter says the same and that it may be reviewed at any time. The end dates are new mock data in one place: `mock/data/qualificationRegistrationEndDates.ts` (keyed by SAQA ID), read through `mock/api/qualificationRegistry.ts` `getQualificationRegistrationEndDate()`. An unknown SAQA ID (e.g. "Pending") shows "registration end date not recorded".
- **Start review:** on an approved QASA, the DD (`config.ts` `QASA_REVIEW_STARTER_ROLE`) sees a "Review" card on the Details tab with a "Start review" button. A reason is required (`shared/ui/ReasonDialog.tsx`); the workflow action `eval-start-review` (`approved` → `asd-evaluation`, `requiresNote`) writes the audit entry "Start review" with the reason. `review.ts` `withReviewStarted()` sets `review` (who, when, reason), resets the ASD and DD checklists, the findings/recommendation, the IAC presentation and minutes, and puts the feedback letter back to "Awaiting CEO signature". The QASA then goes through the normal route (ASD evaluation, DD, Director Office, IAC, CEO). The api refuses a review on a QASA that is not approved or is already under review. The QP and the ASD are each notified once (the QP's notice does not carry the DD's reason).
- **While under review** the QASA stays approved for every other module (`isQasaApproved()` is true), shows "Approved — under review" (status page; the QASA badges add the current stage in brackets), and the Calendar keeps the approved sittings from the hand-off snapshot even if the ASD changes them. The ASD sees "Deputy Director — reason for review" on the Evaluation tab. When the CEO signs the new feedback letter, the review is cleared ("Review completed — QASA approved again" audit entry) and Certification is notified again with the reviewed details.

### 29.5 — SAQA ID next to the qualification title: done (6 Nov 2026)

`modules/qasa-status/api.ts` returns `saqaId` on both row types (approved instruments look it up from the instrument validation's QASA), and `StatusView.tsx` has a "SAQA ID" column in the QASA status table and the approved instruments table. The page is the same on both portals, so internal and external both get it.

### Assumptions (docs only, not on screen)

- **The Deputy Director starts a review** of an approved QASA (`QASA_REVIEW_STARTER_ROLE`). QCTO said a review can happen "at any time if there is a need" but did not say who starts it.
- A review restarts the whole route, with fresh ASD and DD checklists and a new CEO signature; the assessment components and sittings stay filled in for the ASD to confirm or change. Until the review is approved, the approved values (in the Certification hand-off) stay in force for other modules.
- A review can only end in approval: the IAC either approves or asks for remediation (05.7), and remediation loops back through the QP as usual. There is no "cancel review" action.
- The QP sees that its QASA is under review and at which stage, but not the DD's reason (an internal comment, 05.8).
- The negative-evaluation return to the QP counts as "Remediation requested" on the QP's Progress view and in the QASA status label, the same as an IAC remediation, so the QP sees one word for "fix and resubmit".
- The acknowledgement audit entry is recorded against the submitting QP, since the letter is issued by the system on their submission.
- Qualification registration end dates are mock values until the SAQA registry lookup exists.

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION = 'A11'`)

`mock/data/demoSeed/qasa.ts`: every acknowledgement is issued on submission (audit entry + QP notification at the submission time); no ASD/DD acknowledgement steps. Boilermaker (06) is now "IAC requested remediation", back with the QP, with the IAC comment. Electrical Trades, Welding and Gas Practitioner are approved by the CEO's signature (Gas Practitioner no longer waits on an approval letter). Plumbing (03) is approved and then "Approved — under review": Grace Mokoena started the review 6 working days ago and it is back at ASD evaluation. Water and Wastewater Process Controller (11) and Metal Fabricator (12) no longer have an acknowledgement stage. `mock/data/demoSeed/eisaCalendar.ts` uses `isQasaApproved()` and `approvedSittingsOf()`. Every other module's seed is unchanged.

Behaviour: a new submission writes the acknowledgement audit entry and one QP notification and starts at ASD evaluation; the decline action is refused; the feedback letter cannot be signed before IAC approval or on a remediation; Rigger approved by the IAC is at "CEO signature" and not yet approved, then approved after the CEO signs; a review is refused for the ASD, without a reason, on an unsigned QASA and on one already under review; a review notifies the QP once (without the reason) and the ASD once and leaves the QASA eligible for EISA Instrument Validation; Plumbing's full review loop (ASD → DD → Director Office → IAC → CEO) keeps the Calendar at 2 sittings while the ASD records 4, then clears the review, writes "Review completed" and a second Certification notification, and the Calendar shows 4. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and the scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json`, deleted afterwards) pass clean.

## Batch L2 (QCTO 5 Nov review — QP results capture, File 3 and the ID number check)

Builds on Batch L1. EISA results only; the FISA results tab is unchanged.

### 05.24 — Results capture screen for the QP: done (9 Nov 2026)

- `external/SubmitResultsBatchDialog.tsx` is now a capture screen. One row per learner (name, ID number); the headers show each component's name, type, EISA date, total marks and pass mark; one mark field per component and an Absent tick. Ticking Absent clears that learner's marks and locks the fields.
- Live checks as the QP types, from `markEntry.ts` (new shared `markIssue()`, also used by the api and the File 3 upload, so the three always agree): a typed mark must be from 0 to the component total. A blank mark is not flagged in red while the QP is still typing; it keeps the row "Incomplete" and is listed in the summary above Submit.
- Live result per row from `calculation.ts`: C / NYC under each mark, then the weighted score and the final result once the row is complete.
