## Batch T (QCTO 29 Oct 2:24:01 to 2:32:26; 6 Aug 1:42:00, 1:55:18 to 1:57:18; process map "EISA Registration" — EISA Confirmation reversed, 29.20, 29.21)

QCTO said the confirmation must come FROM the QP. The system shares the consolidated learner list; the QP says where and when the learners will be assessed; the ASD reviews it. The old "ASD generates a confirmation, QP acknowledges, QP confirms" flow is gone.

### Done
- **Consolidated learner list (29.20)** → `modules/eisa-confirmation/api.ts` (`shareDueLists`), `sharing.ts`, `listCsv.ts`, `components/LearnerListPanel.tsx`. For every published sitting whose registration has closed, the system builds the list from every Registered registration (learner, ID number, SDP, Formal or RPL/ARPL, registration number; declined learners are not on a registration, so they are not on the list) and notifies the QP. Shown as "Shared with the QP on <date>"; before that, "Will be shared on <closing date>" (`UpcomingSharesTable`, on both dashboards). The QP can view it and download it as CSV (`shared/csv/csv.ts`: BOM and `sep=,`, as File 3 and the FISA sheet).
- **QP confirmation (29.20, 29.21)** → `external/ConfirmationBuilder.tsx`, `ConfirmationDetail.tsx`. The QP picks centres only from the centres accredited for the qualification (list with town and province; `mock/api/accreditation.ts` `listAccreditedAssessmentCentres`, data in `mock/data/assessmentCentres.ts`), allocates every learner (bulk "all from SDP X to centre Y" plus a per-learner choice), enters marking date(s), moderation date(s) and the results submission date, ticks the declaration and submits. "Save draft" keeps everything half done. The api refuses a non-accredited centre, an unallocated learner, a chosen centre with no learners, dates out of order and a missing declaration.
- **Results submission date** defaults to 21 working days after the EISA (`RESULTS_SUBMISSION_DEADLINE_WORKING_DAYS` from approval-of-results config, via `defaultResultsSubmissionDate` in `eisa-confirmation/config.ts`). The QP can change it; a later date shows an amber warning, not a block.
- **ASD review (29 Oct 2:25:00)** → `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx` (`ReviewDialog`), `workflow.ts` (`CONFIRMATION_WORKFLOW`: `qp-draft` → `with-asd` → `accepted`). Accept, or Return to QP with a mandatory reason. A returned confirmation goes back to the QP with the reason in a red banner (status "Returned to the QP"), the QP fixes and resubmits; every return is kept on the record (`returns`). Audit entries (engine) and notifications both ways.
- **Feeds the results batch as before**: an accepted record has `learners`, `centres`, `confirmedCentres`, `markingDate`, `moderationDate` as the old confirmed record did. Approval of Results now asks `isConfirmationAccepted(record)` instead of `stageId === 'confirmed'` (`approval-of-results/api.ts`; `sourceInfo.ts` reads `sharedAt`; seed `approvalOfResults.ts`).
- **Old steps removed**: "Awaiting QP acknowledgement", "Awaiting QP confirmation", the ASD's "Generate EISA Confirmation" and the "Ready to confirm" table. Stored old records are mapped on load (`migrateRecord`, nothing written back, so the same result every time): confirmed becomes accepted; awaiting acknowledgement or confirmation becomes a QP draft with its learners, none allocated. The seed check runs this twice and compares.
- `CONFIRMATION_SHARE_MONTHS_BEFORE = 2` is in `eisa-confirmation/config.ts`; `CONFIRMATION_GENERATOR_ROLE` became `CONFIRMATION_REVIEWER_ROLE` (ASD).

### Assumptions
- **Share date**: the list is shared once the registration closing date has passed (the existing rule, 3 months before the EISA, so it is always inside QCTO's "no later than 2 months before"). The screens show the closing date as the share date, with "At the latest <EISA minus 2 months>" beneath it. An on-demand sitting closes later than 2 months out, so its list is shared as soon as it closes; it cannot meet the 2-month rule.
- **Sharing without a server**: there is no timer, so the list is built whenever the confirmation list is read after the closing date has passed (one run at a time, once per sitting). The stored "shared on" time is the end of the closing date, not the moment of the first read.
- **Centre list without a map**: every accredited SDP is also an accredited assessment centre in its own town (`ASSESSMENT_CENTRES`: Khanya, Limpopo/Polokwane; Thabo, Free State/Bloemfontein; Vuma, Eastern Cape/Gqeberha; Lerato, KwaZulu-Natal/Pietermaritzburg). A learner can be allocated to a centre that is not their own SDP.
- **Results date default**: 21 working days after the EISA. The date the QP enters is stored on the confirmation (`resultsSubmissionDate`) and shown on the ASD's review. The results deadline in Approval of Results still uses the 21-working-day rule (`submissionDeadlineFor`); it does not read the QP's date yet.
- Marking date(s) cannot be before the EISA; moderation date(s) cannot be before the first marking date; the results date cannot be before the EISA. `markingDate` is the first marking date and `moderationDate` the last moderation date (Approval of Results reads these).
- The ASD sees every confirmation (no "My items" toggle any more): the module has no process-stage team, as before.

### Open questions
- Should the results deadline in Approval of Results follow the date the QP confirmed here, instead of 21 working days?
- Is "the day after the closing date" the right moment to share, or should QCTO also be able to release the list manually?
- What happens when a qualification has no accredited centre (Millwright has none)? Today the list is empty and the QP cannot submit.
- Is a list of dates enough for marking and moderation, or does each date need its own centre?

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION` 'A20' to 'A21')
16 confirmations. The 11 past ones plus F13 (Welding today - 20) and F14 (Electrical Trades today - 190) stay accepted with the same learners, stages and ids, so the 10 results batches, the 2 awaiting-submission sittings and the saved capture draft are unchanged (every learner is allocated to their own SDP's centre, as before). New: **F1** Electrical Trades today + 35 submitted and with the ASD (Khanya and Thabo as centres, 5 learners); **F15** Welding today + 55, list shared with a QP draft (Khanya and Vuma chosen, 2 of 3 learners allocated, one marking date, no moderation date); **F16** Plumbing today + 62 (Lerato, 2 learners) returned to the QP with a reason. **Not shared yet**: Electrical Trades, third B1 date (2 Khanya learners registered, registration still open), shown as "Will be shared on <closing date>" to the QP and the ASD. Added for these: sitting S20 (Plumbing today + 62, `eisaCalendar.ts`), two registrations (`eisaRegistration.ts`: Lerato learner-011/012 and Khanya learner-071/072, the last two added to `enrolledLearners.ts`). Registration numbers created after about day 98 ago move up by one.

### Checked
7-weekday seed check (scratchpad `check.ts`, bundled with esbuild, fake dates Mon 9 to Sun 15 Nov 2026 at 00:30 and 23:30 UTC, 14 runs, all pass): 16 confirmations (13 accepted, 1 with the ASD, 1 QP draft with 2 of 3 learners allocated, 1 returned); reading the list shares nothing extra; 1 list still to be shared; 2 sittings awaiting results submission. Through the api: submit with an unallocated learner refused; a non-accredited centre refused; draft saved; submit, return without a reason refused, return with a reason, resubmit, accept; the accepted sitting then appears as awaiting results submission (3); an old stored record migrates to the new stages and gives the same record on every load. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json`, deleted afterwards) pass clean. `npm run build` was not run, by instruction.

## Batch S (QCTO 29 Oct 12:03 to 21:11 and 22:22 to 23:34; 5 Nov 46:50 to 48:48 — Super admin: 29.1, 29.2, 05.11)

A new internal role, **Super Admin**, with an Administration section (Role names, Acting appointments, Overdue items) and one extra power in the EISA Calendar (change a registration closing date). It sees nothing else.

### Done
- **Super Admin role (ASSUMPTION)** → `config/administration.ts` (`SUPER_ADMIN_ROLE`), `config/roles.ts`, `mock/data/users.ts` (one mock person, Nomsa Khanyile), `shared/visibility/moduleAccess.ts` (sees Dashboard, EISA Calendar and `administration/*` only; hidden from everyone else via `INTERNAL_MODULE_ROLE_OVERRIDE`), `config/nav.ts` (`ADMIN_NAV`), `shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx` (an "Administration" group in the side menu). Not in the QCTO documents: QCTO still has to say who the super admin is.
- **29.1 Role names** → `shared/administration/roleLabels.ts`, `RoleNamesPage.tsx`. Every role with its default name, current name, an edit box, Save and "Reset to default"; saving or resetting needs a reason and writes an audit entry. The note "Changing a name does not change what the role can see or do." is on the screen. Only the displayed text changes: role ids, workflow `allowedRoles`, teams and visibility are untouched. Two functions do the work: `roleLabel(role)` for a name on its own (switcher, dashboard, audit lines, approval history, history exports) and `relabel(text)` for a name inside a sentence (status labels in all nine `StatusBadge` files, notifications, audit actions and notes, letter text, signature titles and role lines, including capitals such as "DEPUTY DIRECTOR"). The role switcher and dashboard re-render at once (`useRoleLabelsVersion`); other screens show the new name the next time they render.
- **29.2 Acting appointments** → `shared/administration/acting.ts`, `ActingAppointmentsPage.tsx`, `shared/ui/RoleContext.tsx`, `RoleSwitcher.tsx`. The super admin picks a person, the role, from, to (last day) and a reason. Active, Upcoming and Past lists; "End early" needs a reason. Appointments end by themselves (status is computed from today's date; the acting role is re-checked on every render, so an expired one drops away). While one is active the person sees that role in the role dropdown ("(acting)") and appears in that role's people list. When they act, `person` carries the acting role and the name `"<name> (acting <role>)"`, so every history entry, audit entry and stored "submitted by" name reads that way with no change to the module screens. The "a different person must approve" rule still compares person ids, so an ASD acting as DD cannot approve what the same person submitted as ASD. Audit entries and a notice to the person on create and on early end.
- **29.2 Escalation** → `shared/administration/overdue.ts`, `OverdueMark.tsx`, `useOverdue.ts`. `ESCALATION_WORKING_DAYS = 3` in `config/administration.ts`. An item is overdue when it has been at one internal stage for MORE than that many working days (weekends and SA public holidays skipped, `workingDaysBetween`). The stage-entered time is the latest audit entry whose action leads INTO the current stage (the workflow records hold only the stage); where there is none, the record's own created/submitted date. Resting stages (approved, signed, validated, registered, accepted, published, booked, declined, rejected, agreed) are never overdue, and a visit report waiting for the visit itself is not either. "Overdue (n working days)" shows beside the status on the internal lists of QASA, EISA Instrument Validation (validation and scheduling), EISA Calendar (proposals and requests), Learner EISA Registration, EISA Confirmation, Approval of Results (EISA and FISA), FISA standards and validations, RPL Toolkit and Site Visits; never on the external portal.
- **Notifying the next level, once** → `escalateOverdueItems()`, run whenever an internal screen opens (`AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`). Each (item, stage, time it entered the stage) is recorded in `assessmentsV2:adminEscalationsSent` and notified once; a second run sends nothing. Next level: ASD to DD, DD to Director, Director to Chief Director (to CEO in the EISA Calendar), Sub Domain Admin to Domain Director, Domain Director / Director Office / IAC to Chief Director, Qualifications Development to DD; a role at the top of its chain (CEO, Chief Director) has no next level, so the super admin is told. One message per next-level role and module lists the items (so a first load is not 50 banners); every item still gets its own audit entry. Messages show on the Dashboard banner.
- **29.2 Overdue items screen** → `OverdueItemsPage.tsx`. Module, item, stage, waiting since, days, held by, next level. **Reassign** (reason required, audit entry, both people notified; the new person must hold the role or have an active acting appointment for it) is offered only where the module records a named person: RPL Toolkit at ASD evaluation (`allocatedAsdId`), FISA Instrument Validation at ASD validating (`allocatedAsdId`), Site Visit report at DD review (`reroutedToId`). Every other row says "No person assignment in this module". Reassigning does not move the item to another stage. Notifications can now be addressed to one person (`recipientPersonId`), and `useNotifications(role, personId)` shows them to that person only.
- **05.11 Closing date override** → `modules/eisa-calendar/` `registrationClosing.ts` (`closingDateOf`, `isClosedFor`), `types.ts` (`registrationClosingOverride` on `EisaSitting`), `api.ts` (`setRegistrationClosingOverride`, `resetRegistrationClosingOverride`), `internal/ClosingDateDialog.tsx`, `components/ClosingDate.tsx`, `components/SittingListView.tsx` ("Change closing date" on a published sitting, Super Admin only). The new date can be earlier or later than calculated but never after the sitting date, must differ from the calculated one, and needs a reason; "Reset to calculated" brings the normal rule back (reason required too). Only the Super Admin role is accepted by the api. SDPs and the QP are notified, and there is an audit entry. Wherever a sitting's closing date shows (list, details, month view hover, new-registration dropdown) an overridden date reads "<date> (changed by the super admin)" with the reason on hover or when expanded. Every "registration closed" check on a sitting now uses the overridden date: `startRegistration` (Learner EISA Registration), the new-registration dialog, EISA Confirmation sharing (`sharing.ts` now takes the sitting, so the list is shared on the overridden date). Proposed dates in a batch (no sitting yet) still use the calculated rule.

### Assumptions
- **Who the super admin is.** One mock person, Nomsa Khanyile. QCTO has not said who holds this role, or how many people.
- **3 working days** (`ESCALATION_WORKING_DAYS`). QCTO has not given the number. "Longer than" means day 4 is the first overdue day.
- **Who is told on escalation**: the role one level up (table above), by role, not by person. The CEO and Chief Director have nobody above them, so the Super Admin is told. In the EISA Calendar the Director escalates to the CEO because the CEO decides cancellations.
- **What counts as "waiting"**: an internal stage with a person to act, measured from the audit entry that put the record there. A record can be overdue for reasons the demo cannot see (for example an IAC that meets monthly): the rule is the same for every stage.
- **The Super Admin sees the EISA Calendar** (to change a closing date) but cannot add, approve, postpone or cancel: no workflow lists the role. "Nothing else extra" is read as no other module.
- **Acting records the real person**: id comparisons use the person; only the shown name carries "(acting <role>)". A person acting as DD sees the DD lists (My items = their own items as DD, "All items for my role" = the DD queue).
- The super admin can rename the external roles too (QP, SDP). Their short forms inside sentences are renamed the same way.
- Dates use the same UTC day as the rest of the domain, so around midnight South African time the working-day count can differ by one day from a South African wall clock.
- Not changed: downloaded files (CSV, PDF, Word) still carry the default role names.

### Open questions for QCTO
- Who is the super admin (one person, a team, a role held by an existing official)? Should the super admin see anything besides Administration and the closing date?
- How many working days before an item is overdue? Does the clock stop for items waiting on the QP/SDP or a committee date?
- Who exactly is told at each level, and should the Super Admin always be told too?
- Can a closing date be moved after registration has already closed (the demo allows it)? Should registered learners be told?
- Should an acting person also see the acted role's items under "My items", and should history show only "(acting DD)" or also the person's own role?

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION` 'A21' to 'A22')
- No role name is seeded as renamed.
- Acting appointments (`mock/data/demoSeed/administration.ts`): Lindiwe Khumalo (ASD) acts as Deputy Director for the current week, Monday to Sunday, so it is active on every weekday; Riaan Pretorius (ASD) acted as Deputy Director in the past and it has ended. Both with audit entries and notices.
- Overdue: nothing extra was added; the records seeded earlier already carry real stage-entered times. Today that is 50 overdue items in 10 modules (58 in 11 modules on Thursday and Friday, as seed dates are relative to the current day), among them an ASD evaluation (QASA), an ASD validating (EISA Instrument Validation), registrations at ASD review, results batches at the ASD / DD / Director / Chief Director, and reassignable RPL Toolkit and FISA validation items. Many QASA, FISA standard and RPL Toolkit items are overdue at "Awaiting CEO signature" for weeks: that is how long the older seed has left them there.
- One published sitting (far out, no EISA Confirmation or request on it) has its closing date changed to ten working days later than calculated, with a reason, an audit entry and notices to SDPs and the QP.

### Checked
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2`: no output (clean). Type check with a temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder (deleted afterwards): no errors. `npm run build` / `tsc -b` were not run, as instructed. Nothing outside `src/features/assessments-v2/` and `docs/assessments-v2/` changed.
- **7-weekday seed check** (fake clock Monday to Sunday of the current week, 00:30 and 23:30 UTC each day, 14 runs; the seed re-run each time): on every run the engine's working-day count for all 71 stage waits matched an independent count (own loop over weekdays and the public-holiday list), at least two items were overdue in at least two modules (50 to 58 items, 10 to 11 modules), exactly one overridden sitting with a reason and a date that differs from the calculated one and is not after the sitting, one active ASD-as-DD appointment and one past one, no renamed role, and the escalation pass told every overdue item once and told nothing on a second pass. All 14 runs passed.

## Batch W (the build checked against QCTO's meetings, process maps and templates)

Rule for this batch: what QCTO said in a meeting wins; the process maps only fill gaps the meetings never covered.

### Done
1. **FISA Standards follow the QASA route exactly** (QCTO email 2.9; Ms Maake 29 Oct 3:22:02 "the evaluation process must be the same as the QASA"; process map 3) → `modules/fisa/workflow.ts`: `standard-asd-reject` and `standard-dd-reject` are gone. The ASD keeps "Send to Deputy Director" and "Send back for clarification"; the DD keeps "Send to Director Office" and "Send back for clarification". Kept: the IAC "Declined" outcome and the CEO signing (shown 5 Nov without objection; QCTO still to confirm 05.7). A Standard already declined stays declined.
2. **RPL Toolkits: every "No" returns for rework** (process map 5; nothing in a meeting beyond the ASD step) → `modules/rpl-toolkit/workflow.ts`: `rpl-dd-reject`, `rpl-director-team-reject` and `rpl-iac-decline` are gone. The IAC keeps "Approve" and "Send for remediation". Kept: the ASD's "Reject" (shown 5 Nov 1:05:23 without objection). The two seeded toolkits declined by the IAC stay declined with their history: `mock/data/demoSeed/rplToolkit.ts` still records that step through the old action (`SEED_EVAL_WORKFLOW`).
3. **FISA Standards checklist = QCTO's "Assessment Standards Evaluation Report"** (QCTO 29.41: QCTO's own checklist) → `mock/data/fisaStandardChecklistItems.ts` (six Yes / No items, ids `fsa.1` to `fsa.6`; CR-011 comment on every line kept), `modules/fisa/internal/StandardWorkspace.tsx` (header from the record: title, ID, NQF level, credits, registration start and end date; "Evaluation Findings"; "Recommendation" as text; evaluator = the ASD and the date they send it to the DD), `mock/data/skillsProgrammeDetails.ts` (`getSkillsProgrammeRegistrationDates`, kept apart from the details copied into results batches). **On load** (`upgradeFisaStandardRecord`, `modules/fisa/api.ts`): a Standard not yet past the ASD gets the new items unanswered and its old answers show read-only as "Answered on the previous checklist"; a Standard past the ASD keeps its old answers, read-only. Running it again changes nothing.
4. **RPL Toolkit checklist = QCTO's "QP RPL Evaluation"** → `mock/data/rplToolkitChecklistItems.ts` (63 questions: 37 in Part 1 over eight principles, 26 in Part 2 over five steps), `mock/data/rplQualificationDetails.ts` (header lookup), `modules/rpl-toolkit/{types,selfAudit,api,hooks}.ts`, `components/SelfAuditForm.tsx`, `external/NewApplicationForm.tsx`, `external/ApplicationDetail.tsx`, `internal/ApplicationWorkspace.tsx`.
   - **QP, on submission:** every question needs Yes / No / In process and Notes / Evidence ("n/a" with the reason under No), plus "Self audit checklist completed by" (full name, designation, organisation, date; name and organisation pre-filled). The api refuses an incomplete audit. While the toolkit is back with the QP (corrections), the QP edits the audit on a "QP RPL Evaluation" tab and cannot resubmit until it is complete.
   - **ASD, in evaluation:** a QCTO feedback text per question (required), then "RPL Toolkit Approved (Yes / No)", "Comments" and "Evaluated by (QCTO official)" (name, designation, date). Approved = Yes enables "Send to Deputy Director"; No enables "Return to QP for corrections"; the api refuses the wrong one. Left blank, the evaluator is the ASD, "ASD" and the action date. Resubmitting clears Approved so the next pass decides again; the feedback already written stays.
   - **On load** (`loadAll`, `modules/rpl-toolkit/api.ts`): a toolkit stored before this batch gets a blank self-audit and evaluation, only when missing. Its old checklist answers stay and show read-only as "Answered on the previous checklist". The Director Office moderation checklist is untouched.
   - QCTO's form lists the RIGOROUS question "Do the tools and methods test all required knowledge, skills and experience as stipulated for the relevant modules of qualification or part qualification?" twice; it is used once.
5. **EISA instrument validation checklist** (`mock/data/instrumentValidationChecklistItems.ts`): **no change needed.** The examiner item "Has the Examiner / Developer's report been completed on the QCTO prescribed template?" is already item 2.4, straight after 2.3 "Has the Examiner / Developer completed their report on this instrument?", and the moderator item "Has the Moderator's report been completed on the QCTO prescribed template?" is already 3.4. Both have been there since the checklist was copied from the old build. Nothing was added, and no stored record is affected.
6. **Approval of Results** (QCTO process map 12: the code is allocated when the ASD reviews the batch)
   - **Verification code:** allocated when the batch first reaches the ASD, for EISA (on submission) and FISA (on first submission from draft), by `buildVerificationCode` with the allocation date (same format and financial-year rule as before). It is stored on the batch (`verificationCode`), shown on the Summary description from then on, and used on every letter (drafts included), report, register and the Sent to Certification list. The final decision carries the same code; a resubmission keeps it. Files: `verificationCode.ts` (`verificationCodeOf`, `allocateMissingCodes`), `api.ts`, `fisaApi.ts`, `outcomeLetters.ts`, `eisaVerificationReport.ts`, `fisaVerificationReport.ts`, `fisaSectionE.ts`, `trackingRegister.ts`, `internal/*`. **On load**, a stored batch without a code gets the one it would have had, numbered in the order the batches reached the ASD; running it again changes nothing.
   - **Due date:** `submissionDeadlineFor(sittingDate, confirmedResultsDate)`: where the sitting has an accepted EISA Confirmation with a results submission date (Batch T), the batch is due on that date (QP dashboard, submit dialog, the batch). Otherwise the 21-working-day rule. The review line "File 3 was submitted ... within the 21 working days (deadline ...)" keeps its wording and shows the real deadline.

8. **EISA Calendar notice grammar** → "(1 date)", plural only for 2 or more (`modules/eisa-calendar/api.ts`, and the same line in `mock/data/demoSeed/eisaCalendar.ts`).
9. **Overdue noise** → see "Seed" below.

### Assumptions and open questions (Batch W)
- **The clock for an outcome letter** (QASA, RPL Toolkit) starts when the committee decides, and a letter is not "waiting for the CEO" before that. Before, `shared/administration/overdue.ts` measured it from the application's submission date and listed it for every undecided application, which is why about 20 letters showed as overdue for weeks. The 3-working-day rule, who is told and the resting stages are unchanged. This was needed because those letters have no audit entry of their own to move.
- Approved = Yes at the ASD for the two seeded toolkits the IAC later declined: the ASD approved them and the IAC disagreed. Records past the ASD all show Approved = Yes.
- The RPL header (SAQA ID, NQF level, credits, registration dates) comes from a mock lookup by the toolkit's qualification name (`mock/data/rplQualificationDetails.ts`), as the Skills Programme details do for FISA. Metal Fabricator has no SAQA ID in the seed ("97110" is made up).
- "Evaluated by" designation defaults to "ASD" (never "Assistant Director").
- The FISA "Recommendation" is free text now. The old Recommended / Not recommended choice stays on stored records (read-only) and is no longer asked for.
- Open for QCTO: does the QP also fill the QCTO feedback column of the RPL form, or only the ASD? (Built as the brief says: only the ASD.)

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION` 'A22' to 'A23')
- **Overdue: 6 items in 4 modules** (was 50 in 8): three EISA results batches at the ASD (Approval of Results), the oldest Site Visits planning item (ASD planning), one RPL Toolkit at the IAC, and the QASA for Water and Wastewater at ASD evaluation. Every other open item (49 more) entered its stage within the last 2 working days.
- How: `mock/data/demoSeed/overdueTuning.ts` runs last over the finished seed. For each open item it finds the audit entry that put the item into its stage and moves it to 0, 1 or 2 working days ago (same time of day, later entries and the notice written at that moment move with it, so each trail still reads in order). The escalation rule is not touched. Items measured from a record date rather than an audit entry were set in their own seed: the Plumbing 2027 EISA Calendar batch (2 days ago), the updated Electrical Trades RPL Toolkit (2 days ago; it was 5), the FISA Standard still with Qualifications Development (created 2 days ago). The three results batches could not move: each was submitted within its own deadline, so they stay overdue on purpose.
- FISA Standards had no audit trail at all (so they were measured from a fixed creation date). `mock/data/demoSeed/fisaStandardTrails.ts` adds one for each, following the route it took.

- Open results batches and submitted FISA batches carry a verification code; the codes on approved and declined batches did not change.
- The 21 working-day wording, every stage, figure, letter and registration is as it was. Compared field by field against the committed seed, the only differences are the ones listed here, the new code field, the RPL and FISA checklist data, and the audit trails and notices of the items moved.

## Batch V2 (QCTO 29 Oct 3:05:39 to 3:16:43, item 2.12 and 29.36; process map "Post EISA Monitoring": SDP to acknowledge the site visit, if not follow up; CR-057 at 6 Aug 2:23:20 — Site Visits: stakeholder response, reminders, moved visit, Post-EISA questionnaire gate)

What QCTO asked: the SDP, assessment centre or QP is told about a visit through the system, not by email. They accept the date, or say they are not available with a reason that stays on the audit trail. They get reminders to confirm. If QCTO moves the visit, the new date goes through approval again.

### Done
1. **Stakeholder notice and response** (29.36)
   - **Who is told** (`modules/site-visits/stakeholderResponse.ts` `visitStakeholderOf`): the SDP when the visit is held at one (RPL/ARPL at an SDP, Post-EISA with an SDP), otherwise the QP named on the visit (Marking & Moderation, EISA Readiness and the other QP visit types), otherwise the assessment centre. A special meeting has none.
   - **When** (`api.ts` `sendNotice`): when a notified visit is booked (`plan-book`), and again when a moved visit is booked on its new date. An auto-created RPL/ARPL visit starts booked with no date, so its notice goes out when the officials first give it a date (`updateVisitDetails`). The notice is an in-app notification to that QP or SDP by name; for an assessment centre it goes to the ASD, who records the answer for it (as in Batch V1). Stored on the visit as `stakeholderNotice`, with an audit entry.
   - **Unannounced visits** (CR-051) get no notice and no response step.
   - **External "Site Visits" list for the QP and the SDP** (CR-047; `external/ExternalDashboard.tsx`, `external/VisitDetail.tsx`; `shared/visibility/config.ts` no longer hides Site Visits for QP and SDP): only their own visits (matched on `qpId` / `sdpId`), listed only when they know about them: notified and booked, or being moved to a new date, or shared with them. A plan still in the planning chain and an unannounced visit are never listed. Columns: type, qualification(s), visit date, notice, status, your response. The detail shows the notice, "Accept the date" / "Not available" (`components/RespondToNotice.tsx`; reason required, suggested date optional), the date history, and the shared report when the DD shared it.
   - **The answer** (`api.ts` `respondToVisitNotice`): recorded with who, when and the reason (`stakeholderResponse`), in the audit trail ("Accepted the visit date", "Not available on <date>" with the reason and suggested date), and the ASD and DD are notified. Only the stakeholder answers (or the ASD for an assessment centre), once per date, and not after the visit date.
   - **Shared report for the SDP** (`stakeholderShare.ts` `visitShareRecipientOf`): the DD's "Share report with stakeholder" now also works for a visit at an SDP (the QP when the visit has one, as before). Batch V1 remediation works for the SDP too: the SDP answers its own remediation items on its Site Visits list (`remediation.ts` `remediationResponderOf`: QP, SDP or ASD). Only an assessment centre is still answered by the ASD.
2. **Reminders** (`config.ts` `REMINDER_WORKING_DAYS = 2`, ASSUMPTION; `stakeholderResponse.ts`, `api.ts` `syncStakeholderReminders`): a notified visit with no response gets a reminder 2 working days after the notice and every 2 working days after that, until the stakeholder answers or the visit date passes. Worked out on load (`listVisits`, `getVisit`), SA public holidays skipped (`shared/dates/workingDays.ts`), the same pattern as the Batch S escalation. Each reminder is stored (`reminders`), notified and audited once, so a second load sends nothing. The ASD sees "No response yet (reminded n times)" on the visit (Planning tab, "Notice and response" card) and under the status in the All Visits list.
3. **Moving a visit goes back through approval** (`workflow.ts` action `plan-move` "Move the visit", booked to Deputy Director recommendation, ASD only, reason required; `api.ts` `performPlanningAction`; `components/StakeholderResponseCard.tsx`): on a booked visit not yet carried out the ASD gives a new date and a reason, either "the stakeholder said they are not available" (their reason and suggested date are filled in) or "QCTO's own decision". The visit goes back to Deputy Director recommendation with the new date, then Domain Director approval and Sub Domain Admin booking as for a new plan. The old date, the reason, the source and the stakeholder's answer and reminder count stay in `dateMoves` and the audit trail ("Date history" on the visit). On rebooking the stakeholder gets a new notice that says the date moved and responds again. The visit date on the Details tab is locked once the visit is booked (the api refuses a date change: "Use Move the visit"), otherwise a date could change without approval. The DD is notified of a move.
4. **Post-EISA questionnaires use the real results status** (CR-057; `modules/site-visits/resultsStatus.ts`, `components/ExecutionForm.tsx`): the manual "results approved" switch is gone, with `RESULTS_APPROVAL_IS_SIMULATED`, `setResultsApprovedForSitting`, its hook and the `resultsApprovedForSitting` field (Approval of Results no longer sets it when it creates the Post-EISA visit). The Facilitator and Learner Questionnaires open only when a results batch for the visit's sitting is at the final "approved" stage (read from the real batch list); before that: "Available once the results for this sitting are approved."

### Assumptions and open questions (Batch V2)
- **Two working days** for the first reminder and between reminders (`REMINDER_WORKING_DAYS`). Open for QCTO: is that the number, and do reminders stop at the visit date?
- **Who answers for an assessment centre:** the ASD records it, and the notice and reminders go to the ASD. Open for QCTO: will assessment centres get a portal?
- **EISA Readiness has no SDP in this build** (only a QP), and a Post-EISA visit created from a results batch has no SDP either (the batch has the QP only), so their stakeholder is the QP. QCTO's note said the SDP for those; it needs the SDP on the visit first. A Post-EISA visit that does have an SDP goes to the SDP.
- A visit stored before this batch has no notice, so no response step and no reminders (nothing is notified retroactively).
- Only one answer per visit date. The stakeholder cannot withdraw an answer; QCTO moves the visit if the date has to change.
- A moved visit is "not available" for one stakeholder only. If a Post-EISA visit has both a QP and an SDP, the SDP is asked and the QP sees the visit and the report.
- Open for QCTO: may the stakeholder see the reason a visit was moved, when it was a QCTO decision? (Built: yes, it is in the date history.) Should the DD still be able to reject a moved visit (the visit then becomes "Planning rejected")? (Built: yes, the normal chain applies.)

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION` 'A23' to 'A24')
Five visits added (25 in all; every earlier visit unchanged, with no notice or response):
- **SV21** Marking & Moderation, Johan van der Merwe (Plumbing sitting today + 62): booked 3 working days ago and notified; the QP has not answered and has had **one reminder**.
- **SV22** RPL/ARPL at Thabo Technical College (Welding, today + 25): the SDP **accepted** the date.
- **SV23** RPL/ARPL at Lerato Training Institute (Plumbing, today + 20): the SDP is **not available**, with a reason and a suggested date; the ASD can move it.
- **SV24** RPL/ARPL at Khanya Skills Academy (Welding): the SDP said it was not available on the first date and the ASD **moved** it to the date it suggested (today + 40); it is back at Deputy Director recommendation with the old date, the reason and the SDP's answer in its history.
- **SV25** RPL/ARPL at Thabo Technical College (Plumbing, today + 9): **unannounced**, booked, no notice and no response step.
- The days are working days back from today (from the last working day on a weekend or public holiday), so the reminder counts are the same on every weekday. The Welding Post-EISA visit's questionnaires open because its results batch (the 40% Welding batch) is approved. Overdue stays at **6** (4 to 6 wanted). Every other seeded stage, figure and letter is as it was.

### Checked
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2`: no output (clean). Type check with a temporary `tsconfig.check.json` inside the folder (deleted afterwards): no errors. `npm run build` / `tsc -b` were not run, as instructed.
- **7-weekday seed check** (scratch `check.ts`, bundled with esbuild, fake clock Monday 9 to Sunday 15 Nov 2026 at 00:30 and 23:30 UTC, 14 runs, 79 checks each, **all pass**): 25 visits; SV21 has exactly 1 reminder on every weekday, equal to an independent day-by-day count (own loop over weekdays and the public-holiday list), a second load sends nothing and adds no audit entry; SV22 accepted, SV23 not available with reason and suggested date, SV24 at DD recommendation with its history and the audit entry, SV25 no notice and refused a response; overdue = 6; the Welding Post-EISA batch opens the questionnaires and a sitting with no approved batch does not; the QP and SDP lists show only their own visits (not the unannounced one). Through the api: "not available" refused without a reason, a different QP and the ASD (for an SDP) refused, a past suggested date refused, a second answer refused, the ASD and DD notified, no reminders after an answer or after the visit date, a reminder every 2 working days when the clock is moved forward (each equal to the independent count, once only); move refused without a date, without a reason, to the same or a past date, by the DD, and with a "not available" source nobody gave; the Details date is locked once booked; a move goes back to DD recommendation with notice, answer and reminders cleared, the full chain rebooks it and the SDP gets a new notice saying the date moved, and the second answer sits next to the first; booking a new notified visit notifies the SDP (nothing before booking); an assessment centre's notice goes to the ASD, only the ASD records its answer; remediation on an RPL/ARPL visit shares with the SDP, tells the SDP, refuses the ASD, and the SDP submits it.

## Batch R2 (QCTO 29 Oct 2:03:08 to 2:10:31, row 29.15; 6 Aug 2:01:52, 29.17, 05.26 — Learner EISA Registration: bulk upload in File 4)

Learner EISA Registration only (`modules/eisa-registration/`, plus the readers of the RPL/ARPL flag). `SEED_VERSION = 'A25'`.

### Done (11 Nov 2026)
- **File 4 download** (`file4.ts` `buildFile4Csv`, `external/File4Panel.tsx`). "Download File 4 (pre-filled)" on a registration the SDP is building, named `LEISA<yyyymmdd>-<SDP name>.csv` (the day of the download). One row per learner enrolled with this SDP who can still be registered (not blocked by the CR-040 rule, not declined on this registration; a learner already on the registration is still listed so the file can update them). Exactly QCTO's 43 headings in order. Pre-filled where the mock holds the value: SDPCode (the SDP's accreditation number), QualificationId (the SAQA id), NationalId, LearnerLastName, LearnerFirstName; every other enrolment column and all of the readiness columns and DateStamp are blank. BOM and `sep=,` from `shared/csv`, values are text so a leading 0 is kept. The instruction line is on screen: keep the rows of the learners you want to register, delete the others, fill in the readiness columns, upload.
- **File 4 upload, preview, checks** (`file4.ts` `parseFile4`, `readiness.ts` `checkReadiness`). Same pattern as the FISA learner spreadsheet (Batch P): nothing is changed unless the whole file is clean, otherwise "Nothing was changed". Headings must be the 43 in order, and one message names the missing, extra or moved columns. Per row: NationalId (13 digits, SA ID check, leading 0 restored) or LearnerAlternateID must match a learner enrolled with this SDP ("This learner is not enrolled with your SDP for this qualification"); SDPCode and QualificationId must match the registration (a blank is also an error); the learner twice is an error; a learner blocked by another registration gets the existing block message (now `blockedLearnerMessage`), a learner declined from this registration the declined message. Enrolment columns that differ from the enrolment record are a warning, not an error (today only the two name columns are compared, because the mock holds nothing else). The preview shows one row per learner with its errors and warnings. A clean file adds or updates the learners on the registration with their readiness data in one save (`applyFile4Upload`), stores the file name and date on the registration (`file4`), and writes an audit entry. Learners on the registration who are not in the file are left as they are.
- **Readiness rules** (QCTO Learner Enrolment guideline, July 2022; `readiness.ts`, one function for the file and for adding by hand), column by column:
  - StatementofResultsStatus: 01 issued, 02 not yet issued.
  - StatementofResultsIssueDate: YYYYMMDD, a real date; required when the status is 01, blank when 02; not after the EISA date.
  - AssessmentCentreCode: optional; when given it must be the code (centre id) of a centre accredited for this qualification (`listAccreditedAssessmentCentres`, Batch T).
  - LearnerReadinessforEISATypeId: 1 Enrolled, 2 RPL for access to EISA determined by SDP, 3 Mixed mode to EISA, 4 SDP training and assessment for readiness, 5 SDP e-learning training and assessment for readiness, 6 RPL for access to EISA determined by the Assessment/Quality Partner. Type 1 needs status 02 and a blank date; every other type needs status 01 and a date.
  - FLC: 01 to 08 as QCTO listed them, required. FLCStatementofresultnumber: required; when FLC is 02 it must be RPL followed by a real date (RPLYYYYMMDD).
  - DateStamp: YYYYMMDD, a real date, not in the future.
  - Amber warning (not an error) on the row when the approved QASA for the qualification says FLC is required and FLC is 04, 05 or 07.
  - The other enrolment columns (SDPCode to ExpectedTrainingCompletionDate) are not validated beyond SDPCode, QualificationId and the ID: QCTO already holds them.
- **RPL or formal comes from the readiness type** (`readiness.ts` `isRplArplType`). The manual RPL/ARPL tick box is gone (`setLearnerRplArpl` and its hook are removed). Adding an enrolled learner by hand (still allowed, 29.17) opens a form (`external/AddLearnerDialog.tsx`, React Hook Form) with the readiness type, SOR status, SOR date, assessment centre (optional), FLC, FLC statement number and date stamp, checked by the same function as the file. RPL/ARPL = types 2, 3 and 6; everything else is formal. This feeds the Batch R1 counts, the submission notification, the CR-042/CR-044 automatic RPL/ARPL monitoring visit, the EISA Confirmation pathway column and the RPL flag on results source info. The SOR file upload per learner (CR-036) is unchanged.
- **Stored records mapped on load** (`api.ts` `mapLearner`, idempotent: a learner that already has a readiness type is left alone). Ticked becomes type 2, not ticked type 4; SOR status 01 with the SOR upload date when an SOR was uploaded, otherwise 02. The other readiness fields are blank (shown as "None" or "Not recorded"). Registrations without `file4` read as having none.
- **ASD side** (`internal/RegistrationWorkspace.tsx`). The learner table shows the readiness type (code and words, and RPL/ARPL or Formal), the Statement of Results status and date, the assessment centre and the FLC (code and words); the summary shows the uploaded File 4 name with its upload date.

### Assumptions and open questions (Batch R2)
- **ASSUMPTION (QCTO to confirm): mixed mode (type 3) counts as RPL/ARPL**, with 2 and 6. One constant (`RPL_ARPL_TYPES` in `readiness.ts`).
- **ASSUMPTION: the FLC warning.** The amber warning for FLC 04, 05 or 07 when the QASA says FLC is required is our reading, not a QCTO rule (`FLC_WARNING_CODES`). It never blocks.
- **CSV rather than .xlsx**, because no package may be installed (as for every other upload).
- The mock enrolment has no qualification, so "enrolled with your SDP for this qualification" is checked as enrolled with this SDP; the QualificationId column is the only qualification check. The SDPCode is the SDP's accreditation number and the AssessmentCentreCode is the centre id (`sdp-...`); QCTO's real code formats are open.
- The pre-filled download only holds what the mock holds (id, name, SDP, qualification); QCTO's other enrolment columns (birth date, gender, address and so on) stay blank. A learner's first word is the first name and the rest the last name.
- A learner with an alternate ID only is looked up against the enrolment's ID number, as in Batch P; the SA ID check is only applied to NationalId.
- Statement of Results is the learner's SOR status and date in File 4. It is separate from the SOR file the SDP uploads per learner, which is still needed before submitting.
- Open: a File 4 upload does not remove learners who are on the registration but missing from the file (it "adds or updates"). If QCTO wants the file to be the full list, one line in `applyFile4Upload` changes.
- Open: an old stored learner mapped to type 4 with no SOR has status 02, which type 4 would not allow in a new file. This follows the brief for old records and is not shown as an error.

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION` 'A24' to 'A25')
- **G1** (Khanya, Electrical Trades, still being built) has no File 4, so the download and upload can be tried; its two learners show readiness type 4 and the SOR status the migration rule gives them.
- **G7** (Lerato, Plumbing, submitted, with the ASD) was built from a File 4 upload and shows `LEISA<date>-Lerato Training Institute.csv` (uploaded 14 days ago) and each learner's readiness, centre and FLC.
- G8's RPL learner is type 2 with the statement issued; every other seeded learner is type 4 with status 01 and the SOR upload date (as the migration would give them). Every stage, count, block rule, RPL/ARPL count and the Batch W overdue count are as they were.

### Checked
7-weekday seed check (scratchpad `r2check.ts`, bundled with esbuild; 9 to 15 Nov 2026 at 00:30 and 23:30 UTC, 14 runs, 71 checks each, all pass), plus a comparison against the Batch V2 seed taken from git: registration ids, stages, numbers, learner counts, RPL counts, declined, monitoring items, notification count, confirmation pathways and the overdue count are identical on all 14 runs. Covered: G1 building with no file, exactly one seeded file (G7) with its name, the download (name, 43 headings in order, BOM and `sep=,`, pre-filled and blank columns, leading 0 kept), a clean file with semicolons and a dropped leading 0 restored, preview changes nothing, missing, extra and moved headings, each error above (not enrolled, SDPCode, QualificationId, status, dates, type rules, FLC, FLC number and RPL date, date stamp, centre not accredited, invalid ID, learner twice), the name warning, the FLC amber warning (04, 05, 07 only when FLC is required; checked on `checkReadiness` directly because the seeded Electrical QASA does not require FLC), a dirty file and a file with bad headings changing nothing, a clean file adding and then updating learners, type 3 counted as RPL/ARPL and type 1 as formal, adding by hand (invalid refused, type 6 counted as RPL), the legacy tick box mapping (ticked to 2, not ticked to 4, SOR status from the upload) idempotent also after writing back, the submission notification counts, and the CR-042 monitoring item created from a type 6 learner. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and the scoped `tsc -p` check pass clean. Not clicked through in a browser.

## Batch Q2 (QCTO 29 Oct 1:42:55, 1:49:13 to 1:52:45, 1:53:29 to 1:55:38, 1:40:59; rows 29.12 and 29.11 — EISA Calendar: a date per QASA component day, Excel upload)

EISA Calendar (`modules/eisa-calendar/`), the Approval of Results component dates, the seed and one new shared file. `SEED_VERSION = 'A26'`.

### Done (11 Nov 2026)
- **Types** (`eisa-calendar/types.ts`). `SittingComponentDate` (componentId, componentName, componentType, day, date). `EisaSitting.componentDates?` with `date` kept as the EARLIEST component date, so registration closing, lists and every other reader work unchanged. `EisaSittingBatch.sittings?` and `ProposeSittingBatchInput.sittings?` (one `{ componentDates }` per sitting) with `dates` holding each sitting's earliest date, in the same order. `EligibleQualification.componentSlots`. Older sittings and batches without these load and show as before.
- **One shared helper** (`eisa-calendar/componentDays.ts`), used by the Propose form, the Resubmit/Edit dialog, the Add/Edit sitting dialog, the postponement dialog, the API and the bulk upload. `componentDaySlots(qualificationId)` gives the ordered slots from the approved QASA's components (the ones Certification was notified of, `approvedComponentsOf`); nothing is copied. Label: "Theory (written) – day 1", "Practical – day 1" (the type is added unless the component name already says it). No approved QASA with components: one slot "EISA date" and the note "The QASA has no component details yet, so one date is asked per sitting." `checkSittingComponentDates(slots, dates, today, onDemand)` gives errors per slot: every slot filled, every date through `checkSittingDate`, the days of one component on different dates in order (day 2 after day 1). Two different components may share a date.
- **QP Propose form and Add/Edit dialog** (`external/ProposeDatesForm.tsx`, `internal/AddEditSittingDialog.tsx`, `components/SittingDateCards.tsx`). After choosing the qualification each sitting is a card "Sitting 1, Sitting 2 …" with a date input per slot, the QASA line above ("QASA: 2 components – Knowledge assessment 1 day, Installation and repair practical 2 days"), the per-date checks, and the registration closing date from the sitting's earliest date. Sittings per year and the on-demand rule count sittings, not dates. QCTO adding or editing a sitting directly still only gets a warning. The same cards are used when a QP corrects and resubmits, or edits, a proposal (`external/ResubmitBatchDialog.tsx`), otherwise those saves would have dropped the component dates.
- **Review and display.** ASD review panel lists each sitting's component dates with "checks passed" or the reasons per date and the closing date from the earliest (`components/RegistrationCloses.tsx` `DatesWithClosing`); the QP's own batch list shows the component dates. The sitting details dialog and the list view list every component date. The Month view shows the sitting on every component day, labelled "Electrical Trades – Practical day 2" (`SittingMonthView.tsx`).
- **Postponement** (`changeRequests.ts`, `components/RaiseChangeRequestDialog.tsx`). A sitting with component dates asks a new date per slot (same cards, same checks, the earliest must be after the current date). Stored on the request as `proposedComponentDates` (`proposedNewDate` is the earliest). On approval `applyApprovedSittingChange` writes them to the sitting's `componentDates` and `date`. A sitting without component dates keeps the single new-date field.
- **Bulk upload** (`bulkUpload.ts`, `external/BulkUploadDatesDialog.tsx`). Fixed headings in this order: Qualification title, Qualification ID (SAQA), NQF level, Credit value, Sitting number, Component, Component day, EISA date. The pre-filled template has one row per sitting x component day for each of the QP's qualifications (sittings per year from the QASA, one sitting for on demand), date blank. Checks per row, in the existing preview, nothing saved unless every row is clean: unknown qualification; component not on that QASA; component day beyond the QASA's days; a sitting missing one of its component days; the same sitting, component and day twice; more sittings than the QASA allows (and fewer, as before); every date rule above (also the registration closing date having passed and the date already on the calendar). An old 5-column file gets the heading message plus "This is the old template. Download the new template."
- **29.11 Excel upload** (`shared/files/readXlsx.ts`, no package). Finds the zip central directory, inflates entries with `DecompressionStream('deflate-raw')` (stored entries read as they are), reads `xl/workbook.xml` and its rels to find the FIRST sheet, then `xl/sharedStrings.xml` and that sheet with `DOMParser`. Rows of strings: shared strings, inline strings, numbers; in the EISA date column an Excel date serial (1900 system) becomes yyyy-mm-dd. An .xls or anything unreadable gets "Save the file as .xlsx or .csv and upload it again." The rows go through the same checks as a CSV (`parseBulkTable`). The template download stays CSV; the upload hint says .csv or .xlsx.
- **Approval of Results** (`approval-of-results/componentDates.ts`, `componentSnapshot.ts`, `api.ts`). A component's EISA date is the first day of that component on the sitting's `componentDates` (looked up from the confirmation's calendar sitting). The "next working day" rule stays ONLY as the fallback for a sitting without componentDates (ASSUMPTION comment updated).
- **Seed** (`mock/data/demoSeed/eisaCalendar.ts`, `mock/api/storage.ts` `SEED_VERSION = 'A26'`). Every seeded sitting and batch of Electrical Trades, Plumbing, Welding and Gas Practitioner (28 sittings, 4 batches) carries component dates built from its QASA, on working days: each next component on the next working day, a 2-day practical on two working days in a row (Welding, Plumbing), and S4 (Electrical Trades, today + 35) with both components on the same day. Everything else is the same.

### Assumptions (for QCTO to confirm)
- A component's EISA date on Approval of Results is the first of its days.
- A sitting's `date` is its earliest component date; a postponement that is approved moves `date` and `componentDates` to the new dates.
- The sitting must START in the chosen year (a sitting running into January is not refused).
- Two sittings of one proposal may not start on the same date (as two dates could not before).
- Components are matched in the upload by name (not case sensitive); the single slot for a QASA with no components is called "EISA date".

### Checked
Seed check (scratchpad `q2check.ts`, bundled with esbuild; 9 to 15 Nov 2026 at 00:30 and 23:30 UTC, 14 runs, 84 checks each, all pass), plus a comparison of the whole stored seed against the Batch R2 seed taken from git (clock frozen per run): on all 14 runs the ONLY differences are `eisaCalendar:sittings[].componentDates` (28 sittings) and `eisaCalendar:batches[].sittings` (4 batches); sitting and batch counts, registration ids and stages, closing dates, results batches and their due dates, audit entries and notifications are identical. Covered: the slot builder (labels, summary, no-components slot and note), each date rule (blank, weekend, public holiday, lead time, on-demand notice, day 2 different from and after day 1), two components sharing a date allowed, the same component twice on one date refused (form check, upload and API), the template (headings, rows = sittings x component days, blanks), each upload row error (unknown qualification, component not on the QASA, day beyond the QASA's days, sitting missing a day, duplicate, more sittings than allowed, fewer, weekend, holiday, lead time, missing and invalid date, day order, sitting number), the old template refused (CSV and rows), a small .xlsx built in the script (first sheet found through the rels, one entry deflated, shared and inline strings, numbers, a date serial) read back and through the same checks, .xls and unreadable files refused, the API (batch dates and sittings in step, a 2-day component accepted, postponement per slot raised, checked and approved into `componentDates` and `date`) and the Approval of Results dates (component dates used, fallback kept). `DOMParser` does not exist in Node, so the script uses a small stand-in; the real parser has not run. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and the scoped `tsc -p` check pass clean. Not clicked through in a browser.

## Batch U (QCTO 29 Oct, Madi 1:03:58; Maake 1:04:38, 1:09:25 to 1:11:27, 1:13:27; Ngomane 1:12:06; row 29.6 — exemplar instruments)

### Done (11 Nov 2026)
- **Instruments, not one instrument** (`modules/instrument-validation/types.ts`). A record holds `instruments: SubmittedInstrument[]` (id, file name and size, uploaded at, sitting id and date or none, `proposedExemplar`, `confirmedExemplar`, optional `replacedAt`). A `ValidationOutcome` also keeps `exemplarInstrumentId` and `exemplarNote` (both optional).
- **All the rules in one file** (`instrument-validation/exemplars.ts`): the load mapping, the QP submit rule, the ASD exemplar rule, who the current exemplar is, and the `dd Mmm yyyy` date format. The api, the screens and the seed use the same functions.
- **QP, Instrument tab** (`components/InstrumentsPanel.tsx`, used by `external/ValidationDetail.tsx`). Add one or more files; each row has the file name, "For EISA sitting" (this qualification's approved or published sittings with their date and component dates, or "Not for a specific sitting"), a "Proposed exemplar" radio and Remove. A line says whether the qualification already has a confirmed exemplar. With none, exactly one instrument must be marked; with one, the radio also offers "No exemplar in this submission". Submit stays off until the rule is met, and the api refuses it as well. "Not approved" still clears the instruments.
- **ASD, Validation tab** (`components/ExemplarChoicePanel.tsx`, used by `internal/ValidationWorkspace.tsx`). Each instrument shows its sitting and a "Proposed by the QP" badge. Approved and Approved with conditions need an exemplar choice, pre-selected to the QP's; "No exemplar in this submission" only when the qualification already has one. A different choice needs a note, which goes in the audit trail (action "Exemplar confirmed"). "Not approved" asks nothing about the exemplar.
- **On approval** (`instrument-validation/api.ts`): the chosen instrument gets `confirmedExemplar`; every earlier confirmed exemplar of the same qualification gets `replacedAt` (kept, never deleted). The QP's outcome notification names the exemplar, or says no new exemplar was confirmed.
- **QASA Status and Approved Instruments** (`modules/qasa-status/api.ts`, `StatusView.tsx`). `listApprovedInstruments(portal)` returns a different shape per portal: external gives `{ portal: 'external', exemplars }` (Qualification, Qualification ID (SAQA), QP, Exemplar validated on, Conditions; no file names, instruments or sittings in the data at all); internal gives `{ portal: 'internal', groups }` per qualification with file name, EISA sitting, validation date, outcome and an "Exemplar" or "Former exemplar (replaced dd Mmm yyyy)" badge. The external section is called "Exemplars", the internal one "Validated instruments".
- Files changed outside the module: `mock/api/storage.ts` (`SEED_VERSION`), `mock/data/demoSeed/instrumentValidation.ts`.

### Assumptions (for QCTO to confirm)
- **Load mapping (our assumption).** A record stored before this batch has one `instrument`. It becomes a one-item array with `proposedExemplar` true; when the record is already validated it is also `confirmedExemplar` true, so existing validated qualifications still show an exemplar. Mapping a mapped record changes nothing. If two legacy records of one qualification are both validated, the one validated last is the current exemplar and the other counts as replaced on that date.
- A "qualification" is the QASA the validation was started from (`qsaAddendumId`), so all validations of one QASA share one exemplar.
- The sitting list holds the sittings whose status is approved or published for the same qualification title. The sitting date is copied onto the instrument when it is linked (a later postponement is not followed).
- "No exemplar in this submission" is the state where no instrument is marked.
- The exemplar note is separate from the conditions: Approved with conditions asks for both when the ASD changes the QP's choice.
- The "Validated instruments" outcome is the outcome of the record the instrument was submitted in.

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION` 'A26' to 'A27')
- **V5 (new)**: Electrical Trades, Approved, 3 instruments (two linked to seeded published sittings, one for no sitting), the first one the confirmed exemplar, with an "Exemplar confirmed" audit entry and the notification naming it.
- **V4** (Electrical Trades, the earlier written paper) is the pre-batch record as the load mapping gives it, with its exemplar replaced by V5's: this is the qualification whose exemplar was replaced by a later validation.
- **V2** (Electrical Trades, waiting for the ASD) has 2 instruments, each linked to a seeded sitting, with the QP's proposed exemplar on the first. V1 and V3 are unchanged.

### Checked
- Seed comparison (scratchpad `compare.js` over bundles built from `git archive HEAD` (A26) and the working tree (A27); 9 to 15 Nov 2026 at 00:30 and 23:30 UTC, 14 runs, 518 checks, all pass): the stored keys are the same; every stored store except the validation records, audit entries and notifications is identical; V1 and V3 are identical to the A26 records after the load mapping; V4 is its mapped A26 record plus `replacedAt`; V2 is identical apart from its instruments; the only audit entries added are V5's, the only notifications added are V5's five; nothing was removed; the overdue count and modules are unchanged on every run.
- Behaviour checks (scratchpad `check-entry.js`, 77 checks, all pass) through the real api on the real seed: the load mapping (validated, waiting, none, idempotent, saving writes the new shape); each QP submit rule (no instrument, none marked with no exemplar, "No exemplar" refused before one exists, two marked, none marked allowed once one exists); the sitting list, linking and clearing; the exemplar choice rules (none chosen, "no exemplar" refused, not in this submission, different without a note, blank note, the conditions path, the QP's own choice without a note, "Not approved" with no choice); the replaced exemplar (`replacedAt` set, nothing deleted, an earlier `replacedAt` kept, one current exemplar, other qualifications untouched); the audit note and the QP notification; and the external rows (exactly the five columns plus id, no file name, instrument or sitting text) against the internal groups.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and the scoped `tsc -p` check pass clean. Not clicked through in a browser.

Props `centres`, `selectedIds`, optional `onToggle` (none means read only). Colours are theme tokens (`muted`, `border`, `card`, `primary`, `popover`), so light and dark both work. Caption: "Simplified map for the wireframe. The real system will use a GIS map from the Accreditation data."

New `useCentresByName` hook and `listAssessmentCentresByName` in `mock/api/accreditation.ts` (records keep centre names).
- **Check script** (`components/centreMapCheck.ts`, `checkCentresInsideOutline()`): projects every centre and confirms it is inside the outline and not inside Lesotho or Eswatini. Run by bundling it with esbuild and printing the result (scratch runner, not kept in the repo).

- The read-only map shows centres named on the record; a centre name not in `ASSESSMENT_CENTRES` is not drawn.

## Batch Z1 (QCTO 11 Nov review, items 11.3 to 11.7 — small changes: File 4 wording, EISA evidence, file types, Certification, tracking register as Excel)

### Done (11 Nov 2026)
- **11.3 File 4 wording** (Rafir-Munsaur 12:26–13:40): `eisa-registration/external/File4Panel.tsx` now reads "Download File 4 template". Nothing else in the EISA Registration screens said "pre-filled" about File 4 (only code comments, which are unchanged). The file content and name (`LEISA<date>-<SDP>.csv`) are the same.
- **11.4 EISA evidence** (Rafir-Munsaur 22:53–24:33; `approval-of-results/config.ts`, `workflow.ts`, `api.ts`, `external/EisaEvidenceUpload.tsx`):
  - New tags "Invigilator report" and "Irregularities report", in that order after "Attendance register" (`EisaEvidenceTag` in `types.ts`).
  - Required to submit: marking report, moderation report, attendance register (now required: it confirms the learners attended) and invigilator report. The confirmation letter stays optional.
  - New required question in the QP's evidence step: "Were any irregularities observed during the EISA?" Yes / No, stored as `irregularitiesObserved?: boolean | null` on the batch (optional so older batches load). Yes makes the Irregularities report required; No hides that requirement. **This Yes/No rule is our suggestion; QCTO said the rule can be settled at development.**
  - `EISA_EVIDENCE_INSTRUCTION` names the new documents.
  - The ASD, DD, Director and Chief Director evidence tabs (and the QP's own evidence tab) show the answer through `IrregularitiesAnswer` (`components/EisaSubmissionPack.tsx`). On Yes it reads "The QP reported irregularities – see the Irregularities report". The verification report's Section 5 (the only irregularities list the report has, for a final decline) also gets a row "Irregularities reported by the QP" when the answer is Yes.
  - The rules apply at submit only (`eisaSubmissionIssues()`, the screen and `submitResultsBatch()`). A batch submitted before this change is not blocked and its evidence tab shows "Not asked (submitted before 11 Nov 2026)".
  - FISA tags and FISA required tags are unchanged (not discussed).
- **11.5 Evidence file types** (Rafir-Munsaur 36:59–37:53): evidence uploads already accept any file (only an empty file is refused); that is kept. Every EISA and FISA evidence upload (the QP's step, the SDP's FISA evidence, and the ASD's "Add an evidence file" on both tracks) shows "Any file type: documents, spreadsheets, PDFs, photos, audio and video". Every evidence list shows a small icon per file (document, image, audio, video, other) from the extension. The helper is `shared/files/fileKind.ts` (`fileKindOf`) with `FileTypeIcon.tsx`. No size or length limit was added (video length and retention are for the build). File 3, File 4, the FISA learner spreadsheet and the calendar uploads are unchanged (.csv/.xlsx).
- **11.6 Certification** (Rafir-Munsaur 28:31–30:48): on every approved EISA and FISA batch the recommendation letter line in "Documents for this batch" says "Sent to the Certification domain automatically on <date>. No download or email is needed." (the date is the recommendation letter's `recommendedAt`, the same date the Sent to Certification tab shows). That letter's button reads "Download a copy". `SentToCertificationTab` has one line under the title: "Letters arrive here automatically when results are approved." (`BatchDocumentsCard` takes optional `notes` and `downloadLabels`.)
- **11.7 Tracking register as Excel** (Madi 39:34; Jade 41:00–42:35): `shared/files/writeXlsx.ts` writes a real .xlsx with no package: a zip of stored (uncompressed) entries with CRC32; `[Content_Types].xml`, `_rels/.rels`, `xl/workbook.xml` (and its rels), `xl/styles.xml` and one worksheet with inline strings; a bold header row, the header row frozen, column widths from the content (10 to 50). Date columns are real Excel dates (serial numbers) with the number format `yyyy/mm/dd` (QCTO's register format); counts, days and rates are numbers; ids, codes and quarters stay text. `trackingRegister.ts` has `eisaRegisterSheet()` and `fisaRegisterSheet()`, built from the same rows and the same date filter as the CSV. The file name is the same as before but `.xlsx`; the button reads "Download tracking register (Excel)". The per-batch "Tracking register entry (CSV)" and the audit trail CSV are unchanged.

## Batch Z2 (QCTO 11 Nov review, item 11.9 — monthly visit schedule per sub-unit, in QCTO's schedule layout)

### Done (11 Nov 2026)
- **Sub-units** (`modules/site-visits/subUnits.ts`): `SubUnit` = Assessment Standards, EISA Registrations, Conduct and Management, Approval of Results, Other. `DEFAULT_SUB_UNIT_BY_VISIT_TYPE` (**ASSUMPTION until QCTO confirms**, from QCTO's November schedules): Assessment Standards = QASA development visit, Instrument validation visit; EISA Registrations, Conduct and Management = EISA Readiness, RPL/ARPL monitoring, Marking & Moderation, Marking guideline discussion; Approval of Results = Post-EISA Monitoring; Other / special meeting = the planner chooses; a visit whose domain is not Assessment = Other. `subUnitOf(visit)` reads the stored sub-unit, otherwise the default (an older special meeting with nothing stored reads Other).
- **Three optional fields on `SiteVisit`** (`types.ts`; older visits still load): `subUnit`, `officials` (`{ personId, name }[]`, the planner always first) and `visitEndDate`.
  - Plan form (`internal/NewVisitDialog.tsx`): "Sub-unit" select pre-set from the visit type (required, no default, for a special meeting), "QCTO officials" multi-select of internal staff (`components/OfficialsPicker.tsx`; the planner is ticked and cannot be cleared), and "Last day (if the visit runs over several days)". `createVisit()` in `api.ts` enforces all three.
  - Visit detail (Details tab, `internal/VisitWorkspace.tsx`): all three are shown and editable while the report is still being completed, so they stay editable after a move.
  - Last day rule (`schedule.ts` `visitEndDateIssue`): on or after the visit date and on a working day. Checked on create and on every details edit; moving the date past the last day is refused until the last day is changed.
  - **Move the visit** (`plan-move`) keeps the sub-unit and officials, and moves the last day with the date so the visit keeps its length in working days (a 3-day visit stays 3 days). Editable afterwards.
- **Schedule data** (`schedule.ts`, pure): `visitScheduleFor(visits, year, month, { includePending })`, month 1 to 12. Visits whose first to last day overlaps the month and whose planning stage is `booked`; `includePending` adds visits in the planning chain, marked "Not yet approved" (a rejected plan and a visit with no date are never listed). Columns: QP/SDP, Type of visit (the meeting title for an other meeting), Institution, Physical address (venue), Province (from the SDP or the assessment centre list, otherwise blank), Occupational qualification(s) with SAQA ID, Date of visit ("11 Dec 2026", "11–13 Dec 2026", "30 Nov – 2 Dec 2026"), QCTO official(s), plus Notice and Report stage. Grouped by sub-unit in order, sorted by start date, with a visit count and a days-out count. It always uses all visits and ignores the My / All scope toggle.
- **Panel** (`components/VisitSchedulePanel.tsx`): the "Visit schedule" card in `internal/InternalDashboard.tsx`, above Recent Activity. Month arrows (default next month), the "Show visits still being approved" switch, one block per sub-unit titled like QCTO's ("EISA Registrations, Conduct and Management – December 2026") with its counts, "No visits booked" when empty, rows open the existing visit detail. Tables scroll inside the card at phone width. Dark mode: the panel's own text, borders and badge use theme tokens or `dark:` classes; the card shell it sits in (`SectionCard`) and the rest of this module have no dark styling yet.
- **Download schedule (Excel)** (`scheduleXlsx.ts`, `shared/files/writeXlsx.ts` `writeXlsxWorkbook`): one sheet per sub-unit, a title row, the heading row, the rows with real dates (the first day, and a "Last day of visit" column when the visit runs over several days), then QCTO's sign-off block with blank Name / Signature / Date cells (Compiled by, Verified by, Supported by: Director: Assessment, Approved by: Chief Director: Assessment). File name `Visit_Schedule_<yyyy>-<mm>.xlsx`. `writeXlsx` now takes optional `titleRows` and `footerRows`, and a single sheet without them is written exactly as before (tracking registers unchanged). `readXlsx.ts` gained `readXlsxSheets()` (every sheet with its name); `readXlsx()` still reads the first sheet.
- **Who sees it**: everyone who already opened the internal Site Visits dashboard (ASD, Deputy Director, Domain Director, Sub Domain Admin). The Director and the Chief Director get read-only access that shows ONLY the Visit schedule (no planning, no "Plan a New Visit", no visit records, row clicks do nothing): `shared/visibility/config.ts` `INTERNAL_SCHEDULE_ONLY_ROLES`, `moduleAccess.ts` (`isModuleVisible`, `isScheduleOnlyAccess`, so the nav, dashboard tiles and route guard all follow), and `internal/SiteVisitsInternal.tsx` chooses the view. The link from a Post-EISA batch to its visit (`?visit=`) therefore lands those two roles on the schedule only.

### Seed (`SEED_VERSION` 'A28' to 'A29')
Seven visits appended after SV25 (SV26 to SV32), dated in the month after the seed clock (counted from the clock on every run): SV26 QASA development visit and SV27 Instrument validation visit (Assessment Standards; SV27 has three officials), SV28 EISA Readiness over three days and SV29 Marking guideline discussion (EISA Registrations, Conduct and Management), SV30 Post-EISA Monitoring at an SDP and SV31 an Other / special meeting with the chosen sub-unit Approval of Results, and SV32 an RPL/ARPL visit still with the Deputy Director. So next month has two booked visits in each of the three sub-units. Only the new visits carry the three new fields; the earlier 25 visits are stored exactly as before. The seed's `planVisit` can now plan a Post-EISA Monitoring visit by an official (SDP, qualification, no sitting link), because Post-EISA visits otherwise only come from a low-competency results batch.

### Checked
- Seed comparison, previous seed (A28, `git archive HEAD`) against the working tree (A29), clock frozen per run, Mon 9 to Sun 15 Nov 2026 at 00:30 and 23:30 UTC (14 runs, 672 checks, 0 failures): same stored keys; exactly 7 visits added; every earlier visit identical and in the same order; every other store identical; every earlier audit entry and notification identical (the only additions are the new visits' own entries and notices). Random UUIDs (which differ between two runs of the same seed) are masked.
- Behaviour checks, same 14 clocks plus 30 Nov, 10 Dec and 31 Dec (123 to 124 checks each, 0 failures): the default mapping covers every VisitType and gives the agreed sub-units; the sub-unit override and the non-Assessment default; a visit from 30 Nov to 2 Dec shows in both months; booked only by default and `includePending`; grouping, sorting, counts and the date labels; `visitEndDate` validation (before the date, weekend, public holiday, no date) on the pure check, `createVisit` and `updateVisitDetails`; the planner always first and cannot be removed, a QP is refused as an official; a special meeting without a sub-unit is refused; moving a multi-day visit keeps its length and the fields; the seed numbers above; Director and Chief Director get the schedule only (rendered with `react-dom/server`: no "Plan a New Visit", "All Visits", "Recent Activity"), the ASD gets the full dashboard with the schedule above Recent Activity, other roles' access unchanged; the .xlsx read back with `readXlsxSheets`: sheet names, title row, heading row, a real date cell and last-day date, the sign-off rows with blank cells, and the single-sheet writer unchanged.
- `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and the scoped `tsc -p` check (temporary `tsconfig.check.json`, deleted afterwards) pass clean. `npm run build` was not run, by instruction. Not yet clicked through in a browser, and the .xlsx has not been opened in Excel itself. The check script used a small stand-in for `DOMParser`.

### Assumptions and questions for QCTO
- The default sub-unit per visit type (above) is **ASSUMPTION until QCTO confirms**.
- "Days out" per sub-unit is the working days each visit spans inside the month, added up, so two visits on the same day count twice. QCTO may want distinct days instead.
- The Excel sheet name for EISA Registrations, Conduct and Management is "EISA Reg, Conduct & Mgmt" because a sheet name holds 31 characters at most; the full title is the first row of the sheet.
- The planner is the first official; "QCTO officials" can be any internal staff member except the Super Admin.
- **The in-system Verified by / Supported by / Approved by steps for the monthly schedule were not requested and are a question for QCTO.** For now the sign-off block is printed blank in the Excel file and signed by hand; the Director and Chief Director only read the schedule.
- The system shows all four sub-units, including Other, every month, with "No visits booked" when one is empty.

## Change log

- **2026-10-20** — Phase 0 foundation built: skeleton, config (roles/teams/nav/labels/flags), v2-local role switcher, workflow engine, shared checklist component, letters, audit trail, in-app notifications, mock persistence layer, backend-dependent stubs, old checklist/form wording copied into mock data, module folder skeleton, entry point with its own QueryClientProvider. Router mount (`/assessments-v2/*` in `src/App.tsx`) approved and applied.
- **2026-10-21** — Phase 1 (QSA Addendum, CR-003 to CR-016 per the Phase 1 map) built end to end, external and internal: `modules/qsa-addendum/` — `types.ts`, `config.ts`, `workflow.ts` (four `WorkflowDefinition`s: acknowledgement, evaluation, outcome letter, approval letter), `api.ts` (mock backend + completeness guards), `hooks.ts` (React Query), `components/` (`StatusBadge`, `DocumentsList`, `CorrectionsPanel`), `external/` (`ExternalDashboard`, `NewApplicationForm`, `ApplicationDetail`), `internal/` (`InternalDashboard`, `ApplicationWorkspace`). New Phase-0-adjacent files: `mock/api/saqaRegistry.ts`, `mock/data/ddReviewChecklistItems.ts`. Wired into `index.tsx`/`config/nav.ts` (nav item no longer shows "Phase 1"). `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc --noEmit` pass clean (see "Assumptions made" for why a temporary tsconfig was used instead of the root build).
- **2026-10-21** — Phase 2 (EISA Instrument Validation, CR-017 to CR-025) built end to end, as its own section separate from EISA Registration, external and internal: `modules/instrument-validation/` — `types.ts`, `config.ts`, `workflow.ts` (`SCHEDULING_WORKFLOW` for the CR-017 propose/accept/counter-propose negotiation, `VALIDATION_WORKFLOW` for CR-023/CR-024's QP↔ASD-only outcome loop), `api.ts` (mock backend, entry/completeness guards, cross-module reads of `qsa-addendum/api.ts` and `eisa-calendar/api.ts`), `hooks.ts` (React Query), `components/` (`StatusBadge`, `SchedulingPanel`, `PrefilledFieldsPanel`), `external/` (`ExternalDashboard`, `NewValidationDialog`, `ValidationDetail`), `internal/` (`InternalDashboard`, `ValidationWorkspace`, `StatusDashboard` for CR-025). SDPs cannot see this section at all: `config/nav.ts` (`NavItem.hiddenForRoles`), `shared/ui/RoleBlocked.tsx` (new), `index.tsx`'s `ModuleRoute` blocks the route itself (not just the nav link) so the module's screens never mount for a hidden role, and `shared/ui/AssessmentsV2Layout.tsx`/`shared/ui/Dashboard.tsx` filter their nav/module lists the same way. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc --noEmit` (temporary `tsconfig.check.json`, deleted afterwards) both pass clean. Found and fixed a timezone bug while building `workingDays.ts`: constructing a `Date` from an ISO string without a "Z" suffix parses in the browser's local timezone, so calling the UTC getters/setters on it (as the original draft did) could shift the computed weekday by a day in any timezone ahead of UTC — fixed by parsing with an explicit "Z" and using UTC methods throughout. `npx eslint src/features/assessments-v2` and a scoped `tsc --noEmit` (temporary `tsconfig.check.json`, deleted afterwards) both pass clean.
